# Rulings of 15 October 2026

On 15 October 2026 Jason Hung asked for every ruling waiting on Jason to follow Claude's recommendation, under the project principle of utmost data accuracy and consistency first and scientific rigour second. A choice keeping every panel row accurate and every indicator id on a single definition wins over a choice adding coverage. Among choices equal on accuracy and consistency, the more rigorous choice wins. Every ruling below records the decision, the reason and the work the decision sets.

Earlier rulings still in force are Jason's ruling of 7 October 2026 (a household survey measure gets an id carrying the survey's own definition), Jason's ruling of 8 October 2026 (notified_hiv_cases and new_diagnoses never merge) and the decisions of 13 October 2026 (a sixth source class for civil registration, subject to Adrian Smith's agreement).

## R1. Papua New Guinea viral-load national sums (pg_ndoh_dashboard)
- Decision. REFUSED. No national viral-load row from the provincial HIV dashboard of the Papua New Guinea (PNG) National Department of Health (NDoH) enters the panel.
- Reason. The viral-load columns hold values for 17 of the 22 provinces for 2022 and 14 of the 22 provinces for 2023, so a national sum would present a partial count as a national count. In National Capital District and Western Highlands Province the viral-load tests and the suppressed viral loads exceed the count of people on antiretroviral therapy (ART) in the same province-year, so the province of the viral-load columns is not the province of the ART count. The panel ids viral_load_tests_performed and viral_suppression_count count people on ART.

## R2. Papua New Guinea positive HIV tests
- Decision. ENTERED under new_diagnoses, as province rows for 2022 and 2023 and a national row for every year with all 22 provinces present, under the national-row rule pg_ndoh_dashboard already applies.
- Reason. The panel already carries national programme counts of positive HIV test results under new_diagnoses from global_fund (74 rows over 23 countries, the Global Fund series "Number of HIV positive tests"), th_hivhub (five rows, "HIV Positive (tested positive)") and unaids_services (five rows), beside the counts of the United States President's Emergency Plan for AIDS Relief (PEPFAR). A separate id for the PNG count would split a single measure across two ids. The PNG count is never a notified case count, so Jason's ruling of 8 October 2026 keeping new_diagnoses apart from notified_hiv_cases stands unchanged.
- Check against the panel. The PNG positive tests for 2023 sum to 5,794, the count implied by the who_gho rows (172,875 tests at 3.3516% positivity). The 2022 sum of 4,284 sits above the implied 3,938 (110,808 tests at 3.5539% positivity), in the year the dashboard's 128,297 tests sit 17,489 above the Global AIDS Monitoring (GAM) total of 110,808 carried by who_gho, who_hts and unaids_aidsinfo. The cause of the 2022 gap is NOT YET KNOWN, and the 2022 national row carries the GAM comparison in extra.
- Work. The new_diagnoses spec in hivimpact/config/indicators.py gains a note defining the id as positive HIV test results reported by the programme named in source_id. Counts from different programmes are never added, because a person tested by two programmes appears in the counts of the two programmes, and a person tested twice in a year can count twice. new_diagnoses is never compared with notified_hiv_cases. pg_ndoh_dashboard.py emits ProgramTotalHIVTestPositive.
- Run outcome. Jason's run of 15 October 2026 wrote 319 pg_ndoh_dashboard rows, the count of the offline test after the R2 change, against 273 rows before the R2 change. The 46 added rows are the new_diagnoses rows, 22 province rows and a national row for 2022 and for 2023, with the national row at 4,284 for 2022 and 5,794 for 2023. pepfar_modality and pepfar_panorama count 600 positive tests at PEPFAR-supported sites in PNG for 2022 and 559 for 2023. reconcile declines to classify the new_diagnoses pairs of pg_ndoh_dashboard with pepfar_modality and with pepfar_panorama, because pg_ndoh_dashboard shares two national country-years with pepfar_modality and two with pepfar_panorama, below the reconcile minimum of three.

## R3. Papua New Guinea tests at the antenatal entry point
- Decision. REFUSED.
- Reason. The panel holds anc_hiv_testing_pct, a share of pregnant women, and no count id for antenatal tests. The dashboard column ProgramHIVTestEntryPointANC is a count of tests with no count of pregnant women attending antenatal care, so no share can come from the dashboard, and a count id would hold a number with no denominator.

## R4. Papua New Guinea key-population size estimates and condom counts
- Decision. REFUSED.
- Reason. The provincial size estimates sum to 195,360 female sex workers, 3.7 times the UNAIDS national estimate of 52,200 sex workers, and give the same national sums for 2022 and 2023, so the dashboard repeats a single estimate across years. The condom column covers nine provinces for 2022 only, in round thousands, and the column name does not say whether female condoms are counted, so the condom count differs from male_condoms_distributed in coverage and in definition.

## R5. Reproductive Health Supplies Visualizer (RH Viz), condoms shipped by funder
- Decision. The SingleYear.csv export of the Tableau Public workbook RHViz-2024 is downloaded (approved on 15 October 2026) and inspected before a connector is built. A connector would carry new ids for male condoms and female condoms shipped to a recipient country in a year, with a separate id for every funder, following the panel's agency ids new_diagnoses_usaid, new_diagnoses_cdc and new_diagnoses_other_agency.
- Years. Series for UNFPA (the United Nations Population Fund) and USAID (the United States Agency for International Development) start in 2008, because the RHSC (Reproductive Health Supplies Coalition) page says the historical record holds every UNFPA and USAID contraceptive purchase. Series for every other funder, and totals over all funders, start in 2017, because the RHSC page says the 2008 to 2016 record depended on which organisations submitted data.
- Reason. A shipment count is a different measure from male_condoms_distributed (condoms distributed inside a country), so a shared id would mix two measures. A total over funders for years with incomplete reporting would fall in a year when a funder stopped reporting, and a reader could not tell a fall in reporting from a fall in shipments.
- Check, 16 October 2026. The SingleYear.csv export (19,460 bytes, sha256 prefix 388f8f63a2d0, fetched with the Tableau parameter :showVizHome=no after the plain address redirected to a profile page with HTTP 404) holds 334 rows over 84 countries in four columns, Country Name, L3 Method (the contraceptive method), % of Total CYPs (the share of couple-years of protection) and Shipped Qty. The export carries no year column and no funder column, because a Tableau export holds the data of the view under the default filters of the view, and no row covers male condoms or female condoms. The SingleYear export therefore cannot supply the condom series of R5. The workbook holds three other views, Instructions, Trends and Comparison. HEAD requests give 2,231 bytes for the Trends export and 2,668 bytes for the Comparison export, and the Trends export and the Comparison export are new downloads held for Jason's yes. RH Viz stays NOT BUILT.
- Trends and Comparison, 16 October 2026. The Trends export (2,231 bytes, sha256 prefix c13e059ee1c6) and the Comparison export (2,668 bytes, sha256 prefix 7658c0517e88) were downloaded on 16 October 2026 after Jason's yes. The Trends export and the Comparison export hold three columns, L3 Method, Year of Received or Delivered Date and CYPs, for 11 methods, with years 2022 to 2026 in the Trends export and 2021 to 2026 in the Comparison export. The Trends export and the Comparison export carry no country column and no funder column, no row covers male condoms or female condoms, and a CYP value is a conversion of shipped units into couple-years of protection, not a count of shipped units. No public export of the RHViz-2024 workbook gives condoms shipped by recipient country, year and funder, so RH Viz stays NOT BUILT.

## R6. HIV Policy Lab
- Decision. hiv-policy-lab.csv and codebook.csv are downloaded (approved on 15 October 2026), and the adoption statuses go into a separate policy table under data/policy/, keyed on country, year and policy, outside the panel. The panel's value column stays numeric.
- Reason. An adoption status is a category (adopted, partially adopted, not adopted). Coding the categories as 1, 0.5 and 0 would invent a scale the HIV Policy Lab never published, and the panel would hold a number with no unit. A separate table keeps every status as published and still joins to the panel on country and year.
- Code outcome. BUILT on 16 October 2026. hivimpact/policy_lab.py downloads hiv-policy-lab.csv (6,798,668 bytes, sha256 prefix fe8c56834bc7) and codebook.csv (38,482 bytes, sha256 prefix fa32e1627b15) into data/raw/hiv_policy_lab and writes data/policy/hiv_policy_lab.csv, keyed on country_iso3, year and policy_id, with data/policy/hiv_policy_lab_codebook.csv beside the status table. The data file decodes as Windows-1252 (cp1252) and the codebook as UTF-8, so the module reads every file as strict UTF-8 first and as cp1252 only when strict UTF-8 fails. Of the 128,623 rows of the data file, 71,547 hold no adoption status and are not written, because a blank is no published status. The status table keeps every policy item as published, and a top-level policy with sub-items (for example S1, same-sex sex non-criminalization) holds the statuses in the sub-items (S1i, criminalization, and S1ii, prosecution). The Yes and No statuses of the PrEP sub-items by population and by product keep the published wording. A failed gate refuses the whole table, with gates on the columns, on the match of policy items between the data file and the codebook, on repeated keys, on the six published status values, on the ISO3 codes through normalize.to_iso3 and on the years from 2017.
- Test. The offline test of 16 October 2026 on the downloaded copies, with the network blocked, gave 57,076 status rows over 194 countries and 80 policy items for 2017 to 2024, with 27,967 Adopted, 20,802 Not adopted, 1,938 Partially adopted, 3,650 Yes and 2,719 No, and the 83 codebook rows. The three policy items with no status in any year are HS5 (access to medicines under TRIPS, the World Trade Organization agreement on intellectual property), HS7 (data sharing) and TP5i_Notes (notes on the PrEP policy item), and the sub-items of HS5 and HS7 hold the statuses. Seven altered inputs were all refused, a renamed column, a repeated row, an unknown status, a Yes status on CT1, an unknown ISO3 code, the year 2016 and a codebook item removed. The citations decode with the accents in place, for example 42 citations of "Guía práctica", and Côte d'Ivoire keeps the circumflex.
- Run. Jason runs `python3 -m hivimpact.policy_lab`. The module downloads the two files, 6.8 MB in all, and writes the two tables under data/policy. The policy table stays outside the panel, scripts/run_all.py does not call the module, and reconcile never reads the policy table.

## R7. Reported AIDS cases and deaths among reported AIDS patients
- Decision. Two new ids with source_type routine_programme.
  - aids_cases_reported, the count of people newly reported with AIDS in a calendar year by national surveillance.
  - aids_deaths_reported, the count of all-cause deaths reported in a calendar year among people ever reported with AIDS.
- Sources. WHO/Europe measure HFA_348 (new AIDS cases) in who_europe_hfa, and the Chinese yearly tables under R10. The case definition named by the source travels in extra.case_definition.
- Reason. aids_deaths and new_infections hold modelled estimates, and a surveillance count sharing a modelled id would put two methods under a single id. Case definitions differ between national surveillance systems, so the spec says the ids compare a country across years and compare sources for the same country, and a comparison across countries needs extra.case_definition.
- Fiji. The 117 HIV-related deaths of 2025 and the yearly HIV-related deaths of 2021 to 2024 in the Fijian surveillance report are NOT CARRIED. The Fijian count is a count of deaths related to HIV, while aids_deaths_reported counts deaths from any cause among people reported with AIDS, so the Fijian series would put a second definition under the id.
- Work. HFA_348 is read in a fresh API call before the who_europe_hfa change, because the first year of national AIDS reporting in every country is read from the HFA_348 country notes, never copied from the HFA_350 cut-off years.
- HFA_348 outcome (read on 15 October 2026, 25 country notes). The HFA_348 measure note counts the people newly diagnosed with AIDS during the calendar year and names no AIDS case definition, so extra.case_definition says the note names none. The HFA_348 notes for Spain, Italy and the Netherlands name the national AIDS register or the reporting body and describe no regional reporting, so Spain, Italy and the Netherlands keep every AIDS year from 2000. France (underreporting of 41% for AIDS in 2007 to 2009), Czechia (foreigners on short stays not notified), Ukraine (Crimea, Sevastopol City and parts of the territories outside government control missing from 2014 to 2016) and Turkmenistan (official reports) are carried with the notes in extra.who_country_note, as in HFA_350. The connector refuses AIDS counts narrower than the measure note. Turkey and North Macedonia (every year) and Ireland (from 2012) count AIDS only at the time of HIV diagnosis. The United Kingdom moved toward surveillance of AIDS within three months of HIV diagnosis, and the British note says the figures for 2015 and 2016 are likely lower than earlier figures and names no year for the move, so the connector refuses the United Kingdom from 2011, the year the British count falls from 665 to 419 (by 37%, the largest fall in a single year from 2000 to 2019). Sweden is refused from 2000 (AIDS reporting not mandatory from 2000, discontinued from 2008 to 2016), Belgium for 2016 (the Belgian note says the 2016 AIDS diagnoses were not available) and the Russian Federation for every year (no Russian report to WHO, and the only Russian AIDS value is a zero for 2010, beside 62,581 new HIV diagnoses). The offline test gives 935 aids_cases_reported rows over 49 countries for 2000 to 2019.
- HFA_350 change. The Turkish note in HFA_350 and HFA_348 says the reported HIV cases exclude persons diagnosed with AIDS at the time of HIV diagnosis, so the Turkish HIV count misses the people diagnosed late, and every Turkish notified_hiv_cases year is refused. The North Macedonian AIDS note says the AIDS cases only include people diagnosed with AIDS at the time of HIV diagnosis, and in 10 years from 2000 to 2011 the North Macedonian AIDS count exceeds the HIV count, so the North Macedonian HIV count misses the people diagnosed with AIDS at HIV diagnosis, and every North Macedonian notified_hiv_cases year is refused. notified_hiv_cases from who_europe_hfa falls from 999 to 960 rows over 51 countries. A sum of the HIV and AIDS counts for Turkey or North Macedonia is a figure WHO does not publish, so the panel carries no Turkish or North Macedonian HIV plus AIDS sum. The Romanian note says the Romanian HIV total already adds the HIV and AIDS case reports, so Romania stays.
- Run outcome. The first who_europe_hfa run of 15 October 2026 failed certificate verification, because dw.euro.who.int sends the server certificate without the Sectigo intermediate certificate. who_europe_hfa now verifies the host certificate against the operating-system trust store through the truststore package, with certificate verification on throughout. Jason's second run of 15 October 2026 wrote 1,895 who_europe_hfa rows, 960 notified_hiv_cases rows over 51 countries and 935 aids_cases_reported rows over 49 countries, the counts of the offline test.
- Joint report, 16 October 2026. The joint ECDC and WHO report HIV/AIDS surveillance in Europe 2020 (2019 data), hiv-surveillance-report-2020.pdf (10,875,898 bytes, sha256 00b829aee14e, 126 pages, from www.ecdc.europa.eu), says in the Annex 1 methods on page 113 only confirmed HIV and AIDS cases are reported at European level, under the WHO case definitions for HIV surveillance of 2007 and the EU case definitions of Commission Decision 2002/253/EC. Page 113 also says the national HIV and AIDS case definitions may differ across the WHO European Region and calls the EU and WHO case definitions compatible for surveillance purposes. extra.case_definition on the aids_cases_reported rows of who_europe_hfa now names the WHO and EU case definitions beside the missing name in the HFA_348 measure note.
- Russian HIV counts, 16 October 2026. Page 113 of the joint report says the Russian case definitions changed in 2016, says the updated federal statistical Form N61 from medical facilities counts the individuals newly diagnosed with HIV, and says the Russian data for 2009 to 2015 cannot directly be compared with 2016 to 2019. The Russian count in HFA_350 falls from 100,220 in 2015 to 86,855 in 2016.
  - Decision. who_europe_hfa refuses the seven Russian notified_hiv_cases years from 2009 to 2015 and keeps 2016 to 2019.
  - Reason. The Russian counts from 2016 match the panel measure, a person newly registered as diagnosed, and keeping the Russian years before 2016 would put two Russian case definitions under notified_hiv_cases. The Russian refusal follows the Spanish, Italian and Dutch refusals, which drop the years recorded under an earlier reporting regime and keep the later years. No other source in the panel carries a Russian notified_hiv_cases row, so reconcile loses no pair.
  - Offline test. notified_hiv_cases gives 953 rows over 51 countries, after 66 country-years were refused, with 236 rows carrying a WHO country note. aids_cases_reported is unchanged at 935 rows over 49 countries.
  - Status. CHANGED in the connector, the notified_hiv_cases entry of hivimpact/config/indicators.py and the who_europe_hfa entry of scripts/build_site.py. Jason's run of 16 October 2026 wrote 1,888 who_europe_hfa rows (953 notified_hiv_cases and 935 aids_cases_reported), the panel fell from 453,768 to 453,761 rows, the Russian notified_hiv_cases rows now cover 2016 to 2019 only, and all 935 aids_cases_reported rows name the WHO and EU case definitions in extra.case_definition. The reconcile figures stayed at 66,764 national comparisons, 78 independent pairs, 211 source pairs and 5,291 material divergences.
- Danish AIDS count for 2004, 16 October 2026. The validation of Jason's run flags a single who_europe_hfa row, the Danish aids_cases_reported value of 1 for 2004, beside 40 for 2003 and 44 for 2005 in HFA_348, while the Danish HIV count for 2004 (306) lies within the range of the neighbouring years. Denmark carries no WHO country note. A search for a published Danish AIDS count for 2004 found no primary source, because the Epistemonikos host answered 403 and NCBI sent the PubMed query to the NCBI misuse page, and the Epistemonikos and NCBI blocks were not retried.
  - Decision. who_europe_hfa refuses the Danish aids_cases_reported row for 2004.
  - Reason. A single case beside 40 and 44 in the neighbouring years cannot be the Danish yearly count of new AIDS diagnoses, and carrying the Danish value of 1 would put a partial report under aids_cases_reported. Accuracy comes before keeping a year the source publishes.
  - Offline test. aids_cases_reported gives 934 rows over 49 countries, after 67 country-years were refused. notified_hiv_cases is unchanged at 953 rows.
  - Status. CHANGED in the connector, and Jason's rerun of 16 October 2026 carried the refusal into the panel. who_europe_hfa wrote 1,887 rows (953 notified_hiv_cases and 934 aids_cases_reported), and the panel went from 453,761 to 453,760 rows, with 937 aids_cases_reported rows in all (934 from who_europe_hfa and three from cn_ndcpa). The Danish AIDS series now runs 45 (2002), 40 (2003), 44 (2005) and 51 (2006) with no row for 2004, the validation flags no who_europe_hfa row, and the reconcile figures are unchanged.
- Joint report for 2024 data, 20 October 2026. Ruling R35 changes the who_europe_hfa refusals. HFA_350 now refuses Austria, Luxembourg and Serbia in every year, Ukraine for 2015 and Spain for 2018 and 2019, and the North Macedonian HFA_350 refusal narrows to 2000 to 2016, so who_europe_hfa keeps the North Macedonian counts for 2017 (44) and 2018 (45). HFA_348 now refuses Austria and Serbia in every year and Spain from 2014, which replaces the HFA_348 outcome statement above, under which Spain keeps every AIDS year from 2000. The offline test gives 892 notified_hiv_cases rows over 49 countries and 888 aids_cases_reported rows over 47 countries.

## R8. Mongolia
- Decision. The yearly Mongolian notified_hiv_cases count comes from the annual Health Indicators books of the Health Development Center (HDC), an agency of the Ministry of Health of Mongolia, read from the pdfplumber text layer and never by optical character recognition (OCR). The monthly counts of the National Statistics Office (NSO) of Mongolia serve as a cross-check and never enter the panel. Registered deaths wait on Adrian Smith's agreement to the civil registration source class.
- Reason. A note under the China CDC Weekly tables says monthly statistics lack the annual verification done in February of the following year, so 12 monthly counts added together give a figure no agency published or verified. The HDC yearly count is the published yearly figure.
- Connector. mn_hdc (hivimpact/connectors/country/mongolia_hdc.py), built 16 October 2026, reads five HDC books. The 2024 English book gives Figure 6.23, the count and the rate per 100,000 for 2015 to 2024. The 2025 Mongolian book gives section 6.5.2, the count, the rate and the cumulative count for 2025. The prose counts of the 2019 English, 2023 English and 2024 Mongolian books serve as cross-checks, and the 2023 English book repeats the sentence for 2022. The connector writes 11 national rows, 18, 26, 25, 18, 18, 25, 16, 19, 23, 19 and 39 cases for 2015 to 2025, with sex all and no aimag (province) rows, because the books print no aimag count for HIV.
- Gates. Every chart count sits under a printed rate implying a population between 2.8 and 3.9 million, and the implied population moves by a factor between 0.97 and 1.06 between successive years. The ten chart counts average 20.7, the ten-year average printed in the 2024 English and Mongolian books. The prose counts of 2019, 2022 and 2024 equal the chart counts. The cumulative counts rise from 286 at the end of 2019 to 346 at the end of 2022, 388 at the end of 2024 and 427 at the end of 2025, by the sum of the yearly counts between the years. The 2025 count sits 16.2 cases above the mean for 2016 to 2025, as the 2025 Mongolian book prints. Any failed gate refuses every row.
- Test. The offline test of 16 October 2026 on the downloaded copies, with the network blocked, gave the 11 rows. Eight altered inputs were all refused, three chart counts, the 2025 count, a cumulative count, a prose count, the printed distance from the average and a missing ten-year average.
- Run. Jason runs `python3 scripts/run_all.py --sources mn_hdc --countries all` and then `python3 -m hivimpact.reconcile`. The connector downloads the five books, 94 MB in all, once into data/raw/mongolia_hdc.
- Run outcome. Jason's run of 16 October 2026 (scripts/run_all.py with --sources mn_hdc,unicef_hiv and --countries all) downloaded the five books, 94 MB in all, with every sha256 digest equal to the digest recorded in the connector. Every gate passed, and mn_hdc wrote the 11 rows of the offline test. mn_hdc is the only panel source of notified_hiv_cases for Mongolia, so the Mongolian rows meet no other source in reconcile, and the reconcile counts did not change. A second run of the same command later on 16 October 2026 read the five books from data/raw/mongolia_hdc and wrote the same 11 rows.

## R9. Tested for HIV in the past 12 months with the result received (DHS and PHIA)
- Decision. New id hiv_tested_12m_result_survey_pct, people tested for HIV in the 12 months before the interview who received the result, as a share of all respondents, with source_type survey and the 95% confidence interval where published. The dhs connector gains HA_CPHT_W_T1R (sex female) and HA_CPHT_M_T1R (sex male), national and at DHS level rank 1, under the level-rank rules already in hivimpact/connectors/dhs.py. PHIA testing values enter the same id only where the PHIA indicator definition names all respondents as the denominator.
- Reason. The spec of hiv_tested_12m_survey_pct, cleared by Jason on 7 October 2026, forbids sharing the id with the DHS measure, because Peru ENDES table 3.3 sets no condition on the result. A separate id keeps the two measures apart.
- Age check. The DHS definition text names no age range. The age range of every survey is read from the DHS Program API before a row enters the panel, so reconcile never compares rows covering different age ranges. The API answers of 15 October 2026 label the national DHS value "Total 15-49" for every survey, although the men's samples of 116 surveys run to 54, 59 or 64, so the value covers ages 15 to 49. A DHS row therefore carries the age range of the DHS label in age_group, enters only where the survey's own age range for the sex (MinAgeWomen and MaxAgeWomen, MinAgeMen and MaxAgeMen) covers the label range, and carries the survey's own range in extra.survey_age_range.
- Sample universe. A survey of ever-married respondents (Afghanistan 2015 and Pakistan 2017, women and men) gives a share of ever-married respondents and not a share of all respondents, and the dhs connector writes no row for the survey.
- Regions. The DHS region rows carry no age label, and the DHS answer with breakdown=all for men holds a "Total 15-49" row beside a "Total" row of every man interviewed, so a region tabulation can cover ages outside 15 to 49. The region check uses the unweighted denominators, the whole counts of respondents behind every DHS value, so every comparison is exact. The level-rank-1 region rows of a survey enter in three cases only, and extra.age_basis names the case. The level-rank-1 regions hold every respondent of the national 15-49 row. Or the level-rank-1 regions hold fewer respondents and the top-level region rows (the rows with no leading dots, at any level rank) hold every respondent of the national 15-49 row, as in Haiti 2012 with the camps at no level rank and Chad 2014 with N'Djamena at no level rank. Or the level-rank-1 regions hold fewer respondents and the survey interviewed ages 15 to 49 only. India 2020 (the region "Jammu, Kashmir & Ladakh" beside the regions "..Jammu & Kashmir" and "..Ladakh" at level rank 1, counting 3,738 women and 3,201 men twice) and Uganda 2006 men (region denominators twice the national denominator) give no testing region row, and Mali 2023 (regions below the national row in a survey of ages 15 to 64, with no top-level proof) gives no testing region row for women or for men. A survey with no national 15-49 row written gives no region row (Sierra Leone 2013 and Uganda 2006, women).
- Two surveys in a single year. Uganda carries a DHS survey and an AIDS Indicator Survey (AIS) for 2011, with 41.7% of women tested in the DHS survey against 24.2% in the AIS, and 30.7% of men against 23.2%. The panel key holds no survey column, so a country-year with two surveys gives no national testing row and no testing region row, and the panel never holds two values for a single key.
- Mislabelled regions. The Sierra Leone 2013 survey answers three level-rank-1 regions (Eastern, Northern (before 2017) and Southern) holding every respondent of the national row, with no row for the Western Area, which holds Freetown, and no row for Moyamba district. A Sierra Leone 2013 region label therefore covers a part of the country the label does not name, and no Sierra Leone 2013 region row enters the panel, for prevalence or for testing.
- Prevalence regions. For consistency the same region check now governs the DHS prevalence regions of 13 October 2026. With the DHS answers of 15 October 2026 the prevalence region rows fall from 2,270 to 2,115. The check refuses 146 rows, 30 for women and men together (Angola 2023, India 2006 and Kenya 2003) and 116 for men (Angola 2015 and 2023, Burkina Faso 2003, the Democratic Republic of the Congo 2013 and 2023, Ethiopia 2005 and 2016, Guinea 2005, India 2006, Kenya 2003 and Niger 2012), and the Sierra Leone 2013 refusal removes nine rows. Every women's prevalence region passes the check, and the 252 national prevalence rows stay identical to the panel.
- Rows in the offline test of 15 October 2026. 287 national testing rows (143 women, 144 men) and 2,959 testing region rows (1,505 women, 1,454 men), all with age_group 15-49, from 2003 to 2025, with no duplicate panel key.
- Run outcome. Jason's run of 15 October 2026 wrote 5,613 dhs rows. hiv_tested_12m_result_survey_pct entered the panel with 3,246 rows, the 287 national rows and 2,959 region rows of the offline test. The hiv_prevalence_15_49 rows in the panel fell from 12,468 to 12,313, a fall of 155 region rows (146 refused by the region check and nine by the Sierra Leone 2013 refusal). The run log refuses the testing measure for Afghanistan 2015 and Pakistan 2017 (ever-married respondents) and for Uganda 2011 (two surveys), and writes no testing region row for India 2020, Uganda 2006 men and Mali 2023. The validation step warns of yearly falls in the count of countries reporting the DHS testing measure, and the falls follow the survey calendar, because a country runs a DHS survey once in several years.

## R10. China
- Decision. Chinese rows of aids_cases_reported and aids_deaths_reported come from yearly tables only, the yearly reports of the National Disease Control and Prevention Administration (NDCPA) for 2023, 2024 and 2025 and the China CDC annual page for 2023, never from summed monthly tables. The NDCPA yearly table and the China CDC annual page read 58,903 cases and 22,137 deaths for 2023, and the NDCPA yearly tables read 54,931 cases and 19,970 deaths for 2024 and 49,326 cases and 19,097 deaths for 2025.
- Reason. The 12 NDCPA monthly counts sum to 59,533 cases for 2023, 55,344 for 2024 and 49,756 for 2025, above the yearly totals, because the monthly tables count cases by the date of final review and the yearly tables count verified cases by the date of onset. The monthly series stays out of the panel, which keeps the Chinese years before 2023 empty.
- Guangdong. The monthly Guangdong table counts clinical and confirmed AIDS cases and excludes people with HIV who have not reached the AIDS stage, the definition of the national AIDS row. A Guangdong yearly table, if found, would enter at admin1. Monthly Guangdong files are NOT CARRIED under the R8 reason.
- Work and outcome. Connector 36, hivimpact/connectors/country/china_ndcpa.py (source_id cn_ndcpa), BUILT and RUN on 15 October 2026. The connector reads the NDCPA epidemic information channel (channel code c100016) through the public queryList route, which held 51 articles on 15 October 2026, keeps the three yearly articles titled "{year}年全国法定传染病疫情概况" and never reads a monthly table. A yearly table enters only where the table holds a single 艾滋病 (AIDS) row with all four numbers and the first table note names the count by the date of onset. A death row also needs the third table note to name the all-cause deaths reported in the year among cumulative reported AIDS patients. Where two yearly articles for a year disagree on the AIDS row, the year gives no row. The China CDC annual page serves as a check and never as a second source. The China CDC annual page for 2023 agrees with the NDCPA table, and a China CDC value differing from the NDCPA value would give no row for the indicator. The China CDC listing pages hold no yearly page for 2024 or 2025, and the rows for 2024 and 2025 say so in extra.china_cdc_annual_page. The offline test on the saved answers of 15 October 2026 gives six national rows (aids_cases_reported and aids_deaths_reported for 2023, 2024 and 2025) with no duplicate panel key. The gate tests give five rows with a China CDC mismatch for 2023 cases, six rows with a failed China CDC request, four rows with a second 2024 article carrying other numbers and three rows with no third note in any table. Jason's run of 15 October 2026 wrote the six rows, aids_cases_reported rose from 935 to 938 rows in the panel, and aids_deaths_reported entered with three rows.

## R11. ECDC Surveillance Atlas of Infectious Diseases
- Decision. NOT CARRIED.
- Reason. The atlas disclaimer, read again on 15 October 2026, asks the recipient of TESSy (The European Surveillance System) data to refrain from distributing the datasets without the written consent of the European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC). The paragraph headed Terms of use, which authorises reproduction with acknowledgement, sits under the disclaimer for the Ebola virus disease atlas and names WHO and the ministries of health of the affected countries as the data providers, so the Terms of use paragraph does not cover TESSy data. The project site publishes the panel, so carrying the atlas counts would distribute TESSy data. A series running HFA_350 to 2019 and the atlas from 2020 would also join two sources, and for the 943 country-years in HFA_350 and in the atlas only 479 are equal, with 96 more than 5% higher in the atlas and 68 more than 5% lower.
- Correction. The aggregator notes read the Terms of use paragraph as a later paragraph covering the atlas, and the notes are corrected.
- Joint report licence and tables, 16 October 2026. Page 4 of the joint ECDC and WHO report HIV/AIDS surveillance in Europe 2020 (2019 data) places the report under the Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-ShareAlike 3.0 IGO licence (CC BY-NC-SA 3.0 IGO), which allows copying, redistribution and adaptation for non-commercial purposes with citation, and requires adapted material to carry the same licence. Table 1 of the report (pages 68 and 69) gives new HIV diagnoses by country for 2010 to 2019. For Austria, Belgium, Bulgaria, Croatia, Cyprus and Czechia, HFA_350 reproduces Table 1, with all 59 Table 1 counts equal to the HFA_350 counts in the panel and the Belgian count for 2019 blank in Table 1 and in HFA_350. Table 1 of the joint reports for later data years is a candidate source of European notified counts for 2020 to 2024, on the same upstream series as HFA_350 and under the CC BY-NC-SA 3.0 IGO licence, apart from the TESSy atlas. A connector reading Table 1 of the later joint reports is NOT BUILT, because the joint reports for 2020 data onward were not read and the connector would read counts from PDF tables.
- Joint report for 2024 data, 20 October 2026. The joint report HIV/AIDS surveillance in Europe 2025 (2024 data) was read on 20 October 2026, and connector ecdc_hiv_report reads Table 1 of the 2025 report from the pdfplumber text layer under rulings R25 and R35. The atlas stays NOT CARRIED.

## R12. AVAC Global PrEP Tracker
- Decision. REFUSED. The seven CSV files are not downloaded.
- Reason. The tracker's methods text says a PrEP initiation ideally counts the unique individuals who ever used PrEP and more often counts the individuals who used PrEP at least once in a given project, programme or site, so the unit counted changes between countries and a person who stops and restarts can count twice. The methods text settles the definition, and a download would add no fact the ruling needs. The cumulative files are refused for the same reason, and a cumulative count is not a yearly measure.

## R13. PHIA through the UNAIDS Inequalities dashboard (connector 32)
- Decision. Connector 32 is built, reading national values for ages 15 to 49 from the 18 Population-based HIV Impact Assessment (PHIA) surveys in the public Power BI model 367430.
  - HIV prevalence enters hiv_prevalence_15_49 with source_type survey, the rule the dhs connector applies to DHS prevalence.
  - ART coverage among all people living with HIV enters the new id art_coverage_survey_pct.
  - Viral suppression among all people living with HIV enters the new id community_viral_suppression_survey_pct.
  - Recent infection enters the new id hiv_incidence_survey_per_1000, new infections per 1,000 uninfected people a year, published with no interval.
  - Testing in the past 12 months with the result received enters hiv_tested_12m_result_survey_pct under the R9 condition.
- Refused. Ethiopia 2018, because the PHIA project records the Ethiopian survey as a survey of urban areas only, and an urban survey is not a national value. The splits by wealth, residence and education, because the panel has no column for the splits. The PHIA sampling regions, because the regions match no admin1 name in the panel. The KPA (Key Populations Atlas) study-site rows, because a study site is not a country or a province.
- Reason. hiv_prevalence_15_49 already carries DHS survey prevalence beside the modelled values, with the age range in the id name, so PHIA prevalence follows the existing rule. art_coverage_pct and community_viral_suppression_pct hold modelled values for all ages, and the PHIA values cover ages 15 to 49 with a sampling interval, so the PHIA coverage and suppression values take survey ids under Jason's ruling of 7 October 2026.
- Work and outcome. Connector 32, hivimpact/connectors/unaids_phia.py (source_id unaids_phia), BUILT and RUN on 15 October 2026. The connector reads the PHIA databases from the Source table of model 367430, the dashboard unit and definition of every carried measure from the Indicator table, and the national rows for ages 15 to 49 from the Data table, with no split by residence, wealth, education or key population.
  - Checks. A changed dashboard unit or definition gives no row for the measure. A year differing from the year in the database id gives no row. A total row for females and males together enters as sex all only where the total row's unweighted respondents equal the sum of the female and male respondents, a sum that held for all 85 national survey measures on 15 October 2026. The interval enters only where the value lies inside the interval. A PHIA database outside the 17 reviewed surveys gives no row until reviewed.
  - Held. Testing in the past 12 months with the result received (HTS_TEST_RECVRES_1) gives no row under the R9 condition. The dashboard definition names no denominator and points to the PHIA tabulation plan (29-09-2021-20-47-13-6154d0d140c1f.pdf on phia-data.icap.columbia.edu, 3,699,658 bytes), downloaded on 16 October 2026. The tabulation plan is also the document to confirm the annual basis of the PHIA incidence, which the dashboard defines as new infections "in the reporting period".
  - Tabulation plan read, 16 October 2026. Jason approved the download on 16 October 2026 (https://phia-data.icap.columbia.edu/storage/Country/29-09-2021-20-47-13-6154d0d140c1f.pdf, 3,699,658 bytes, sha256 prefix 063cf476b377, 325 pages with a text layer).
  - Incidence basis, closed. Tables 5.3.A and 5.3.B of the tabulation plan (pages 32 and 33) print "Annual HIV incidence" among persons aged 15 to 49, so the PHIA incidence is an annual rate and the PHIA values meet the definition of hiv_incidence_survey_per_1000.
  - Testing denominator. Table 7.3.A of the tabulation plan (males, page 42) prints the percentage who received HIV testing in the past 12 months and received the results, with rows for HIV positive, HIV negative and not tested by the survey HIV test, and a footnote relating the measure to PEPFAR HTS_TST. The table universe on page 44 is every interviewed respondent of the sex (indstatus 1) with a testedreceiveddetail answer coded 1 to 7, and page 7 says most tables remove people with missing values for the analytic variables. The PHIA denominator therefore holds every interviewed respondent whatever the HIV status and the testing history, minus any respondent with no testing answer.
  - Testing, REFUSED under the R9 condition, 16 October 2026. The DHS Guide to DHS Statistics page on coverage of prior HIV testing (dhsprogram.com/data/Guide-to-DHS-Statistics/Coverage_of_Prior_HIV_Testing.htm, read 16 October 2026) gives the denominator of HA_CPHT_W_T1R and HA_CPHT_M_T1R as every woman or man aged 15 to 49 and counts a missing answer on testing as not tested and a missing answer on the result as no result received. The PHIA tabulation plan removes a respondent with no testing answer from the denominator. A PHIA row would therefore put a second denominator under hiv_tested_12m_result_survey_pct, and the R9 condition (all respondents as the denominator) fails. HTS_TEST_RECVRES_1 gives no row, and the connector emits the same rows as before, so the panel needs no new run. No separate indicator id is opened for the PHIA testing measure, because the PHIA testing values would sit beside no other source in the panel.
  - Offline test of 15 October 2026 on the saved answers, with the network blocked. 204 rows over 17 surveys in 13 countries from 2016 to 2021, with 51 rows for hiv_prevalence_15_49, 51 for art_coverage_survey_pct, 51 for community_viral_suppression_survey_pct and 51 for hiv_incidence_survey_per_1000, and no duplicate panel key. Malawi 2020 gives prevalence 7.98% (7.35% to 8.65%), ART coverage 85.2% (83.1% to 87.2%), viral suppression 85.7% (83.7% to 87.6%) and incidence 2.31 per 1,000. The gate tests give 153 rows with a changed VLS definition, 202 rows with a conflicting duplicate of the Malawi 2020 male suppression row (the male row and the total row drop), 203 rows with the respondent count of the Kenya 2018 total ART coverage row changed, 204 rows with an unreviewed database added, 24 rows for Malawi alone and 201 rows with 15 rows outside the national rows for ages 15 to 49 added.
  - Run outcome. Jason's run of 15 October 2026 wrote the 204 rows of the offline test. hiv_prevalence_15_49 rose from 12,313 to 12,364 rows in the panel, and art_coverage_survey_pct, community_viral_suppression_survey_pct and hiv_incidence_survey_per_1000 entered with 51 rows for every id. reconcile matches the national PHIA prevalence rows with 17 country-years of unaids_aidsinfo, 17 of world_bank, three of unaids_naomi and three of dhs. The run added a single material divergence (a relative difference above 10% on a pair reconcile infers as independent), Kenya 2018 males, with PHIA at 2.74% (2.33% to 3.19%) and unaids_naomi at 3.31% (3.10% to 3.52%), and the PHIA interval and the Naomi interval overlap between 3.10% and 3.19%.
  - Naomi pair. The Naomi model fits district prevalence to household survey data among other inputs, so a PHIA value and a Naomi value for the survey year share an input, and unaids_naomi and unaids_phia are not separate measurement systems in the sense of the reconcile docstring. hivimpact/reconcile.py declares no pair of unaids_naomi with a survey source and holds no relationship for a model fitted to a survey, so the pair of unaids_naomi and unaids_phia keeps the inferred classification, under the reconcile rule of declaring a relationship only where the relationship is known.

## R14. South Asia
- Cumulative counts. REFUSED, for the registered HIV cases of the Pakistani National AIDS Control Programme (NACP), the Maldivian cumulative count and any difference of two cumulative counts. A cumulative count is not a yearly measure, and a difference of two Wayback captures covers a period set by the capture dates, a figure no agency published.
- Sri Lankan districts. District counts of reported HIV cases from the National STD/AIDS Control Programme (NSACP) of Sri Lanka enter as province rows only, as a sum over the districts of a province, and only when every district of the province holds a value for the year. Districts are the second administrative level, and a district name in admin1 would mix two levels in a single column. The Wayback reads behind the earlier district years wait on the fifth ni_mapasalud run.
- Modelled estimates. A modelled estimate by province is carried as published and never summed to a national figure, because the national estimate comes from a separate model run.

## R15. District estimates in the UNAIDS databook (unaids_databook)
- Decision. REFUSED for the 32 countries other than Nigeria and Eritrea.
- Reason. The district rows sit below the first administrative level in at least Benin, Côte d'Ivoire and the Democratic Republic of the Congo, and hivimpact/schema.py carries admin1 only. A sum of district estimates to the province level would be a sum of modelled estimates, refused under R14, and unaids_naomi already carries the region level for most of the 32 countries.

## R16. El Salvador and Jamaica through the browser pane
- Decision. REFUSED.
- Reason. The Salvadoran and Jamaican hosts refused or did not answer the requests of the Bash tool, and the project rule forbids switching tools to get past a block.

## R17. Age groups
- Decision. The AIDSinfo age exports of the 2025 round enter under unaids_aidsinfo with age_group filled, beside the all-ages rows of the same round. The UNICEF age estimates of the 2026 round enter under unicef_hiv as a whole series, with the pair unicef_hiv and unaids_aidsinfo declared derived in reconcile for the age ids. The orphan estimates stay refused. The out-of-date DECLARED_UNMAPPED reason in hivimpact/connectors/unaids_aidsinfo.py ("needs an age_group column") is replaced.
- Reason. The panel carries an age_group column, scripts/build_site.py keeps only age_group "all" for the national charts, and reconcile keys on age_group, so an age row is compared only with a row of the same age group and never inflates a total. The AIDSinfo age rows come from the round behind the all-ages AIDSinfo rows, so the age rows and the all-ages rows of unaids_aidsinfo stay internally consistent. The UNICEF series is carried whole under a separate source_id for the reason recorded in hivimpact/connectors/unicef_hiv.py, where joining two rounds reverses the direction of a change.
- Code outcome. BUILT and RUN on 15 October 2026. hivimpact/connectors/unaids_aidsinfo.py reads the 18 age-group exports through AGE_MAP, and the offline test on copies of data/raw/unaids gives 104,532 rows, the 49,127 all-ages rows unchanged plus 55,405 age rows, with 26,631 of the 55,405 censored, no repeated key and a rule for every export. hivimpact/connectors/unicef_hiv.py reads HVA_EPI_LHIV, HVA_EPI_INF_ANN and HVA_EPI_DTH_ANN for 10 UNICEF age groups, and the offline test on the SDMX answer of 15 October 2026 gives 49,625 rows, 46,889 age rows over 138 countries for 2010 to 2025 beside the 2,736 PMTCT rows. reconcile.DECLARED_RELATIONSHIPS declares the unaids_aidsinfo and unicef_hiv pair derived for plhiv, new_infections and aids_deaths. The 5,785 shared values with no censoring differ by a median of 8.3 per cent between the 2025 round and the 2026 round, and Colombia's new infections at ages 10 to 19 read 620 for 2012 in the 2025 round against 2,400 in the 2026 round, with the all-ages count at 11,000 in the 2025 round and the 2026 round. The AIDSinfo prevalence exports for ages 15 and over and 15 to 24 stay refused, because hiv_prevalence_15_49 names the age range in the id (R18). The UNICEF female and male values by age group are not read, because R17 covers age and the AIDSinfo age-group exports hold no split by sex. hivimpact/config/indicators.py drops "(all ages)" from the names of plhiv, new_infections and aids_deaths and adds a note saying age_group carries the age range.
- Run outcome. Jason's run of 15 October 2026 (scripts/run_all.py with --sources unaids_aidsinfo,unicef_hiv and --countries all) wrote 104,532 unaids_aidsinfo rows and 49,625 unicef_hiv rows, the counts of the offline tests, and the panel grew by 102,294 rows, from 251,160 to 353,454. The unaids_aidsinfo log reads 69 of the 146 exports, refuses 77 exports on purpose and leaves no export awaiting a decision. The run validation warns of repeated values of 100 in the age rows, for example 9,910 aids_deaths values of 100 in unicef_hiv, and 100 is the value the censoring convention writes for a published "<100". reconcile now lists the declared derived pairs for unaids_aidsinfo and unicef_hiv, with 4,495 country-years for plhiv, 4,217 for new_infections and 4,333 for aids_deaths, and the declared pairs rise from eight to 11. The za_thembisa age rows for South Africa (ages 0 to 14, 15 to 24 and 15 to 49) now meet the UNAIDS age rows and account for every other change in the reconcile report. The independent comparisons rise by 204, from 31,583 to 31,787, the independent pairs rise from 103 to 105 with plhiv and new_infections for unicef_hiv and za_thembisa, the subset comparisons rise by nine with the aids_deaths values for unicef_hiv and za_thembisa at ages 0 to 14, and the material divergences rise by 59, from 9,036 to 9,095. Ruling R19 settles the pairs between za_thembisa and the UNAIDS sources.
- Date correction. The unicef_hiv docstring and the reconcile.DECLARED_RELATIONSHIPS reasons for pmtct_coverage_pct and vertical_transmission_pct named the AIDSinfo exports "of 5 September 2026", the retrieved_at date of the panel rows. The 146 exports in data/raw/unaids were saved on 18 July 2026, and the unicef_hiv docstring and the reconcile reasons now give the save date.

## R18. Age group of hiv_prevalence_15_49
- Decision. Every source writes hiv_prevalence_15_49 with age_group "all". za_thembisa wrote age_group "15-49" for 690 rows in the panel of 15 October 2026, and hivimpact/connectors/country/south_africa_thembisa.py now writes "all" for the three Thembisa prevalence labels for ages 15 to 49 (all people, males and females).
- Reason. The id name carries the age range, and dhs (2,522 rows), unaids_aidsinfo (3,694 rows), unaids_naomi (1,794 rows) and world_bank (3,768 rows) wrote age_group "all" for hiv_prevalence_15_49 in the panel of 15 October 2026. reconcile keys on age_group, so the Thembisa prevalence rows were never compared with the UNAIDS, World Bank or DHS values for South Africa. A single age label for the id keeps hiv_prevalence_15_49 on a single definition across sources. unaids_phia follows the same rule.
- Work and outcome. Edited and RUN on 15 October 2026. Jason's run of 15 October 2026 rewrote the 14,150 za_thembisa rows with no change in the panel row count, and the 690 Thembisa prevalence rows now carry age_group "all". reconcile now compares the Thembisa prevalence with the other sources for South Africa, adding 46 comparable country-years and 46 independent comparisons, with no new material divergence.

## R19. Republished UNAIDS estimates in reconcile
- Decision. reconcile.DECLARED_RELATIONSHIPS declares derived the pair unaids_aidsinfo and who_gho for the seven GHO estimate series (plhiv, new_infections, aids_deaths, art_coverage_pct, know_status_pct, viral_suppression_pct and community_viral_suppression_pct). The same table declares derived the pairs between za_thembisa and the UNAIDS sources for South Africa, with unaids_aidsinfo, unicef_hiv and who_gho for plhiv, new_infections and aids_deaths, unaids_aidsinfo and world_bank for hiv_prevalence_15_49, and unaids_aidsinfo and who_gho for art_coverage_pct, know_status_pct, viral_suppression_pct and community_viral_suppression_pct. The za_thembisa pairs with programme counts (on_art_count and prep_recipients from global_fund, pepfar_panorama, unaids_aidsinfo and who_gho), with dhs and with the World Bank population stay inferred.
- Reason. reconcile infers a pair from the numbers, and two publications of the same estimate fall through the derived test when the publications differ, which labels a revision as an independent disagreement. hivimpact/connectors/who_gho.py records the seven GHO estimate series as the same estimates AIDSinfo publishes. In the panel of 15 October 2026 WHO GHO carries a 2025 value in all seven estimate series and every AIDSinfo export stops at 2024, so WHO GHO holds a later annual round of the UNAIDS/WHO estimates, and over the 3,146 uncensored plhiv values AIDSinfo and WHO GHO share, the median ratio of the AIDSinfo value to the WHO GHO value is 1.0. reconcile inferred every AIDSinfo and WHO GHO pair with shared values as independent and flagged 2,964 material divergences between AIDSinfo and WHO GHO. For South Africa, Leigh Johnson of the University of Cape Town, the lead developer of Thembisa, and Yogan Pillay, previously a deputy director-general of the South African Department of Health, are quoted by Bhekisisa and Daily Maverick in reports of 23 July 2024 as saying South Africa submits Thembisa estimates to UNAIDS in place of Spectrum. The know_status_pct values of AIDSinfo and za_thembisa differ by a median of 0.5 per cent over 16 years in the panel. The Thembisa pairs carried 116 material divergences under the inferred labels, and the aids_deaths values at ages 0 to 14 for unicef_hiv and za_thembisa carried the subset label, which states a difference in scope where unicef_hiv and za_thembisa count the same age group. The UNAIDS feature story of 28 June 2019 on the South African model sits on unaids.org, which answers 403 to the project, and was not read, so the South African link rests on the statements of Leigh Johnson and Yogan Pillay and on the agreement in the panel. The Bhekisisa page answered 403 to a direct read on 15 October 2026, and the quotes come from the extracts a web search returned for the Bhekisisa and Daily Maverick pages. docs/PHASE3_FEASIBILITY_AFRICA.md line 144 calls a Thembisa gap against UNAIDS a difference between Thembisa and Spectrum, and the line is kept as written in Phase 3.
- Thembisa 4.8 report, 16 October 2026. The Thembisa 4.8 report of March 2025 (ThembisaVersion4.8.pdf, 2,418,946 bytes, sha256 prefix 204c3400549f, 232 pages), downloaded after Jason's yes, never says South Africa submits Thembisa estimates to UNAIDS. The report names UNAIDS as an earlier funder of the Thembisa model and mentions the UNAIDS 95-95-95 targets and the UNAIDS advice on antenatal testing data. The South African link therefore still rests on the statements of Leigh Johnson and Yogan Pillay and on the agreement in the panel. za_thembisa reads the Thembisa 5.0 outputs (ProvOutputs5_0), a later model version than the 4.8 report describes.
- Code outcome. BUILT and RUN on 15 October 2026. hivimpact/reconcile.py holds 37 declared entries, seven for unaids_aidsinfo and who_gho and 19 for za_thembisa. Jason's reconcile run of 15 October 2026 gives the figures a read of data/panel/hiv_panel.csv with the reconcile rules predicted. The independent pairs fall from 105 to 84, the independent comparisons fall by 14,293 to 17,494, the derived comparisons rise from 28,789 to 43,091, the subset comparisons fall by nine to 6,106, the comparable country-years on independent pairs fall from 29,681 to 15,390, the material divergences fall by 3,080 to 6,015, and the declared pairs with shared values rise from 11 to 35. The national comparisons stay at 66,764.
- Correction of 15 October 2026. The first text of R19, and of the reason R19 put in hivimpact/reconcile.py, rested on 780 equal plhiv values of 10,000 or more and called an equal value a match on a five-digit count. AIDSinfo and WHO GHO round every estimate of 10,000 or more to two significant figures, so an equal value is a match on two significant figures, and two separate estimates a few per cent apart often round to the same figure. The R19 reason now rests on the record in hivimpact/connectors/who_gho.py and on the later annual round in WHO GHO. The R19 decision and the R19 run figures are unchanged.

## R20. Reported series and the pregnant women estimate in AIDSinfo and WHO GHO
- Decision. reconcile.DECLARED_RELATIONSHIPS declares derived the unaids_aidsinfo and who_gho pairs for on_art_count, pmtct_arv_count and pregnant_women_hiv, and the hiv_test_positivity_pct pairs of unaids_aidsinfo with who_gho, unaids_aidsinfo with who_hts, and who_gho with who_hts. The on_art_count pairs of global_fund and of who_tme, and the new_diagnoses pair of pepfar_modality and pepfar_panorama, stay inferred.
- Reason. docs/ACCESS_LADDER.md section 9m found AIDSinfo and WHO GHO pairs inferred independent at a median difference of one per cent or less and held the classification for a decision, and ruling R19 settled the seven estimate series. For on_art_count, AIDSinfo rounds the reported number on antiretroviral treatment to two significant figures and prints a small count as a bound (for example <500), and WHO GHO publishes the count unrounded under HIV_0000000009. In the panel of 15 October 2026 the WHO GHO value rounded to two significant figures equals the AIDSinfo value in 1,602 of 2,030 shared country-years, in 238 more the AIDSinfo bound holds the WHO GHO value, and 146 of the remaining 190 country-years sit within 10 per cent. Under the inferred label the on_art_count pair carried 256 material divergences, 212 of the 256 an AIDSinfo bound holding the WHO GHO value. For pmtct_arv_count the AIDSinfo value equals the WHO GHO value in 1,162 of 1,515 shared country-years and in 111 of the 204 shared country-years at 10,000 or more, on counts AIDSinfo and WHO GHO publish unrounded, and an equal unrounded five-digit count is the same count published twice. The pmtct_arv_count pair carried 112 material divergences. WHO GHO series HIV_0000000021 is the UNAIDS/WHO modelled estimate of pregnant women living with HIV, the estimate AIDSinfo publishes as pregnant women needing antiretroviral medicines. WHO GHO carries a 2025 value for 90 countries where the AIDSinfo export stops at 2024, and over the 759 uncensored values AIDSinfo and WHO GHO share, the median ratio of the AIDSinfo value to the WHO GHO value is 0.98, so the R19 reason applies, and the pregnant_women_hiv pair carried 306 material divergences. For hiv_test_positivity_pct, hivimpact/connectors/who_hts.py reads the Global AIDS Monitoring editions, the AIDSinfo value is the WHO GHO value rounded to two significant figures in 221 of 321 shared country-years, the AIDSinfo value equals the who_hts value in 240 of 399, and who_hts and WHO GHO agree exactly on the national test total in all 325 country-years compared. The positivity pairs carried three material divergences. The Global Fund on_art_count value equals the WHO GHO value in 29 per cent of 773 shared country-years, so the Global Fund figure is the national count in some country-years and a count of patients in Global Fund grants in other country-years, and a single label cannot describe the national count and the grant count at once. who_tme reads the number on antiretroviral treatment from the WHO tuberculosis data collection, a separate reporting form. hivimpact/connectors/pepfar_modality.py emits the PEPFAR country totals on purpose, so the gap between the PEPFAR country row and the Panorama partner sum shows in reconcile as a divergence.
- Code outcome. BUILT and RUN. hivimpact/reconcile.py holds 43 declared entries, six of the 43 from R20. The comment on the reported series in hivimpact/connectors/who_gho.py now records the rounding. The predicted figures and the figures of Jason's reconcile run with R20 and R21 together sit under R21.

## R21. Censored values in reconcile
- Decision. reconcile reads extra.censored. A comparison where source a or source b prints the value as a bound stays out of the statistics classify_pairs reads a relationship from, and the minimum of three comparisons counts the comparisons with a point value from source a and from source b. A comparison whose bound holds the other value takes the comparison status within_censored_bound and is never a material divergence. A comparison whose bound does not hold the other value stays comparable. reconciliation_pairs.csv gains n_point_cells, reconciliation_cells.csv gains censored_a and censored_b, and n_cells keeps counting every comparison. The Basis sentence in scripts/build_site.py counts the comparisons with a point value from source a and from source b, names the comparisons set aside as bounds, and opens with "Classified by our project from documents." for a declared pair outside DECLARED_BASIS.
- Reason. AIDSinfo prints small figures as <100, <200, <500, <0.1, <1, >95 and >98, unicef_hiv carries the same notation, and the connectors write the bound as the value and the notation in extra.censored, on 63,652 national rows of the panel of 15 October 2026. reconcile compared the bound as a point value. Afghanistan's on_art_count for 2013 is <500 in AIDSinfo and 214 in WHO GHO, a relative difference of 80 per cent flagged as material, where 214 lies inside the AIDSinfo bound. Before R20, 288 of the 6,015 material divergences were a bound holding the other value. The tbhiv_incidence pair of unaids_aidsinfo and who_tme shares 4,252 country-years, and in all 2,437 censored comparisons the AIDSinfo bound holds the who_tme value. Over the 1,815 comparisons with point values the AIDSinfo value equals the who_tme value in 97.5 per cent and sits within 2 per cent in 99.4 per cent, while the censored comparisons gave the pair the subset label, which states a difference in scope. With the censored comparisons out of the statistics the pair is inferred derived. The site home page already describes the set-aside count as "matched figures set aside as immaterial or not comparable", which covers the new status. Before R21 the Basis sentence gave a declared pair outside DECLARED_BASIS the statistics sentence, which read as if the statistics produced the declared label.
- Code outcome. BUILT and RUN. hivimpact/reconcile.py and scripts/build_site.py compile, and an offline test of load_panel, build_comparisons, classify_pairs and flag_divergences on synthetic rows in the scratchpad gave the status within_censored_bound to a <500 against 214 and to a <200 against 114, kept a <100 against 400 comparable and divergent, and read the relationship from the three point comparisons. With R20 and R21 together, a read of data/panel/hiv_panel.csv with the new rules predicts 53,543 derived comparisons, 11,294 independent, 1,854 subset and 73 insufficient, over the same 66,764 national comparisons. The independent pairs fall from 84 to 78, the declared pairs with shared values rise from 35 to 41, the independent-pair comparisons split into 10,495 comparable, 727 immaterial, 56 within_censored_bound and 16 zero_or_negative, and the material divergences fall from 6,015 to 5,291.
- Run outcome. Jason's reconcile run, reported on 16 October 2026, gives every predicted figure, with 53,543 derived comparisons, 11,294 independent, 1,854 subset and 73 insufficient over 66,764 national comparisons, 78 independent pairs, 41 declared pairs, 10,495 comparable, 727 immaterial, 56 within_censored_bound and 16 zero_or_negative comparisons on independent pairs, and 5,291 material divergences. The status column of the reconcile summary was 18 characters wide, narrower than within_censored_bound, and is now 22 characters wide.

## R22. HIV survey measures of the Pacific Data Hub (pacific_data_hub)
- Decision. New connector pacific_data_hub reads table DF_HEALTH of the SDMX (Statistical Data and Metadata eXchange) service of the Pacific Data Hub, the statistics service of SPC (the Pacific Community), for three measures under the Demographic and Health Survey (DHS) standard definitions, as three new survey ids with sex and age_group. hiv_comprehensive_knowledge_survey_pct takes HIV_14 (women 15-49), HIV_16 (men 15 and over), HIV_47 (women 15-24) and HIV_49 (men 15-24). condom_last_higher_risk_sex_survey_pct takes HIV_34 (women 15-49) and HIV_38 (men 15 and over), as a share of respondents who had higher-risk intercourse in the 12 months before the interview. sex_before_15_survey_pct takes HIV_51 (women 15-24) and HIV_53 (men 15-24). A value with observation status O (missing) gives no row, and a value with status U (low reliability) carries extra.obs_status. The SPC age codes give age_group 15-49, 15+ and 15-24, and the connector never merges two age ranges.
- Refused. The other 50 HIV codes of DF_HEALTH, with the reason for every code in the docstring of hivimpact/connectors/pacific_data_hub.py. The SPC labels of HIV_31, HIV_33, HIV_35 and HIV_37 read "more than 2 partners" where the DHS standard counts two or more partners, so the SPC label and the DHS standard give two definitions and the values cannot enter a standard id. SH_HIV_INCD in table DF_SDG_03 and the Blue Pacific 2050 tables, new HIV infections per 1,000 uninfected people, is a rate the panel can compute from new_infections, plhiv and population, the reason unaids_aidsinfo.DECLARED_UNMAPPED gives for the AIDSinfo export of the same rate. Table DF_PAM (the Pacific Aid Map of the Lowy Institute) has no sector dimension, so a Global Fund total in DF_PAM mixes HIV, tuberculosis and malaria. Table DF_NMDI_HEA (the National Minimum Development Indicators for public health) holds no HIV code.
- Reason. Jason's instruction of 14 October 2026 puts every aggregator connector in the build. The SPC labels of the eight codes follow the wording of the DHS standard indicators, so every new id holds a single definition. The panel held no knowledge or behaviour id before ruling R22, so no existing id changes. The men's values for ages 15 and over and the women's values for ages 15 to 49 carry separate age_group labels, and reconcile keys on age_group (ruling R17), so reconcile never compares rows covering different ages.
- Consistency with AIDSinfo. The AIDSinfo exports "Young people_Knowledge about HIV prevention among young people (15-24)" and "Combination prevention_Condom use at last high-risk sex_Population_ Adults (15-49)" report the two measures with no split by sex, and the footnotes cite DHS, MICS (Multiple Indicator Cluster Surveys), Behavioural Surveillance Surveys, surveys of knowledge, attitudes, behaviour and practices, and in the condom export an online survey. A single AIDSinfo export therefore mixes several survey definitions, and no Pacific country appears in the knowledge export or the condom export. The two exports stay unread, and the refusal reasons in unaids_aidsinfo.DECLARED_UNMAPPED now name the missing split by sex and the mixed survey types in place of "a different research question". The condom export had matched the key-population substring "condom use " and the reason "single-point 2024 snapshot", and now matches an entry naming the condom export. The count of refused AIDSinfo exports stays at 77.
- Consistency with DHS. The DHS Program API answer of 16 October 2026 lists HA_CKNA_W_CKA and HA_CKNA_M_CKA (comprehensive correct knowledge, with women and men interviewed as the denominators), HA_CKNY_W_CKA and HA_CKNY_M_CKA (the same measure at ages 15 to 24), and HA_HRSX_W_CND and HA_HRSX_M_CND (condom use at last higher-risk sex with a non-marital, non-cohabiting partner). The dhs connector reads the six DHS indicators under the same ids through the testing pass of ruling R9, with the same rules for age, sample universe, two surveys in a single year and region rows. The DHS API holds Papua New Guinea 2017 and Timor-Leste 2009 and 2016 for the Pacific and no Pacific Demographic and Health Survey of the pacific_data_hub connector, so the two sources share no country. A search of the 4,655 DHS labels found no indicator of sex before age 15 at ages 15 to 24, and FE_TNBH_W_SEX and FE_TNBH_M_SEX cover ages 15 to 19 only. HA_MSXP_W_CND and HA_MSXP_M_CND name a denominator of respondents with 2+ partners, the DHS standard behind the refusal of HIV_31, HIV_33, HIV_35 and HIV_37.
- Code outcome. BUILT. hivimpact/connectors/pacific_data_hub.py, the three specs in hivimpact/config/indicators.py, the registration in hivimpact/connectors/__init__.py and the AIDSinfo reasons compile. The offline test on the SDMX answer of 16 October 2026 (580 HIV values) and on the answer filtered to the eight codes (80 values) gives 71 rows from the full answer and 71 from the filtered answer, 40 knowledge rows, 13 condom rows and 18 rows of sex before 15, over eight countries (KIR, MHL, NRU, SLB, TON, TUV, VUT and WSM) and the years 2007, 2009, 2012, 2013, 2014 and 2015, with no repeated key. The nine refused values carry status O, and no value of the eight codes carries status U. Values run from 0.5 to 60.7 per cent.
- DHS code outcome. BUILT on 16 October 2026. hivimpact/connectors/dhs.py lists the six indicators in DHS_SURVEY_INDICATORS beside the two testing indicators, with the DHS denominator of every indicator in extra.dhs_denominator. The national rows of HA_CKNA and HA_HRSX carry the label "Total 15-49", and the national rows of HA_CKNY carry the bare label "Total", so the connector gives HA_CKNY the age range 15-24 of the DHS denominator and refuses a HA_CKNY row with any other label. DHS reports print a value on 25 to 49 unweighted cases in parentheses and suppress a value on fewer than 25 cases, so a value on fewer than 25 cases gives no row and a value on 25 to 49 cases carries extra.dhs_precision. The DHS API answers of 16 October 2026 hold no value on fewer than 25 cases and 391 condom values on 25 to 49 cases, seven national values for women and 384 region values. No prevalence or testing row rests on fewer than 50 cases. The offline test on the DHS API answers of 16 October 2026 with the network blocked gives 6,438 new rows over 66 countries from 2000 to 2025, with no repeated key. Knowledge gives 593 national rows (145 women and 132 men at ages 15-49, 166 women and 150 men at ages 15-24) and 2,842 region rows at ages 15-49, and condom use gives 335 national rows and 2,668 region rows at ages 15-49. The 5,613 prevalence and testing rows of the test are identical to the rows before the change. The sample-universe rule refuses 48 knowledge rows of surveys of ever-married or currently married respondents and a single condom row, and the two surveys of Uganda 2011 give no knowledge or condom row.

## R23. Female and male values of the UNICEF age-group counts (unicef_hiv)
- Decision. unicef_hiv reads the female and male values of HVA_EPI_LHIV, HVA_EPI_INF_ANN and HVA_EPI_DTH_ANN for the 10 UNICEF age groups, with sex "female" or "male", beside the totals for all sexes read under R17. A gate admits a female and male pair only beside the total for all sexes of the same indicator, country, year and age group, and only when the female and male values can add up to the total, given the UNICEF rounding to two significant figures and the censoring thresholds "<100", "<200" and "<500". A refused pair leaves the total in place. The two PMTCT indicators and any all-ages count keep sex "all" only.
- Reason. The female and male values come from the UNAIDS 2026 round behind the totals already carried, so the sex rows add no second definition to plhiv, new_infections or aids_deaths. The panel already carries sex "female" and "male" for the three ids (unaids_naomi at all ages and za_thembisa at ages 15 and over), reconcile keys on sex, and scripts/build_site.py keeps sex "all" and age_group "all" for the national charts, so a sex row never enters a total or a national chart. No other panel source holds female or male values for the UNICEF age groups, all under age 25, so the sex rows meet no other source in reconcile.
- Check, on the UNICEF answer of 15 October 2026, read offline on 16 October 2026. Every total of the three age-group counts has a female value and a male value, 46,889 sets over 138 countries for 2010 to 2025, and no female or male value lacks a total. In the 13,669 sets with no censored value, the female and male values add up to the total within the rounding of three figures printed to two significant figures in every set, with a median gap of zero and a largest gap of 7.5 per cent. In the 33,220 sets with a censored value, no uncensored value breaks the printed thresholds.
- Code outcome. BUILT on 16 October 2026. hivimpact/connectors/unicef_hiv.py maps SEX through _SEX and checks every pair in _split_agrees. The offline test gives 143,403 rows, the 49,625 rows for all sexes identical to the rows of the connector before R23, plus 46,889 female and 46,889 male rows, with no repeated key and no refused pair. Four altered inputs were refused as a female and male pair with the total kept, a female value raised by 20 per cent, a male value removed, a total removed and a female value set to "<100" beside a total of 99,000. A PMTCT value given sex F was not read.
- Run. Jason runs `python3 scripts/run_all.py --sources unicef_hiv --countries all` and then `python3 -m hivimpact.reconcile`. The panel grows by 93,778 rows.
- Run outcome. Jason's run of 16 October 2026 (scripts/run_all.py with --sources mn_hdc,unicef_hiv and --countries all) wrote the 143,403 unicef_hiv rows of the offline test, 49,625 rows for all sexes, 46,889 female rows and 46,889 male rows, and the gate refused no female and male pair. plhiv holds 16,861 rows for every sex value, new_infections 13,599 and aids_deaths 16,429, and the two PMTCT indicators keep sex "all" only, with 1,365 and 1,371 rows. The panel grew by 93,789 rows, from 359,963 to 453,752, the 93,778 female and male rows and the 11 mn_hdc rows. The reconcile counts did not change, with 53,543 derived comparisons, 11,294 independent, 1,854 subset and 73 insufficient, 78 independent pairs and 5,291 material divergences. The run validation warns of repeated values of 100 in unicef_hiv, 32,499 of the 49,287 aids_deaths rows, 18,796 of the 40,797 new_infections rows and 11,328 of the 50,583 plhiv rows, and 100 is the value the censoring convention writes for a published "<100". A second run of the same command later on 16 October 2026 rewrote data/panel/hiv_panel.csv with the same 453,752 rows and the same byte size, 243,404,960 bytes, and reconcile gave the same counts. The second run also overwrote data/panel/hiv_panel.prev.csv with the 453,752-row panel, so the copy of the 359,963-row panel from before the first run is gone.

## R24. UNODC estimates of people who inject drugs and of HIV among people who inject drugs (World Drug Report 2026 annex table 4.1)
- Decision. A new connector carries two series of table 4.1 of the statistical annex of the World Drug Report 2026 of UNODC (the United Nations Office on Drugs and Crime), HIV prevalence among people who inject drugs as kp_hiv_prevalence and the number of people who inject drugs as kp_size_estimate, with population_group pwid, at the year of estimate printed by UNODC and with the low and high estimates as the lower and upper bounds. A row enters only when the coverage reads National, the year of estimate is a single four-digit year, the medium estimate is a number, the gender reads "Both" or is blank, and the country name maps to an ISO3 code. A row whose reference or source names UNAIDS or GARPR (the Global AIDS Response Progress Reporting of UNAIDS) is not carried. The definition of injecting, the age group, the gender, the reference, the method and the source go into extra as printed. The hepatitis C and hepatitis B sheets stay outside the panel, and table 4.2 waits on a reading of workbook 4.2.
- Reason. kp_hiv_prevalence and kp_size_estimate already carry the same measures for people who inject drugs from unaids_aidsinfo, so the UNODC rows add no second definition. The UNAIDS export stamps every value with 2024, the year UNAIDS compiled the figure, while UNODC prints the year of the survey behind the estimate, so the UNODC rows place an estimate in the year the estimate describes. A row with a blank, subnational or city coverage would enter a national row with a figure for part of a country, and a range of years has no single year to carry. Of the 36 national UNODC rows meeting a UNAIDS value, 16 equal the UNAIDS value to the first decimal place, and a UNODC row citing UNAIDS republishes a figure the panel already carries from UNAIDS. reconcile holds a single relationship for a source pair, so a source mixing republished UNAIDS figures with figures from other reporting would show a republished figure as corroboration.
- Check. The 41 rows of the HIV sheet with a national coverage, a single year and a medium estimate hold 15 rows naming UNAIDS in the reference, which leaves 26 rows before the source check and the ISO3 check. The counts of the PWID sheet come with the build.
- Amendment, 16 October 2026. A row enters only when the method class printed by UNODC on the Notes sheet of the workbook (the classification of Mathers and colleagues, 2008) is a class carried for the sheet. For HIV prevalence the carried classes are A, A1 and A2 (seroprevalence studies) and D1 (official government estimates with no method reported, and modelling studies). For the number of people who inject drugs the carried classes are A (indirect estimates), B1 (mapping and enumeration), B2 (general population surveys) and D1 (official government estimates, expert judgement, modelling studies and consensus estimates). A prevalence from the registration or notification of cases at treatment services (class B) or from self-reported HIV status (class C), a number from treatment or other registers of drug users (class C), and a row with no method printed are not carried. A row whose definition of injecting names lifetime injecting ("lifetime" or "ever") is not carried, while a blank definition and every recent period (past month, past six months, past year, current, active) are carried, with the period in extra. The coverage gate reads the coverage as exactly "National", so a coverage with a qualifier is not carried.
- Reason for the amendment. The indicator catalogue defines kp_hiv_prevalence with the key population as the denominator, while a prevalence from treatment services takes the people tested at treatment services as the denominator. The Italian prevalence of 29.8 per cent for 2024 comes from 126,686 people at treatment services, against 1.7 per cent from UNAIDS. A register covers the people known to services and not the whole key population, so register counts would put a second definition under kp_size_estimate. A row with no method printed gives no way to confirm the definition. A count or a prevalence over everyone who ever injected takes in people who stopped injecting years before, while the metadata of the World Drug Report defines people who inject drugs by injecting during a specific reference period. The Kenyan survey of 2024, printed with the coverage "National" and a qualifier, covered four counties.
- Code outcome. hivimpact/connectors/unodc_pwid.py (source_id unodc_pwid) is registered in hivimpact/connectors/__init__.py and described in scripts/build_site.py. The connector downloads the workbook once into data/raw/unodc_pwid and checks the size and the sha256 against the copy of 16 October 2026 (169,407 bytes, sha256 e6ef72dd4725). sex and age_group read "all", as in the unaids_aidsinfo rows of kp_hiv_prevalence and kp_size_estimate, and the printed definition, age group, gender, coverage, sample size, reference, method, method class and source go into extra. A header differing from the header of 16 October 2026 refuses the whole sheet, and a country printed twice among the rows kept loses every row.
- Test.
