With the network blocked, the copy of 16 October 2026 gives 41 rows with no repeated key, 11 HIV prevalence rows for 11 countries from 2013 to 2024 and 30 rows of the number of people who inject drugs for 30 countries from 2009 to 2025, for 38 countries in all. The HIV sheet refused 192 rows for the coverage, 17 rows for citing UNAIDS or GARPR, nine rows of class B and three rows of lifetime injecting. The PWID sheet refused 152 rows for the coverage, 29 rows for citing UNAIDS or GARPR, 10 rows for a range of years, two rows with a medium estimate printed as text, four rows of class C, a single row with no method and three rows of lifetime injecting. A changed header refused the whole sheet in the test, and a repeated Indonesian row removed every Indonesian row. Of the 28 UNODC rows for a country with a UNAIDS value, only the Zambian prevalence shares the year 2024 (24.0 per cent from Harm Reduction International against 10.9 per cent from UNAIDS), so reconcile holds a single comparison for the pair, below the three comparisons needed to classify a pair.
- Table 4.2, read on 16 October 2026. Workbook 4.2 (245,497 bytes, sha256 4a965d1de561) prints 22 rows of the number of people who inject drugs by sex and 67 rows of HIV prevalence among people who inject drugs by sex, with a year, a source and free-text notes, and with no geographical coverage, no method class and no definition of injecting. Table 4.2 is not carried, because the gates of R24 read the coverage, the method class and the definition of injecting, and table 4.2 prints no coverage, no method class and no definition of injecting. Matching a row of table 4.2 to a row of table 4.1 on country and year gives three HIV rows (Indonesia 2023, Latvia 2022 and Spain 2023) and two rows of the number (Mexico 2016 and the United States 2018), and nothing in the two workbooks says a matched row comes from the same study, so the coverage of table 4.1 cannot pass to table 4.2. The Austrian HIV row of table 4.2 comes from a single low-threshold service in Vienna, as the notes print.
- Run. Jason runs `python3 scripts/run_all.py --sources unodc_pwid --countries all` and then `python3 -m hivimpact.reconcile`. The partial run replaces only the unodc_pwid rows of the panel.

## R25. Sri Lanka, yearly reported HIV cases of the National STD/AIDS Control Programme (NSACP)
- Decision. The Sri Lankan notified_hiv_cases rows come from the yearly count printed in the NSACP annual reports, read from the pdfplumber text layer. The NSACP quarterly returns serve as a cross-check and never enter the panel as a sum of quarters.
- Reason. Under R8 a yearly row is the yearly figure an agency published, and a sum of sub-year counts is a figure no agency published or verified. The quarterly returns print the cases reported in every quarter and the cumulative counts at the start and at the end of every quarter, and print no yearly total. NSACP revises quarterly counts after the first print. The fourth-quarter file of 2024 carries the words "Cleaned data" in the file name, and a search snippet of the second-quarter file of 2024 read 215 cases for the quarter and a cumulative 6,107, where the fourth-quarter file reads 214 and 6,105.
- Quarterly check, 16 October 2026. In the 12 quarterly files read (the fourth-quarter files of 2012 and of 2016 to 2025 and the second-quarter file of 2026), every quarterly table passes the chain check, and every year printed twice carries the same four quarterly counts in the file of the year and in the file of the following year. The quarterly counts add to 146 for 2011, 186 for 2012, 234 for 2015, 249 for 2016, 285 for 2017, 350 for 2018, 439 for 2019, 363 for 2020, 410 for 2021, 607 for 2022, 694 for 2023, 824 for 2024 and 874 for 2025.
- Sex. The quarterly files print the split by sex only as cumulative counts, so a yearly count by sex would be a difference of two cumulative counts, refused under R14. The cumulative male count at the end of 2022 reads 3,658 in the 2022 file and 3,661 in the 2023 file, and a difference of cumulative counts would move the revision into a single year.
- Annual reports read, 16 October 2026. Jason approved the downloads on 16 October 2026, and every file downloaded at the HEAD size. Figure 2.6 of the 2025 annual report ("Reporting of HIV Cases by Age and Sex", page 20) prints a data table of the HIV cases reported in every year from 2010 to 2025, with rows for children aged 0 to 14, women over 15, men over 15 and the total, and the rows for children, women and men add to the printed total in every year. The totals read 121 for 2010, 144 for 2011, 186 for 2012, 196 for 2013, 228 for 2014, 235 for 2015, 249 for 2016, 285 for 2017, 350 for 2018, 439 for 2019, 363 for 2020, 411 for 2021, 607 for 2022, 693 for 2023, 824 for 2024 and 874 for 2025. The prose on page 21 names 121 cases for 2010 and 874 for 2025, and 76 men over 15 for 2010 and 762 for 2025, the values of the table.
- Earlier annual reports. The annual report of the same year prints the Figure 2.6 total for 2016 (249), 2018 (350), 2019 (439), 2020 (363), 2021 (411 confirmed cases), 2023 (693 confirmed cases, with 693 in Table 1.1) and 2024 (824).
- Quarterly returns against the table. With the fourth-quarter files of 2010, 2011, 2013 and 2014 added to the 12 files read earlier, every year from 2010 to 2025 has a full-year quarterly table, and the end of the fourth quarter of every year equals the start of the first quarter of the next year. The quarterly sums equal the Figure 2.6 total in 11 years and differ by one or two cases in five years, 2011 (144 in the table and 146 in the quarters), 2014 (228 and 229), 2015 (235 and 234), 2021 (411 and 410) and 2023 (693 and 694). For 2021 and 2023 the annual report of the same year prints the table value, so the table holds the later NSACP count.
- Vintage. Every Sri Lankan row comes from the 2025 table, so every year carries the same vintage of the count and a single definition. The 2025 table starts at 2010 and the 2026 return covers two quarters, so the rows run from 2010 to 2025.
- Disagreements inside the reports, recorded only. The column labels of the Figure 2.5 chart in the 2025 report give 146 for 2011 and 438 for 2019, where the Figure 2.6 table prints 144 and 439. The 2023 report prints 611 men in a pie chart and 613 in the text and in Table 1.1. The connector reads the Figure 2.6 table alone.
- Sex in the annual report. For 2025 the gender figure reads 759 male, 112 female and three transgender cases, 874 in all, and the row of men over 15 (762) equals the male and transgender cases together. The row of children has no split by sex. A male row from the table would count transgender people as men, so the connector emits the total with sex "all" and carries the rows for children, women and men in extra.
- Status. Connector lk_nsacp (hivimpact/connectors/country/sri_lanka_nsacp.py) built on 16 October 2026 and registered as connector 55. The offline test on the scratch copies passed every gate and gave 16 rows for 2010 to 2025. Jason ran the connector on 16 October 2026. All 17 downloaded PDFs matched the hash of the copy read earlier on 16 October 2026, the connector emitted 16 rows, and the panel grew from 453,752 to 453,768 rows. The 16 Sri Lanka rows raise no validation warning.

## R26. Global Fund Price and Quality Reporting (PQR), units procured
- Decision. REFUSED. No PQR connector is built, and no id for units procured with Global Fund money is opened.
- Reason. The PQR note on data caveats (psm_pqrdatacaveats_note_en.pdf, 96,936 bytes, sha256 prefix 26d1d3b6ab69, downloaded on 16 October 2026 after Jason's yes) names the number of units, the pack size and the currency as the fields most often entered wrongly. According to the note, PQR reports include transactions still waiting for verification by the Local Fund Agent, and duplicated transactions have occurred in the PQR data warehouse. A yearly sum of units per recipient country would carry the unit errors, the unverified entries and any duplicated transaction into the panel, and a reader could not tell a corrected year from an uncorrected year.
- Access. The public Tableau export of the PQR transaction view timed out on 15 October 2026 and was not repeated.

## R27. Lithuanian TB/HIV incidence for 2009 (who_tme and unaids_aidsinfo)
- Decision. REFUSED for the single country-year. who_tme and unaids_aidsinfo refuse the Lithuanian tbhiv_incidence row for 2009 and keep every other Lithuanian TB/HIV row, including the Lithuanian tbhiv_deaths row for 2009.
- Reason. The validation of Jason's run of 16 October 2026 flags a Lithuanian TB/HIV incidence of 2,800 for 2009 (bounds 2,000 and 3,600) in who_tme and in unaids_aidsinfo, beside 88 for 2008 and 74 for 2010. The WHO Global Health Observatory (GHO) API, read on 16 October 2026, gives the same 2,800 under TB_e_inc_tbhiv_num and gives 3,400 Lithuanian TB cases of all kinds for 2009 under TB_e_inc_num. A TB/HIV incidence of 2,800 would put 82 per cent of Lithuanian TB cases among people with HIV, against 88 of 3,900 for 2008 and 74 of 3,100 for 2010, and would exceed the UNAIDS estimate of 2,000 people living with HIV in Lithuania for 2009 (1,900 in WHO GHO). A TB/HIV incidence above the whole estimated HIV population cannot be accurate, and AIDSinfo republishes the WHO TB/HIV estimates, so the refusal in unaids_aidsinfo keeps the derived pair consistent.
- Deaths. The Lithuanian tbhiv_deaths estimate of 73 for 2009 doubles the 36 for 2008 and the 29 for 2010, but 73 deaths breaks no bound in the WHO or UNAIDS estimates, and the earlier Lithuanian values reach 64 (2002), so the deaths row stays as published.
- Money rows. The who_tme docstring names Gabon's 2024 TB/HIV expenditure as CFA francs filed into a US dollar column, and the who_tme money rows the validation flags stay as published until a check of every flagged money series against the WHO TB dictionary.
- Offline test. who_tme refuses the Lithuanian incidence row for 2009 and keeps the incidence rows for 2008 and 2010 and every deaths row. The AIDSinfo TB/HIV export gives 4,748 rows, against 4,749 in the panel.
- Status. CHANGED in who_tme and unaids_aidsinfo, and Jason's rerun of 16 October 2026 carried the refusal into the panel. who_tme wrote 23,704 rows and unaids_aidsinfo wrote 104,531 rows, the Lithuanian refusal appears in the who_tme log and in the unaids_aidsinfo log, and the panel went from 453,760 to 453,758 rows with 9,199 tbhiv_incidence rows. Reconcile shows 66,763 national comparisons, 53,542 derived comparisons and 4,251 comparisons in the derived tbhiv_incidence pair, with 78 independent pairs and 5,291 material divergences unchanged. The validation flags 39 rows far above the scale of a series, down from 41.

## R28. Seven who_tme money values on the wrong scale (Gabon 2024, Timor-Leste 2024 and 2025, Vanuatu 2019)
- Decision. REFUSED for seven money values. who_tme refuses tbhiv_expenditure, tbhiv_funding_received, tb_funding_received_gf and tb_funding_received_domestic for Gabon in 2024, tb_funding_received_gf for Timor-Leste in 2024, tb_funding_expected_gf for Timor-Leste in 2025 and tb_funding_received_gf for Vanuatu in 2019, and keeps every other money value. The ruling settles the money item R27 kept open.
- Gabon. According to the who_tme docstring, the WHO TB data dictionary declares the money columns in US dollars. Gabon reported 731,795,829 of Global Fund TB money received in 2024, 573 times the 1,277,451 US dollars of Global Fund TB money Gabon expected for 2024 in the budget file, and the CFA franc is pegged at 655.957 to the euro. Gabon's domestic TB money received in 2024 (91,724,701) is 415 times the 220,967 US dollars Gabon expected for 2024, and the Gabonese TB/HIV expenditure of 325,375,873 for 2024 is 33,406 times the 9,740 of 2023. Read as CFA francs at about 606 to the US dollar, the rate the euro peg implies for 2024, the TB/HIV expenditure would still come to about 537,000 US dollars, 55 times the value for 2023, so who_tme refuses the four Gabonese values and converts no Gabonese value.
- Timor-Leste. Timor-Leste uses the US dollar, so a currency error cannot explain the Timorese values. The Global Fund TB money received in 2024 (309,530,584) is 114 times the 2,722,867 of 2023, and the Global Fund TB money expected for 2025 (272,525,512) is 112 times the 2,443,205 expected for 2024. Divided by 100, the Timorese values become 3,095,306 and 2,725,255, inside the range of every other year of the same series (1,402,591 to 10,236,642 received and 1,195,522 to 2,972,238 expected). The panel carries Global Fund HIV disbursements to Timor-Leste for 2024 of 4,884,899 US dollars in iati and 5,180,849 US dollars in oecd_crs.
- Vanuatu. The Global Fund TB money Vanuatu received in 2019 (10,758,488) is 53 to 120 times every other year of the series above 1,000 US dollars (89,647 to 202,206). The Vanuatu values of 256 received in 2021 and 272 expected for 2022 sit far below the series and stay as published, because a low value on a small Pacific grant may be real.
- Kept. The Kazakh TB/HIV budget and expected TB/HIV funding for 2024 (2,707,824) stay, because the Kazakh TB/HIV expenditure reached 2,377,607 in 2023 and 2,707,824 in 2024, and the actuals of two years support the budget. The Beninese TB/HIV budget for 2018 (506,789) stays, because 2018 opens the budget file and the Beninese expected TB/HIV funding for 2018 (404,065) sits on the same scale. The Moldovan TB/HIV budget and expected TB/HIV funding for 2023 (437,320) and the Nigerien expected TB/HIV funding for 2020 (96,538) and 2025 (392,308) stay, because no second figure shows the values to be wrong.
- Test. An offline test on synthetic budget and expenditure files refuses all seven values with a logged reason and keeps every other row.
- Effect. On a copy of the panel in memory, the refusals remove seven who_tme rows, the panel falls from 453,758 to 453,751 rows, and the rows far above the scale of a series fall from 39 to 32 (from 14 to seven in who_tme). No other source carries the five indicator ids, so the reconcile figures should stay unchanged.
- Status. CHANGED in who_tme, and Jason's rerun of 16 October 2026 carried the refusals into the panel. who_tme wrote 23,697 rows and logged all seven refusals, and the panel went from 453,758 to 453,751 rows. The validation flags 32 rows far above the scale of a series, down from 39, and the who_tme rows among the 32 are the seven money values R28 keeps (four tbhiv_funding_expected rows and three tbhiv_budget rows). Reconcile shows 66,763 national comparisons, 53,542 derived comparisons, 78 independent pairs and 5,291 material divergences, all unchanged.

## R29. Definition of tbhiv_comanagement_pct (unaids_aidsinfo)
- Decision. CORRECTED in the indicator catalogue. hivimpact/config/indicators.py now names tbhiv_comanagement_pct as the percentage of estimated HIV-positive incident TB cases on TB treatment and ART, and every published value stays in the panel.
- Reason. The catalogue described tbhiv_comanagement_pct as the percentage of TB patients living with HIV who are on ART, the ratio of tbhiv_on_art to tb_patients_hiv_positive. The validation of Jason's run of 16 October 2026 flags 67 AIDSinfo co-management values above 105 per cent, from 108 to 15,773.5, and a share of notified patients cannot pass 100. On the 1,227 country-years where the panel holds the AIDSinfo value and the WHO TME counts (2015 onwards, all ages), the AIDSinfo value lies within a tenth of tbhiv_on_art over the WHO estimate tbhiv_incidence in 702 country-years and within a tenth of tbhiv_on_art over tb_patients_hiv_positive in 32. The AIDSinfo denominator is therefore the WHO estimate of HIV-positive incident TB, and a value above 100 means the notified count on treatment exceeds the estimate, as in Albania in 2015 (six on ART against an estimate of four) and Bahrain in 2018 (six against an estimate of one).
- Values. The values above 105 per cent follow from the definition and stay as published. The 74 percent rows outside the range from zero to 105 in the validation include the 67 AIDSinfo co-management values, and the remaining seven rows (five pepfar_snu viral suppression values, one AIDSinfo TB preventive treatment value and one unicef_hiv vertical transmission value, kept under R30) are outside the scope of R29.
- Status. CHANGED in the catalogue. The panel rows are unchanged, and the corrected name reaches the site with the next site build after the site freeze ends.

## R30. Bounds of vertical_transmission_pct on the wrong scale (unicef_hiv)
- Decision. CHANGED in unicef_hiv and unaids_aidsinfo, with a new check in writers.validate. unicef_hiv and unaids_aidsinfo now drop a pair of published bounds whenever the bounds exclude the value, record the bounds as published in extra under bounds_refused and log the count. No connector divides or repairs a bound.
- Reason. In the panel of Jason's run of 16 October 2026, the published bounds exclude the value in all 1,371 unicef_hiv vertical transmission values. The lower bound runs from 2.86 to 10 times the value and the upper bound from 10.37 to 32.8 times the value. Kyrgyzstan reads 46.1 per 100 for 2010 with bounds of 440 and 486, and South Africa reads 7.0 for 2010 with bounds of 55 and 151. The published bounds divided by 10 bracket the value in all 1,371 values, and the AIDSinfo export of the previous round gives Kyrgyzstan 2010 as 47 with bounds of 45 and 51. The UNICEF SDMX service therefore publishes the bounds of HVA_PMTCT_MTCT on a scale 10 times the value. A connector records what the source said, and R28 refuses a money value in the wrong unit without converting the money value, so a bound on the wrong scale is refused in the same way. No other unicef_hiv indicator and no AIDSinfo vertical transmission value has bounds which exclude the value.
- Kyrgyzstan 2012. KEPT. The UNICEF value reads 106.1 per 100, above the ceiling of a share, and the AIDSinfo export of the previous round gives 98 with a lower bound of 90 and no upper bound. The UNAIDS indicator registry defines the rate as the estimated number of children newly infected through mother-to-child transmission among children born in the previous 12 months to women living with HIV, over the estimated number of women living with HIV who delivered in the previous 12 months. The numerator and the denominator are separate model estimates, the Kyrgyz value for 2012 continues a series which reads 80.2 for 2011 and 70.0 for 2013, and nothing in the source shows a unit error. The panel keeps the value as published, and the validation keeps listing the value among the percent rows above 105.
- AIDSinfo. The only other panel rows with bounds which exclude the value are eight AIDSinfo early infant diagnosis values, and the AIDSinfo export publishes the contradiction itself. Brunei reads ">98" for 2013, 2018 and 2019 with upper bounds of 79, 36 and 33, and reads 61 and 51 for 2022 and 2023 with upper bounds of 27 and 26. Timor-Leste reads 9, 8 and 25 for 2013, 2014 and 2022 with a lower and an upper bound of ">98". The export gives no way to tell whether the value or the bounds are wrong, so the eight values stay as published and the bounds go to extra, under the rule applied to the unicef_hiv bounds.
- Validation. writers.validate now flags a row whose lower bound exceeds the value or whose upper bound falls below the value, a check which the comment above the bound test already promised and the code never made. On the panel of 16 October 2026 the new check flags 1,379 rows, the 1,371 unicef_hiv values and the eight AIDSinfo values, and after Jason's rerun of unicef_hiv and unaids_aidsinfo the check should flag no row.
- Test. An offline run on the saved SDMX answer of 15 October 2026 gives 143,403 rows from the new unicef_hiv connector and 143,403 rows from the connector before R30, the same count as the unicef_hiv rows in the panel. The 1,371 vertical transmission rows differ only in the two bound columns, now empty, and in the bounds_refused entry, which holds the bounds as published, "440 to 486" for Kyrgyzstan 2010. An offline run of unaids_aidsinfo on scratchpad copies of the AIDSinfo exports for early infant diagnosis and PMTCT coverage gives 2,745 rows from the new connector and 2,745 rows from the connector before R30, and the eight Bruneian and Timorese rows differ only in the bounds, with ">98 to >98" in bounds_refused for Timor-Leste 2013. Every other row is identical between the new and the old connector.
- Effect. A rerun of unicef_hiv and unaids_aidsinfo keeps 143,403 and 104,531 rows and the panel total of 453,751, and empties the bound columns of the 1,371 vertical transmission rows and the eight infant diagnosis rows. Reconcile never reads the bound columns, so the comparison counts stay as before, and the new validation line goes from 1,379 rows to no row.
- Status. CHANGED in unicef_hiv, unaids_aidsinfo and writers, and Jason's rerun of 18 October 2026 carried the change into the panel. unicef_hiv wrote 143,403 rows and unaids_aidsinfo 104,531, the log reported 1,371 vertical transmission values and eight infant diagnosis values entered without bounds, the panel holds 453,751 rows with no row whose bounds exclude the value, reconcile is unchanged and the validation no longer lists rows whose bounds exclude the value.

## R31. Georgian notified HIV cases from Geostat (ge_geostat), the sex split and table 04
- Decision. BUILT. Connector ge_geostat (hivimpact/connectors/country/georgia_geostat.py) carries Georgian notified_hiv_cases for 2002 to 2025 from the Geostat morbidity table, and carries women and men from table 05 with age_group "all" for 2012 to 2025, the years in which women plus men equals the national count of the morbidity table. ge_geostat refuses the sex rows for 2010 and 2011, refuses table 04 whole, and carries no deaths, no cumulative count and no transmission category as a row. Reconcile declares the pair of ge_geostat and who_europe_hfa under notified_hiv_cases as derived.
- Source. The National Statistics Office of Georgia (Geostat) publishes the PC-Axis database through an open PxWeb API at pc-axis.geostat.ge, with no sign-in and no key. The SOURCE field of the Geostat tables names the Ministry of Internally Displaced Persons From The Occupied Territories, Labour, Health and Social Affairs of Georgia. Geostat last updated the morbidity table ("Morbidity of patients with HIV/AIDS and Number of Deaths") on 28 July 2026 and table 05 ("New Cases of HIV Infection (persons)") on 5 August 2026.
- National count. A year enters only when the cumulative registered count rises from the year before by exactly the new cases of the year, and on the API answers of 20 October 2026 every year from 2003 to 2025 meets the check. The count for 2002 opens the morbidity table and carries "first year of the table" in extra.cumulative_check.
- Sex split. Geostat labels the sex categories of table 05 "Women (age 15+)" and "Men (age 15+)". Women plus men equals the all-age count of the morbidity table in every year from 2012 to 2025, so the women and the men of table 05 together hold every new case of the year at any age, and table 05 counts transmission from mother to child among women and among men. The sex rows therefore carry age_group "all", and the Geostat label travels in extra.source_sex_label. Women plus men comes to 427 against 455 for 2010 and to 198 against 424 for 2011, so ge_geostat carries no sex row for 2010 or 2011.
- Table 04. REFUSED. In table 04 ("New Cases of HIV/AIDS (persons)", 1999 to 2025 by sex), women plus men equals the HFA_348 AIDS cases for 2012 to 2017 and 2019 and equals the new HIV cases for 2009 and for 2023 to 2025, differs from the AIDS cases and from the new HIV cases in every other year to 2019, and differs from the new HIV cases for 2020 to 2022, years for which the panel holds no Georgian AIDS count. The table 04 definition switches between AIDS cases and HIV cases without a note, so no year of table 04 can be read under a known definition.
- Deaths. NOT CARRIED. The Geostat deaths in the reporting year cover everyone registered with HIV and name no cause, and aids_deaths_reported counts deaths among people ever reported with AIDS, so the Geostat deaths would put a second definition under aids_deaths_reported.
- Cumulative counts and transmission. NOT CARRIED as rows. No panel id holds a cumulative register or notified cases by transmission category, and the seven transmission categories of table 05 travel in extra.transmission_as_printed as printed.
- Panel fit. who_europe_hfa holds Georgian notified_hiv_cases for 2000 to 2019 from measure HFA_350. The Geostat count equals the HFA_350 count in every year from 2009 to 2019 and differs by one to seven cases for 2002 to 2008, the years Geostat revised after Georgia joined the ECDC HIV/AIDS surveillance system (TESSy) in 2008 and 2009, according to the NOTEX field of the morbidity table. ge_geostat adds 2020 to 2025 (530, 530, 617, 612, 615 and 562) and the first Georgian notified case counts by sex in the panel.
- Reconcile. Geostat republishes the Georgian case register, and Georgia reports the same register to the European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC) through TESSy, so reconcile declares the Geostat and HFA_350 pair derived. Inference alone would class the Geostat and HFA_350 pair as independent, because the Geostat and HFA_350 counts agree within two per cent in 15 of 18 years, below the 90 per cent share the inference needs, and a revision of one register would then pass as two measurements disagreeing.
- Test. The offline test on the API answers saved on 20 October 2026 gives 52 rows, 24 for sex "all" (2002 to 2025), 14 for women and 14 for men (2012 to 2025), with logged warnings for 2010 and 2011 and no validation finding. An in-memory reconcile of the Georgian panel rows with the 52 rows adds 18 comparisons under notified_hiv_cases, all inside the declared derived pair.
- Effect. A run of ge_geostat should add 52 rows and take the panel from 453,751 to 453,803 rows, the national comparisons from 66,763 to 66,781 and the derived comparisons from 53,542 to 53,560, with 78 independent pairs and 5,291 material divergences unchanged.
- Status. Connector ge_geostat built on 20 October 2026 and registered as connector 56. Jason ran ge_geostat, by_belstat and ua_npsi together on 20 October 2026 with `--sources ge_geostat,by_belstat,ua_npsi --countries all`. The panel went from 453,751 to 454,990 rows (52 ge_geostat rows, 1,009 by_belstat rows and 178 ua_npsi rows). Reconcile shows 66,805 national comparisons and 53,584 derived comparisons, with 78 independent pairs and 5,291 material divergences unchanged, and infers ge_geostat and who_europe_hfa as derived over 18 comparisons, by_belstat and who_europe_hfa as derived over 20 comparisons and ua_npsi and who_europe_hfa as derived over four comparisons. No validation finding names any of the three sources. The ge_geostat log refuses the sex rows of 2010 and 2011, where women plus men in table 05 is 427 against 455 and 198 against 424.

## R32. Moldovan HIV counts of the National Bureau of Statistics databank
- Decision. NOT CARRIED. No connector reads the HIV tables of the statistical databank of the National Bureau of Statistics of the Republic of Moldova (NBS).
- Source. The NBS databank at statbank.statistica.md publishes three tables with HIV counts through an open PxWeb API, read on 20 October 2026. Table SAN020400 ("Population morbidity with separate infectious diseases", 1995 to 2017) carries "HIV carrier" and "AIDS infection" cases, table SAN020400rcl ("Population morbidity with infectious diseases", 2014 to 2024) carries "HIV" and "AIDS infection" cases, and table SAN021350 ("HIV incidence by Age group, Years and Sex", 2017 to 2025) carries new HIV cases by sex and six age groups. The SOURCE fields name the National Agency for Public Health for the morbidity tables and the Dermatology and Communicable Diseases Hospital for table SAN021350.
- Territory. The NOTEX field of all three NBS tables says the information is presented without the data on the districts on the left side of the river Nistru and on the municipality of Bender, from 2001 in table SAN020400. who_europe_hfa holds Moldovan notified_hiv_cases for 2000 to 2019 from measure HFA_350, with no WHO country note. The HFA_350 count exceeds the NBS count by 10.3 per cent for 2001 and by 24.0 to 43.0 per cent in every year from 2002 to 2019 (922 against 701 for 2019), while the HFA_350 and NBS counts for 2000 differ by two cases (176 against 174). The size of the gap from 2001 shows HFA_350 counting the left bank of the Nistru and Bender, and the NBS counts leave out the left bank of the Nistru and Bender.
- Reason. NBS rows under notified_hiv_cases would put a second territorial definition under the Moldovan series, and the NBS years 2020 to 2025 would extend the HFA_350 series with counts about 24 per cent lower for a reason unrelated to the epidemic. R7 carries the Ukrainian HFA_350 counts with the WHO note on the missing Ukrainian territories because HFA_350 is the only Ukrainian source and the WHO note travels in extra.who_country_note, a different case from two Moldovan sources counting two territories. The panel column admin1 names a subnational unit, and Moldova without the left bank of the Nistru and Bender is no subnational unit, so the NBS counts have no place in the panel.
- Further check. Table SAN020400 prints 618 for 2017 where table SAN021350 prints 615, and table SAN020400rcl prints 534 for 2020 where table SAN021350 prints 533. In table SAN021350 the women outnumber the men for 2021 (375 against 261), while the men outnumber the women in every other year from 2017 to 2025. The NBS tables carry no note on the 2017 and 2020 differences or on the sex split of 2021.
- Status. NOT CARRIED on 20 October 2026.

## R33. Belarusian notified HIV cases from the Belstat data portal (by_belstat)
- Decision. BUILT. Connector by_belstat (hivimpact/connectors/country/belarus_belstat.py) carries Belarusian notified_hiv_cases for 2000 to 2025 from Belstat indicator 10104000078, nationally by sex and by the age groups 0-14, 15-19, 20-29, 30-39 and 40+, and for the six regions and Minsk city by sex at all ages. by_belstat carries no row from indicator 10104000068 (transmission from mother to child) or from indicator 10104000066 (HIV cases at ages 0 to 19). Reconcile infers the pair of by_belstat and who_europe_hfa under notified_hiv_cases as derived, with no declaration.
- Source. The National Statistical Committee of the Republic of Belarus (Belstat) publishes the data portal dataportal.belstat.gov.by with an open JSON service (osids-public-api), with no sign-in and no key. The Belstat service answers a values query for at most 10 years, so by_belstat asks in windows of 10 years. The metadata of indicator 10104000078 define the count as "The number of cases of HIV infection newly identified in the reporting year", name the Demographic Statistics and Population Census Department of Belstat as the contact, and give the last update as 8 June 2026.
- Age groups. Belstat prints the youngest group as "up to 15 years", and Belstat indicator 10104000066 prints the same counts under "0-14 years" in every year from 2005 to 2025, so by_belstat writes age_group "0-14". The other groups keep the Belstat limits (15-19, 20-29, 30-39 and 40+), and the territory, sex and age labels as printed travel in extra.
- Checks. A row enters only when men plus women equals the total of the same territory and age group, when the five age groups add to the all-age count of the same sex (national rows), and when the six regions and Minsk city add to the national count of the same sex (region rows). The checks read an empty entry as zero, because indicator 10104000066 prints 0 for the women under 15 of 2025 where indicator 10104000078 leaves the entry empty, and an empty entry emits no row. On the answers of 20 October 2026 every check passes in every year, and five national entries under 15 are empty (men and women together for 2003, men for 2002 and 2003, women for 2003 and 2025).
- Transmission from mother to child. NOT CARRIED. Indicator 10104000068 gives the cases of transmission from mother to child as a percentage of the children born to mothers with HIV, an observed programme rate, and vertical_transmission_pct holds the modelled UNAIDS estimate, so the Belstat rate would put a second definition under vertical_transmission_pct.
- Panel fit. who_europe_hfa holds Belarusian notified_hiv_cases for 2000 to 2019 from HFA_350. The Belstat national count equals the HFA_350 count in 19 of the 20 years and differs for 2018 (2,353 against 2,386, 1.4 per cent lower), and the six regions and Minsk city add to the Belstat count of 2,353. by_belstat adds 2020 to 2025 (1,427, 1,496, 1,644, 1,463, 1,228 and 1,088), the first Belarusian notified counts by sex and age in the panel, and the first Belarusian region rows.
- Reconcile. Inference classes the by_belstat and who_europe_hfa pair as derived, because the Belstat and HFA_350 counts agree within two per cent in all 20 years, with a median difference of zero. The Georgian pair of R31 needed a declaration because the Geostat revisions put three of 18 years outside two per cent, and the Belarusian pair needs no declaration.
- Test. The offline test on the answers saved on 20 October 2026 gives 1,009 rows, 463 national and 546 for the regions and Minsk city, with no validation finding. A changed Brest count for men in 2010 removes eight rows with logged warnings, and an unreadable value stops the connector with no row. An in-memory reconcile of the Belarusian panel rows with the national by_belstat rows adds 20 comparisons under notified_hiv_cases, all inside the inferred derived pair.
- Effect. A run of by_belstat should add 1,009 rows. With the 52 rows of ge_geostat (R31), the panel should go from 453,751 to 454,812 rows, the national comparisons from 66,763 to 66,801 and the derived comparisons from 53,542 to 53,580, with 78 independent pairs and 5,291 material divergences unchanged.
- Status. Connector by_belstat built on 20 October 2026 and registered as connector 57. Jason ran ge_geostat, by_belstat and ua_npsi together on 20 October 2026 with `--sources ge_geostat,by_belstat,ua_npsi --countries all`. The panel went from 453,751 to 454,990 rows (52 ge_geostat rows, 1,009 by_belstat rows and 178 ua_npsi rows). Reconcile shows 66,805 national comparisons and 53,584 derived comparisons, with 78 independent pairs and 5,291 material divergences unchanged, and infers ge_geostat and who_europe_hfa as derived over 18 comparisons, by_belstat and who_europe_hfa as derived over 20 comparisons and ua_npsi and who_europe_hfa as derived over four comparisons. No validation finding names any of the three sources.

## R34. Ukrainian notified HIV cases from the PHC HIV monitoring portal (ua_npsi)
- Decision. BUILT. Connector ua_npsi (hivimpact/connectors/country/ukraine_npsi.py) carries Ukrainian notified_hiv_cases for 2016 to 2024 from the HIV monitoring portal of the Public Health Centre of the Ministry of Health of Ukraine (PHC), nationally for all ages, for the age groups 0-14, 15-24, 25-49 and 50+, and for men and women aged 15 and over, and for 22 oblasts and Kyiv city for 2017 to 2021. ua_npsi refuses the 2025 counts, refuses the Donetsk and Luhansk oblasts in every year, refuses every oblast row from 2022, and carries no AIDS case and no AIDS death. Reconcile infers the pair of ua_npsi and who_europe_hfa under notified_hiv_cases as derived, with no declaration.
- Source. The PHC portal "Моніторинг ВІЛ-інфекції в Україні" (HIV monitoring in Ukraine) at npsi.phc.org.ua reads an open JSON service with no sign-in and no key, with POST /Portal/GetHIVMonitoring for the catalogue of 494 indicators and POST /Portal/GetHIVIndicatorValues?id=N for the values of indicator N, read on 20 October 2026. ua_npsi sends the same anonymous requests as the public portal page. The descriptions of indicator 456 ("Кількість ЛЖВ, вперше взятих на облік"), of the age groups (parent 225 "Вікові групи") and of the sex split (parent 224 "Стать (15+)") name table 2000 ("Офіційно зареєстровані випадки ВІЛ-інфекції") of the annual reporting form No. 2-ВІЛ/СНІД, the people taken under medical supervision in the reporting year with a first-in-life HIV diagnosis. The description of indicator 456 counts HIV-exposed children only once the diagnosis is confirmed.
- Checks. A national row needs the four age groups of the same year to add to the national count, which holds exactly in every year from 2016 to 2024. A sex row needs men 15+, women 15+ and the children aged 0-14 to add to the national count, which holds in every year from 2016 to 2024, so the men and women of indicators 226 and 452 carry age_group "15+". An oblast row needs the 25 regions of the year (24 oblasts and Kyiv city) to add to the national count, which holds for 2017 to 2024. The regions add to 14,333 against 14,334 for 2016 and to 8,022 against 8,024 for 2025, with no Luhansk entry for 2025. The portal prints the oldest age group as "> 50", and the four age groups add to the national count, so the group holds ages 50 and over and ua_npsi writes age_group "50+" with the printed label in extra.age_note.
- 2025. REFUSED until the 2025 counts pass the checks. The portal prints no age group for 2025, so the national count of 8,024 for 2025 cannot be checked, and the home page of the portal says the update of the indicators for the results of 2025 has started ("Розпочате оновлення даних для показників за результатами 2025 року"). ua_npsi emits the 2025 rows once the 2025 counts pass the checks, with no change to the code.
- Territory. The description of PHC indicator 481 (people with HIV on the register at year end, from the same table 2000) says the register covers the territories under the control of the Government of Ukraine and names Crimea, Sevastopol and the Donetsk, Luhansk, Zaporizhzhia and Kherson oblasts as territories with incomplete counts, and the portal lists no region for Crimea or Sevastopol. The national rows carry the PHC territory statement in extra.territory_note, as the HFA_350 rows carry the WHO note on the missing Ukrainian territories under R7. The panel column admin1 names a whole subnational unit (R32), and parts of the Donetsk and Luhansk oblasts have been outside the control of the Government of Ukraine in every year since 2014, so ua_npsi refuses the Donetsk and Luhansk oblasts in every year. ua_npsi refuses every oblast row from 2022, the year of the full-scale invasion according to PHC indicator 736, because occupation during 2022 reached oblasts the PHC list does not name, Kharkiv oblast among the unnamed oblasts, where the PHC count falls from 575 for 2021 to 323 for 2022 and returns to 589 for 2023, and the portal does not say which oblast count covers a whole oblast for which year. The description of indicator 456 asks for the counts of the last three years to be read with the migration, the temporary occupation, the destroyed infrastructure and the difficulty of collecting data which followed the invasion, and the national rows from 2022 carry the PHC statement in extra.war_note.
- Panel fit. who_europe_hfa holds Ukrainian notified_hiv_cases for 2010 to 2019 from HFA_350. The PHC count equals the HFA_350 count for 2019 (16,357) and exceeds the HFA_350 count by 93 for 2016, 55 for 2017 and 63 for 2018, 0.35 to 0.65 per cent. ua_npsi adds 2020 to 2024 (15,658, 15,360, 12,212, 11,658 and 10,038), the first Ukrainian notified counts by age and sex in the panel, and the first Ukrainian oblast rows of notified cases.
- AIDS cases. NOT CARRIED. PHC indicator 440 ("Нові випадки", under the AIDS indicator 453, from table 3000) equals HFA_348 in every year from 2016 to 2019, and PHC indicator 672 prints the same counts as the people with an AIDS diagnosis among the newly registered. The 2020 to 2025 counts fit an AIDS diagnosis at HIV registration (4,139 of 15,658 people for 2020, beside 4,479 people with a CD4 count below 200 among the 13,791 tested at registration, from PHC indicator 488). The 2016 to 2019 counts equal HFA_348, which counts every person newly diagnosed with AIDS in the year, and an AIDS diagnosis at registration for 8,852 of the 14,334 people of 2016 (62 per cent) would need at least 2,865 people with an AIDS-defining illness despite a CD4 count of 200 or more, beside 4,226 people with a CD4 count below 200 and 1,761 people without a CD4 test. The count falls from 7,511 for 2019 to 4,139 for 2020 while the new HIV registrations fall from 16,357 to 15,658, and the portal carries no note on the fall. The PHC AIDS series therefore reads as every new AIDS diagnosis to 2019 and as AIDS at HIV registration from 2020, a change of definition without a note, the case R31 refused for Georgian table 04. AIDS at HIV registration is also the North Macedonian AIDS definition R7 refuses.
- AIDS deaths. NOT CARRIED. PHC indicator 202 ("Смерть від СНІД", death from AIDS) reads table 3002 ("Нові випадки смерті серед ВІЛ-інфікованих осіб", new deaths among people with HIV) and counts 3,253 deaths for 2016 and 917 for 2025. The indicator name gives a cause of death, the table title gives deaths among everyone with HIV, and aids_deaths_reported counts deaths from any cause among people ever reported with AIDS, a third definition, so the PHC deaths would put a second definition under aids_deaths_reported, as the Fijian HIV-related deaths would under R7.
- Other indicators. PHC indicators 206 and 327 print the national counts of indicator 456 again from other tables and add no row.
- Reconcile. Inference classes the ua_npsi and who_europe_hfa pair as derived, because the PHC and HFA_350 counts agree within two per cent in all four years from 2016 to 2019, with a median difference of 0.38 per cent. The Belarusian pair of R33 needed no declaration for the same reason, so the Ukrainian pair carries no declaration.
- Test. The offline test on the answers saved on 20 October 2026 gives 178 rows, nine national counts for all ages, 36 age rows, 18 sex rows and 115 oblast rows (22 oblasts and Kyiv city for 2017 to 2021), with logged warnings for the regions of 2016 and for 2025 and no validation finding. A changed Odesa count for 2018 removes the 23 oblast rows of 2018 with a logged warning, a value of 12.5 stops the connector with no row, and a renamed age group stops the connector with no row. An in-memory reconcile of the panel with the 178 rows adds four comparisons under notified_hiv_cases, all inside the inferred derived pair.
- Effect. A run of ua_npsi should add 178 rows. With the 52 rows of ge_geostat (R31) and the 1,009 rows of by_belstat (R33), the panel should go from 453,751 to 454,990 rows, the national comparisons from 66,763 to 66,805 and the derived comparisons from 53,542 to 53,584, with 78 independent pairs and 5,291 material divergences unchanged.
- Status. Connector ua_npsi built on 20 October 2026 and registered as connector 58. Jason ran ge_geostat, by_belstat and ua_npsi together on 20 October 2026 with `--sources ge_geostat,by_belstat,ua_npsi --countries all`. The panel went from 453,751 to 454,990 rows (52 ge_geostat rows, 1,009 by_belstat rows and 178 ua_npsi rows). Reconcile shows 66,805 national comparisons and 53,584 derived comparisons, with 78 independent pairs and 5,291 material divergences unchanged, and infers ge_geostat and who_europe_hfa as derived over 18 comparisons, by_belstat and who_europe_hfa as derived over 20 comparisons and ua_npsi and who_europe_hfa as derived over four comparisons. No validation finding names any of the three sources. The ua_npsi log refuses the 2025 count of 8,024 (no age group printed), the region rows of 2016 (25 regions add to 14,333 against 14,334) and 2025, and the region rows of 2022 to 2025 under R34.

## R35. European notified HIV cases from Table 1 of the joint report for 2024 data (ecdc_hiv_report)
- Decision. BUILT. Connector ecdc_hiv_report (hivimpact/connectors/ecdc_hiv_report.py) carries notified_hiv_cases for 2015 to 2024 for 51 countries and territories of the WHO European Region from Table 1 of the joint report of the European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC) and the WHO Regional Office for Europe, HIV/AIDS surveillance in Europe 2025 (2024 data), with Serbia excluding Kosovo under SRB and Kosovo under XKX. who_europe_hfa now refuses the Austrian, Luxembourg and Serbian HFA_350 counts in every year, the Ukrainian HFA_350 count for 2015 and the Spanish HFA_350 counts for 2018 and 2019, refuses the Austrian and Serbian HFA_348 counts in every year and the Spanish HFA_348 counts from 2014, and keeps the North Macedonian HFA_350 counts for 2017 and 2018. Reconcile declares the pair of ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa under notified_hiv_cases as derived. Tables 2, 3 and 13 of the 2025 report are NOT BUILT and go to ruling R36.
- Source. 2025-Annual-HIV-Report.pdf (20,246,576 bytes, sha256 dba8d8c5999d, last modified on 3 December 2025), downloaded from www.ecdc.europa.eu on 20 October 2026 after a HEAD request. Page 2 of the 2025 report places the report under the Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-ShareAlike 3.0 IGO licence (CC BY-NC-SA 3.0 IGO), the licence of the 2020 edition under R11. Table 1 (pages 62 and 63, two identical printed copies) gives the count and the rate per 100,000 of HIV diagnoses for 2015 to 2024 and the cumulative total since reporting began, for the 30 countries of the European Union and European Economic Area (EU/EEA) and 24 other countries of the WHO European Region, with Serbia printed as a whole and again as Serbia excluding Kosovo and Kosovo. The 2025 report is born-digital, and ecdc_hiv_report reads the pdfplumber text layer with no optical character recognition, under R25.
- Why the 2025 edition. HFA_350 reproduces Table 1 of the 2020 edition (R11) and stops at 2019. Countries keep sending late case reports for earlier years, so every edition revises the earlier years. The 2025 edition holds the latest revision of 2015 to 2019 and the only count in the panel for 2020 to 2024 in 48 of the 51 countries and territories ecdc_hiv_report carries, because among the European countries only Belarus (by_belstat), Georgia (ge_geostat) and Ukraine (ua_npsi) carry notified_hiv_cases rows for 2020 to 2024. A single table of a single edition keeps every year of a country series on one vintage of the count. ecdc_hiv_report therefore reads 2015 to 2019 from the 2025 edition as well, and reconcile compares the 2025 vintage with the 2020 vintage of HFA_350.
- What the count is. The value is the count of HIV diagnoses a country reported to The European Surveillance System (TESSy) for the calendar year of diagnosis, as Table 1 prints the count, with no adjustment for reporting delay. Footnotes 7 and 8 on page 29 say the reported diagnoses hold the people diagnosed with HIV for the first time together with the people with a previous positive diagnosis made abroad or in another setting of the reporting country, and 14.7 per cent of the EU/EEA diagnoses of 2024 were previous positive diagnoses. notified_hiv_cases counts a person the ministry of health registers as newly diagnosed in the country, and a person diagnosed abroad enters the register of the reporting country for the first time, so the Table 1 count fits notified_hiv_cases. A person diagnosed in another setting of the reporting country may also sit in the count of an earlier year, and Table 1 cannot show how many people repeat an earlier count, so extra.definition names the previous positive diagnoses on every row. The HFA_350 counts for 2015 to 2019 add to 99 per cent of the 2025 counts for the same country-years, so HFA_350 and Table 1 count the same group of people apart from late reports. Annex 5 says Armenia and Ukraine report by the date of statistics (the date a case enters the national statistics) in place of the date of diagnosis, and the Armenian and Ukrainian rows say so in extra.country_note. The cumulative totals are refused under R14.
- Refused by ecdc_hiv_report, 25 country-years.
  - Austria, every year (10 country-years). Footnote c of Table 1 says Austria uses study cohort data, and Annex 5 says Austrian surveillance rests on the Austrian HIV cohort study (AHIVCOS), which in 2022 held 64 per cent of the people who had ever received antiretroviral therapy in Austria. A cohort holding 64 per cent of the people ever treated in Austria does not count every Austrian diagnosis.
  - Türkiye, every year (10 country-years). Annex 5 says the Turkish HIV cases exclude people diagnosed with AIDS at the time of HIV diagnosis, the reason R7 gives for refusing the Turkish HFA_350 counts.
  - North Macedonia, 2015 and 2016. Annex 4a says North Macedonia reported the separate HIV record type for 1993 to 2016 and the joined HIV and AIDS record from 2017. Under the separate HIV record the North Macedonian AIDS count exceeds the HIV count in 10 years from 2000 to 2011 (R7), so a North Macedonian HIV count before 2017 can miss the people diagnosed with AIDS at HIV diagnosis. A joined record holds the AIDS diagnosis inside the HIV case, and the North Macedonian AIDS count of Table 13 stays below the HIV count of Table 1 in every year from 2015, so ecdc_hiv_report keeps 2017 to 2024.
  - The Russian Federation, 2015. Annex 5 says the Russian case definitions changed in 2016 and the Russian data for 2009 to 2015 cannot be compared directly with 2016 to 2024, the reason R7 gives for refusing the Russian HFA_350 counts before 2016.
  - Spain, 2018. Annex 5 says the 2018 data from Catalonia are not available. The Spanish comment in Annex 5 of the 2025 edition names no Spanish HIV gap for 2019, so ecdc_hiv_report keeps the Spanish count of 3,985 for 2019.
  - Ukraine, 2015. Table 1 prints 13 000, a round thousand, where every other Ukrainian year is an exact count, and HFA_350 prints the same 13,000.
- Dashes. Table 1 prints a dash for Andorra and Monaco from 2019 and for Turkmenistan and Uzbekistan in every year, 32 entries in all, and a dash emits no row.
- Serbia and Kosovo. The panel holds Kosovo under XKX, and the World Bank population under SRB (6,586,476 for 2024) excludes Kosovo, whose World Bank population under XKX is 1,594,353. Before R35 the XKX rows of the panel came from global_fund, global_fund_allocations, iati, oecd_crs and world_bank, and no SRB row mentioned Kosovo in extra. ecdc_hiv_report writes the Table 1 row for Serbia excluding Kosovo under SRB and the Kosovar row under XKX, and reads the row for Serbia as a whole for the gates only, so no case sits under two codes. The Serbian HFA_350 counts for 2010 to 2019 equal the row for Serbia as a whole in Table 1 of the 2020 edition (152, 134, 137, 156, 137, 183, 179, 186, 188 and 215), and the Serbian HFA_348 counts for 2010 to 2019 equal the row for Serbia as a whole in Table 14 of the 2020 edition (52, 60, 57, 49, 49, 50, 61, 64, 70 and 73). The HFA_350 and HFA_348 answers carry no note on Kosovo. The Serbian HFA counts therefore put Serbia and Kosovo together under SRB, and who_europe_hfa refuses the 20 Serbian HFA_350 years and the 20 Serbian HFA_348 years.
- Changes to who_europe_hfa (R7 amended).
  - Austria, HFA_350 and HFA_348, every year. Annex 4a of the 2020 and 2025 editions names a single Austrian HIV data source (AT-HIV) from 1980, and the Austrian counts of the 2025 edition for 2015 to 2019 (346, 321, 337, 236 and 268) exceed the HFA_350 counts (324, 291, 302, 199 and 225), so the HFA_350 counts rest on the Austrian cohort as well. Footnote c of Table 13 of the 2025 edition says Austria uses study cohort data, and Annex 4b of the 2025 edition gives the Austrian AIDS data source (AT-AIDS, from 1982) the note on the Austrian HIV cohort study.
  - Luxembourg, HFA_350, every year. Footnote c of Table 1 of the 2020 edition (page 68) gives 96 new HIV diagnoses for 2019 in the Luxembourg national statistics, counted by date of notification, against the 48 of HFA_350 and of the 2020 Table 1, counted by date of diagnosis. Table 1 of the 2025 edition prints 111 for 2019, gives 162, 169, 140, 120 and 111 for 2015 to 2019 against the HFA_350 counts of 75, 81, 67, 57 and 48, between 2.1 and 2.3 times the HFA_350 count in every year, and raises the cumulative Luxembourg count from 1,830 to 3,777. Late case reports raise the latest years of an edition most, as in the Dutch ratio of the 2025 count to the HFA_350 count (1.06 for 2015, 1.73 for 2019) and the Portuguese ratio (1.19 for 2015, 1.94 for 2019), while the Luxembourg ratio stays between 2.1 and 2.3 from 2015, four years before the latest year of the 2020 edition. The Luxembourg counts adjusted for reporting delay in Annex 6 of the 2020 edition (92, 80, 75 and 70 for 2016 to 2019, page 122) stay below the 2025 counts, as the Dutch and Portuguese adjusted counts for 2019 do (666 against 976 and 1,344 against 1,508), so the adjusted counts alone do not set Luxembourg apart, and the flat Luxembourg ratio does. HFA_350 holds the Luxembourg series on an earlier basis counting about half of the people the national register counts, so who_europe_hfa refuses the 20 Luxembourg years, and ecdc_hiv_report keeps the 2025 Luxembourg counts with footnote e of Table 1 in extra.country_note.
  - Spain, HFA_350 for 2018 and 2019 and HFA_348 from 2014. Annex 5 of the 2020 edition (page 121) says the 2018 data for Catalonia are not available because of extraction issues, says Asturias, Aragón, Cantabria and Melilla reported no data for 2019 while some other regions reported provisional data for 2019, and says the AIDS reports of several Spanish regions could not be included for 2014 to 2019. The Spanish HFA_348 refusal from 2014 replaces the R7 statement under which Spain keeps every AIDS year from 2000, and the R7 refusal of the Spanish HFA_350 counts before 2013 stays.
  - Ukraine, HFA_350 for 2015. HFA_350 prints 13,000 for 2015, a round thousand where every other Ukrainian year from 2000 to 2019 is an exact count.
  - North Macedonia, HFA_350, narrowed from every year to 2000 to 2016. Annex 4a of the 2025 edition dates the joined HIV and AIDS record from 2017, so who_europe_hfa keeps the HFA_350 counts for 2017 (44) and 2018 (45), which equal the 2025 Table 1 counts.
  - Counts. notified_hiv_cases from who_europe_hfa falls from 953 rows over 51 countries to 892 rows over 49 countries, with 127 country-years refused, and aids_cases_reported from who_europe_hfa falls from 934 rows over 49 countries to 888 rows over 47 countries, with 113 country-years refused.
- Kept with notes. ecdc_hiv_report keeps 15 countries with a comment of Annex 5 or a footnote of Table 1 in extra.country_note, Armenia and Ukraine (date of statistics), Luxembourg (footnote e), the Netherlands (the ATHENA cohort), France (underreporting estimated at 30 per cent for 2023), Ireland, Finland and Greece (people previously diagnosed abroad), Czechia and Poland (arrivals from Ukraine), Portugal (earlier years raised by work on underreporting), Italy (national surveillance from 2012), the Russian Federation (an aggregated data set), and Liechtenstein and Switzerland (Liechtenstein reported through Switzerland until 2021). Dutch surveillance rests on the ATHENA cohort, which holds 98 per cent of the people who entered HIV care in the Netherlands, while the Austrian cohort holds 64 per cent of the people ever treated in Austria. A count missing two per cent of the people in care stays a national count with a note, and a count missing more than a third of the people ever treated does not.
- Gates. ecdc_hiv_report refuses every row unless every printed copy of Table 1 reads the same, the year line runs from 2015 to 2024, every row holds 10 counts with a rate and a cumulative total with every count grouped in thousands as printed, Table 1 holds the 56 expected rows (30 EU/EEA rows and 26 non-EU/EEA rows), the country rows add to the EU/EEA, non-EU/EEA and area totals in every year, the area totals and the EU/EEA and non-EU/EEA totals add to the regional total in every year, Serbia excluding Kosovo plus Kosovo equals Serbia in every year, and the cumulative totals add to the printed cumulative totals. A cached copy unlike the copy of 20 October 2026 in size or sha256 is logged and still parsed, because every gate runs on the text. The gate test of 20 October 2026 refused all 12 altered copies of the Table 1 text, a Belgian count changed on one printed copy, the Belgian count changed on the two printed copies, a changed Kosovar count, a missing Maltese row, a misgrouped Spanish count, a changed Spanish cumulative total, a changed year line, a changed regional total for 2015, a rate printed with a comma, a dash beside a number, a row closed by another name and a report with no Table 1 page.
- Reconcile. Over the 225 country-years for 2015 to 2019 in ecdc_hiv_report and in who_europe_hfa, over 47 countries, the counts are equal in 123 (three with a zero in ecdc_hiv_report and in who_europe_hfa), the HFA_350 count is lower in 76 and higher in 26, and the HFA_350 counts add to 596,582 against 602,827 in the 2025 edition, 99 per cent. 170 of the 222 comparisons with a count above zero agree within two per cent (77 per cent), 52 differ by more than two per cent and 15 by more than 10 per cent. Inference needs 90 per cent of the comparisons within two per cent to class a pair as derived, so inference alone would treat a later revision of one count as two measurements disagreeing, and reconcile declares the pair of ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa derived. The largest gaps are Portugal for 2019 (1,508 in the 2025 edition against 778 in HFA_350), Spain for 2017 (4,351 against 3,795), the Netherlands for 2019 (976 against 563), Portugal for 2018 (1,501 against 1,109) and Portugal for 2017 (1,678 against 1,336), and the Dutch and Portuguese gaps grow toward 2019, the pattern of late case reports. Annex 6 of the 2020 edition adjusts the 2019 counts of the Netherlands and Portugal for reporting delay by factors of 1.18 and 1.73. Inference classes the pairs of ecdc_hiv_report with by_belstat (10 comparisons), ge_geostat (10) and ua_npsi (nine, median difference 0.64 per cent) as derived, with every comparison within two per cent.
- Tables 2, 3 and 13. NOT BUILT, for R36. Tables 2 and 3 give the diagnoses by sex, and men plus women fall short of the Table 1 count in 27 of the 56 country rows, with a regional shortfall of 192 for 2015 rising to 336 for 2024. Page 29 counts 218 transgender people and 80 people of unknown gender among the EU/EEA diagnoses of 2024. Table 13 gives the AIDS cases and would need refusals mirroring the HFA_348 refusals.
- Test. The offline test of 20 October 2026 on the downloaded copy, with the network blocked, gives 493 ecdc_hiv_report rows over 51 countries and territories (48 for 2015, 50 for 2016, 51 for 2017, 50 for 2018 and 49 for every year from 2019 to 2024), after 25 country-years refused and 32 dashes, with no validation finding. Restricted to XKX or to SRB the connector gives 10 rows, and restricted to 2020 to 2024 the connector gives 245 rows over 49 countries and territories. who_europe_hfa gives 892 notified_hiv_cases rows over 49 countries and 888 aids_cases_reported rows over 47 countries, with no validation finding.
- In-memory reconcile. Reconcile of the panel of 20 October 2026, with the who_europe_hfa rows replaced by the test rows and the 493 ecdc_hiv_report rows added, gives 67,059 national comparisons (from 66,805), 53,838 derived comparisons (from 53,584), 78 independent pairs, 43 declared pairs (from 42) and 218 source pairs (from 214). The 254 new comparisons all fall inside derived pairs, 225 in the declared pair of ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa and 29 in the inferred pairs of ecdc_hiv_report with by_belstat, ge_geostat and ua_npsi. The pairs of who_europe_hfa with by_belstat (20 comparisons), ge_geostat (18) and ua_npsi (four) are unchanged, and the independent comparisons stay at 11,294, so the 5,291 material divergences stay unchanged.
- Effect. A run of ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa should take the panel from 454,990 to 455,376 rows, adding 493 ecdc_hiv_report rows and removing 107 who_europe_hfa rows (61 notified_hiv_cases rows and 46 aids_cases_reported rows), with 229 countries and territories unchanged.
- Status. Connector ecdc_hiv_report built on 20 October 2026 and registered as connector 59, with the changes in who_europe_hfa, the declared pair in hivimpact/reconcile.py, the notified_hiv_cases entry of hivimpact/config/indicators.py and the who_europe_hfa entry of scripts/build_site.py. ecdc_hiv_report downloads the 2025 report, 20 MB, once into data/raw/ecdc_hiv_report. Jason ran ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa on 21 October 2026, and the R36 run outcome gives the figures.

## R36. European AIDS cases from Table 13 of the joint report for 2024 data (ecdc_hiv_report), and two more HFA_348 refusals
- Decision. BUILT. Connector ecdc_hiv_report now carries aids_cases_reported for 2015 to 2024 for 43 countries and territories of the WHO European Region from Table 13 (AIDS diagnoses and rates per 100 000 population, by country and year of diagnosis) of the 2025 joint report of ECDC and the WHO Regional Office for Europe, beside the Table 1 counts of R35. who_europe_hfa now refuses the Luxembourg HFA_348 counts in every year and the German HFA_348 count for 2019 (R7 amended). Reconcile declares the pair of ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa under aids_cases_reported as derived. Tables 2, 3, 14, 15, 17 and 18 of the 2025 report stay NOT BUILT.
- Source. The 2025-Annual-HIV-Report.pdf of R35 (20,246,576 bytes, downloaded from www.ecdc.europa.eu on 20 October 2026), and the HFA_348 answer of the WHO/Europe Data Warehouse (HFA_348.json, 345,536 bytes, dw.euro.who.int, 20 October 2026). Table 13 sits on pages 82 and 83, two identical printed copies, with the 56 rows of Table 1, a count and a rate for every year from 2015 to 2024 and a cumulative total. Table 13 marks Kosovo and Serbia excluding Kosovo with footnote f where Table 1 uses footnote g, and prints a dash in place of every Russian count and of the Russian cumulative. The text layer is read with pdfplumber and no optical character recognition, under R25.
- Why Table 13. HFA_348 reproduces the AIDS table of the 2020 edition (R7, R11) and stops at 2019, so Table 13 of the 2025 edition holds the latest revision of 2015 to 2019 and the only European AIDS count in the panel for 2020 to 2024. Table 13 plays the part for aids_cases_reported which Table 1 plays for notified_hiv_cases under R35, and keeps every year of a country series on one vintage.
- What the count is. The value is the count of AIDS diagnoses a country reported to TESSy for the calendar year of diagnosis, as Table 13 prints the count. Annex 1 of the 2025 report says only confirmed cases are reported at European level, under the WHO case definitions and the EU case definitions of Commission Decision 2002/253/EC, and national definitions may differ. extra.case_definition carries the Annex 1 text on every AIDS row, as the indicator definition of aids_cases_reported asks.
- Refused by ecdc_hiv_report, 81 country-years. A country refused under HFA_348 for a definition reason is refused in Table 13 as well, because Table 13 reads the same national AIDS surveillance, and aids_cases_reported then holds one definition across ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa.
  - Austria, every year (10 country-years). Footnote c of Table 13 says Austria uses study cohort data, and Annex 4b gives the Austrian AIDS source (AT-AIDS) the note on the Austrian HIV cohort study (R35).
  - Türkiye, every year (10). Annex 5 says the reported Turkish AIDS cases only include people diagnosed with AIDS at the time of HIV diagnosis, the reason R7 gives for refusing the Turkish HFA_348 counts.
  - North Macedonia, every year (five, with five dashes). Annex 5 says the North Macedonian AIDS cases include only people diagnosed with AIDS at the time of HIV diagnosis, the reason R7 gives for refusing the North Macedonian HFA_348 counts.
  - Ireland, every year (10). Annex 5 says the Irish AIDS cases and deaths among AIDS cases are now only reported if at the time of HIV diagnosis, after the change of the Irish reporting system in 2012, the reason R7 gives for refusing the Irish HFA_348 counts from 2012.
  - The United Kingdom, every year (10). Annex 5 of the 2025 report prints no British comment, and the British AIDS surveillance behind Table 13 is the surveillance whose move toward AIDS within three months of HIV diagnosis R7 gives for refusing the British HFA_348 counts from 2011.
  - Spain, 2015 to 2023 (nine). Annex 5 says the AIDS data of two Spanish regions could not be included for 2015 to 2022 and of three regions for 2023. Table 13 prints a dash for Spain for 2024, so the panel holds no Spanish AIDS count from ecdc_hiv_report, and who_europe_hfa refuses the Spanish HFA_348 counts from 2014 (R35).
  - Cyprus, every year (nine, with one dash). The 2025 edition raises the Cypriot AIDS counts for 2015 to 2019 from 13, 22, 15, 13 and 3 in HFA_348 to 70, 75, 67, 73 and 85, while the Cypriot HIV counts of Table 1 stay unchanged, and Annex 5 gives no reason. A revision raising the AIDS counts three to 28 times with no reason given and no change in the HIV counts is not a late case report, and a series moved by an unexplained revision cannot be checked against HFA_348.
  - Kyrgyzstan, every year (10). The 2025 edition lowers the Kyrgyz AIDS counts for 2015 to 2019 from 125, 72, 59, 56 and 46 in HFA_348 to 35, 33, 46, 47 and 20, and prints a Kyrgyz cumulative of 884 AIDS cases, below the 999 AIDS deaths the Kyrgyz national HIV centre counts to 1 January 2026 in the reports read from aidscenter.kg on 20 October 2026. A cumulative count of AIDS cases below the cumulative count of AIDS deaths cannot be complete, so ecdc_hiv_report refuses the Kyrgyz AIDS years, and who_europe_hfa keeps the Kyrgyz HFA_348 counts for 2000 to 2019.
  - Germany, 2019 (one). The German AIDS rows of the 2020 and 2025 editions are identical, because Germany stopped reporting AIDS cases to TESSy after the 2020 reporting round. The German count for 2019 (74) is therefore a first report frozen at the vintage of the 2020 edition, and falls to 0.31 times the German count for 2018 (242) while the German HIV diagnoses of Table 1 rose from 2,899 for 2018 to 3,134 for 2019. A ratio of 0.31 is the lowest of the 32 country rows of the AIDS table of the 2020 edition, and R7 refused the single Danish AIDS case of 2004 on the same kind of fall. Germany reports no AIDS count from 2020, and Table 13 prints dashes.
  - Malta, 2020 and 2023 (two). Annex 5 says the Maltese AIDS count for 2020 should read 1 and the count for 2023 should read 2, where Table 13 prints 0. The corrected values sit in a comment outside the table, where the gates cannot check the corrected values against the table totals, so ecdc_hiv_report refuses the two Maltese years in place of carrying the printed 0 or the corrected values.
  - Ukraine, 2020 to 2024 (five). The Ukrainian AIDS definition changed in 2020 to AIDS at HIV registration, the reason R34 gives for refusing the Ukrainian AIDS counts from 2020.
- Dashes. Table 13 prints 74 dashes among the country-years not refused, and a dash emits no row.
- Kept with notes. ecdc_hiv_report keeps nine countries with a Table 13 note in extra.country_note, Belgium (Annex 4b says the Belgian AIDS diagnoses are not exhaustively reported), France (Annex 4b gives French AIDS underreporting of 41 per cent for 2007 to 2009, 46 per cent for 2016 and 2017 and 42 per cent for 2023, and the count stays the national reported count), Germany (identical rows in the 2020 and 2025 editions, and footnote d on the German cumulative), Luxembourg (footnote e, date of diagnosis against the national date of notification), Liechtenstein and Switzerland (Liechtenstein reported through Switzerland until 2021), Armenia (the Annex 4b source code Se and the date of statistics), Azerbaijan (the AIDS counts for citizens of Azerbaijan in workbook 001_2_28.xls of the State Statistical Committee lie just below the Table 13 counts) and Ukraine (the date of statistics). A count underreported by an estimated 42 per cent stays a national reported count with a note, because underreporting lowers the French count without changing who the French count covers, while a count limited to AIDS at the time of HIV diagnosis counts a different group of people.
- Changes to who_europe_hfa (R7 amended).
  - Luxembourg, HFA_348, every year (20 country-years). The Luxembourg HFA_348 counts for 2015 to 2019 (10, 12, 6, 4 and 3) run at 0.56 to 0.67 times the Table 13 counts (18, 19, 9, 7 and 5), and the 2025 edition raises the cumulative Luxembourg AIDS count from 334 to 599. The ratio stays flat from 2015, four years before the latest year of the 2020 edition, the pattern R35 found for the Luxembourg HFA_350 counts, so HFA_348 holds the Luxembourg AIDS series on an earlier basis, and ecdc_hiv_report keeps the Table 13 counts.
  - Germany, HFA_348, 2019 (one country-year), for the reason given under ecdc_hiv_report above.
  - Counts. aids_cases_reported from who_europe_hfa falls from 888 rows over 47 countries to 867 rows over 46 countries, with 57 zeros and 370 rows with a note, and notified_hiv_cases stays at 892 rows over 49 countries.
- Gates. The Table 1 gates of R35 run on Table 13 apart, with the name map of Table 13 (footnote f in place of g) and the regional totals of the copy of 20 October 2026 (15,697 for 2015 falling to 7,161 for 2024), and the dash in place of the Russian cumulative counted as zero in the cumulative sums. A failed Table 13 gate refuses every Table 13 row and leaves the Table 1 rows in place, and a failed Table 1 gate refuses every Table 1 row and leaves the Table 13 rows in place. The gate test of 20 October 2026 refused Table 13 on all 15 altered copies of the Table 13 text, a Belgian count changed on one printed copy, the Belgian count changed on the two printed copies, a changed Kosovar count for 2024, a missing Maltese row, a misgrouped Spanish count, a changed Spanish cumulative, a changed year line, a changed regional total for 2015, a rate printed with a comma, a dash beside a number, a row closed by another name, a number in place of the Russian cumulative dash, a changed Kosovar count for 2015 with Serbia unchanged and a report with no Table 13 page, and Table 1 kept 493 rows on every altered copy. With the Table 1 pages removed, Table 1 was refused and Table 13 kept 395 rows.
- Reconcile. Over the 190 country-years for 2015 to 2019 in ecdc_hiv_report and in who_europe_hfa after the refusals, over 39 countries, the counts are equal in 120, the HFA_348 count is lower in 65 and higher in five, and the HFA_348 counts add to 68,702 against 69,757 in the 2025 edition, 98 per cent (99 per cent for every year from 2015 to 2018 and 96 per cent for 2019). 135 of the 190 comparisons agree within two per cent (71 per cent), and 25 differ by more than 10 per cent. Inference needs 90 per cent of the comparisons within two per cent to class a pair as derived, so reconcile declares the pair derived, as R35 does for notified_hiv_cases. The largest gaps are the Netherlands for 2019 (191 in the 2025 edition against 93 in HFA_348), Portugal for 2019 (267 against 172), Israel for 2019 (35 against 23) and France for 2019 (589 against 430), the latest year of the 2020 edition, the pattern of late case reports. The five higher HFA_348 counts (Albania 2019, Georgia 2019, Kazakhstan 2018, Montenegro 2016 and Tajikistan 2017) exceed the 2025 counts by one to three cases.
- Test. The offline test of 20 October 2026 on the downloaded copy, with the network blocked, gives 888 ecdc_hiv_report rows with no validation finding, 493 notified_hiv_cases rows over 51 countries and territories (unchanged from R35) and 395 aids_cases_reported rows over 43 countries and territories (43 for every year from 2015 to 2018, 40 for 2019, 37 for 2020, 38 for 2021, 36 for 2022, 35 for 2023 and 37 for 2024), after 81 country-years refused and 74 dashes. who_europe_hfa gives 867 aids_cases_reported rows over 46 countries and 892 notified_hiv_cases rows over 49 countries, with no validation finding.
- In-memory reconcile. Reconcile of the panel of 20 October 2026, with the who_europe_hfa rows replaced by the test rows and the 888 ecdc_hiv_report rows added, gives 67,249 national comparisons (from 66,805), 54,028 derived comparisons (from 53,584), 78 independent pairs, 44 declared pairs (from 42) and 219 source pairs (from 214). The 190 comparisons added after R35 all fall inside the declared aids_cases_reported pair of ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa, the independent comparisons stay at 11,294, and the 5,291 material divergences stay unchanged.
- Effect. A run of ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa should take the panel from 454,990 to 455,750 rows, adding 888 ecdc_hiv_report rows (493 notified_hiv_cases rows and 395 aids_cases_reported rows) and removing 128 who_europe_hfa rows (61 notified_hiv_cases rows and 67 aids_cases_reported rows), with 229 countries and territories unchanged. The R35 figure of 455,376 rows is superseded.
- Status. Built on 20 October 2026, with the Table 13 reading in hivimpact/connectors/ecdc_hiv_report.py, the two refusals in hivimpact/connectors/who_europe_hfa.py, the declared pair in hivimpact/reconcile.py, the aids_cases_reported comment of hivimpact/config/indicators.py and the ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa entries of scripts/build_site.py. Jason ran ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa on 21 October 2026, together with kg_aidscenter and me_ijzcg.
- Run outcome. Jason's run of 21 October 2026 (scripts/run_all.py with `--sources ecdc_hiv_report,who_europe_hfa,kg_aidscenter,me_ijzcg --countries all`) wrote the 888 ecdc_hiv_report rows of the offline test and 1,759 who_europe_hfa rows, 892 notified_hiv_cases rows over 49 countries and 867 aids_cases_reported rows over 46 countries, the counts of the offline test. The sha256 of the downloaded ecdc_hiv_report copy matched the copy read on 20 October 2026. The panel went from 454,990 to 455,790 rows, the sum of the R36, R38 and R39 effects. Reconcile shows 67,295 national comparisons, 54,074 derived comparisons, 11,294 independent, 1,854 subset and 73 insufficient, 223 source pairs, 78 independent pairs, 47 declared pairs and 5,291 material divergences, and the national comparisons, the source pairs, the declared pairs and the independent figures are the figures the in-memory reconcile of R39 predicted. The declared pairs of ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa hold 225 notified_hiv_cases comparisons and 190 aids_cases_reported comparisons. No validation finding names ecdc_hiv_report or who_europe_hfa.

## R37. Azerbaijan, the HIV and AIDS workbook of the State Statistical Committee (NOT CARRIED)
- Decision. NOT CARRIED. The panel takes no row from the State Statistical Committee of the Republic of Azerbaijan. The Azerbaijani notified_hiv_cases rows stay with who_europe_hfa (HFA_350, 2000 to 2019) and ecdc_hiv_report (Table 1, 2015 to 2024), and the Azerbaijani aids_cases_reported rows stay with who_europe_hfa (HFA_348) and ecdc_hiv_report (Table 13). Azerbaijan counts as closed for the Eastern Europe and Central Asia build, with no code change.
- Source. Workbook 001_2_28.xls (35,840 bytes), workbooks 001_2_6.xls (42,496 bytes) and 001_5_4.xls (408,576 bytes), the health yearbook healthcare_2026.pdf (6,106,025 bytes) and the health page of the committee (16,182 bytes), all downloaded from www.stat.gov.az on 20 October 2026. The cross-checks are the joint ECDC and WHO Europe report HIV/AIDS surveillance in Europe 2008 (0912_SUR_HIV_AIDS_surveillance_in_Europe.pdf, 5,669,014 bytes, published in 2009), the 2020 edition (hiv-surveillance-report-2020.pdf, 10,875,898 bytes) and the 2025 edition of R35, all from www.ecdc.europa.eu. The workbooks are read with a reader of the old Excel binary format written for the project, and the PDFs with pdfplumber and no optical character recognition (R25).
- Why. Workbook 001_2_28.xls counts citizens of Azerbaijan only. Table 10 of the 2025 joint report counts 1,152 of the 1,171 Azerbaijani HIV diagnoses of 2024 as people originating from Azerbaijan, and the workbook prints 1,152 for 2024. Against Table 1 of the 2025 joint report the workbook HIV counts for 2015 to 2024 run 1.4 to 4.9 per cent lower (704 against 727 for 2015, 541 against 556 for 2016, 559 against 567 for 2017, 630 against 656 for 2018, 686 against 721 for 2019, 547 against 559 for 2020, 671 against 690 for 2021, 749 against 766 for 2022, 886 against 919 for 2023 and 1,152 against 1,171 for 2024). Against Table 13 the workbook AIDS counts for 2015 to 2024 run zero to six cases lower. Carrying the workbook under notified_hiv_cases or aids_cases_reported would put a second population definition, citizens of Azerbaijan alone, under an id whose other Azerbaijani rows count everyone diagnosed in Azerbaijan, and population_group holds key populations only, so a citizens-only count has no place in the panel (the reasoning of R32).
- Early years. For 2000, 2005, 2007 and 2008 the workbook differs from HFA_350 and HFA_348 by more than the gap for foreign citizens can explain. The HIV count for 2000 reads 63 in the workbook against 40 in HFA_350 and 40 in the 2008 joint report. The AIDS count for 2000 reads four in the workbook against nine in HFA_348 and nine in the 2008 joint report, the AIDS count for 2005 reads 55 against 20 and 18, and the AIDS count for 2007 reads 41 against 200 and 201. The AIDS count for 2008 reads 59 in the workbook and in the 2008 joint report against 76 in HFA_348, which reproduces the 2020 edition, a later upward revision of the kind late case reports make. The HIV counts for 2005, 2007 and 2008 read 210, 441 and 436 in the workbook against 206, 437 and 433 in HFA_350 and 206, 441 and 436 in the 2008 joint report. The who_europe_hfa values stay unchanged, because the HFA counts and the joint reports, which cover everyone diagnosed in Azerbaijan, agree within four cases in every early year except the revised AIDS count for 2008.
- Other rows of the workbook. Rows 8 and 9 hold cumulative registers, which no panel id holds (R31), and row 7 holds children under 18 among the new AIDS cases, which no panel id holds.
- Other routes closed. Workbooks 001_2_6.xls and 001_5_4.xls hold no HIV row, page 69 of the health yearbook prints only the citizens table of workbook 001_2_28.xls, and the site of the Republican AIDS Centre (aids.az) carries news text with no table.
- Kept note. ecdc_hiv_report keeps the Azerbaijani note on Table 13 (R36).
- Status. Decided on 20 October 2026. No connector, no reconcile change and no run.

## R38. Kyrgyzstan, notified HIV cases from the situation report of aidscenter.kg (kg_aidscenter)
- Decision. BUILT. Connector 60, kg_aidscenter (hivimpact/connectors/country/kyrgyzstan_aidscenter.py), carries national notified_hiv_cases for Kyrgyzstan for 2013 to 2025 from page 1 of the situation report of 1 July 2026 of the Republican Centre for the Control of Blood-borne Viral Hepatitis and HIV of the Ministry of Health of the Kyrgyz Republic, as citizens of the Kyrgyz Republic plus foreign citizens registered in the Kyrgyz Republic. Reconcile declares the pair of kg_aidscenter and who_europe_hfa under notified_hiv_cases as derived.
- Source.
