"Ситуация во ВИЧ в КР на 01.07.2026" (the HIV situation in the Kyrgyz Republic on 1 July 2026), https://aidscenter.kg/uploads/topics/17853979831150.pdf (155,511 bytes, sha256 prefix 1cb790432d6f), with the report of 1 January 2026, https://aidscenter.kg/uploads/topics/17706325639491.pdf (153,513 bytes), as a cross-check, downloaded on 20 October 2026. The reports are born-digital and read with pdfplumber and no optical character recognition (R25). The connector downloads the July report once into data/raw/kg_aidscenter and reads the cached copy on every later run.
- Why citizens plus foreign citizens. Page 1 prints the citizens of the Kyrgyz Republic by region and year of registration, then a separate row of foreign citizens. Citizens plus foreign citizens equals Table 1 of the 2025 joint ECDC and WHO Europe report for 2015, 2016, 2017, 2020, 2022 and 2023, and differs by one case for 2018, 2019 and 2021 and by two cases for 2024. The sum therefore counts everyone diagnosed in Kyrgyzstan, the population of the other Kyrgyz notified_hiv_cases rows, and keeps one definition under notified_hiv_cases. The citizens count alone would repeat the citizens-only definition R37 refuses for Azerbaijan.
- Gates. The report enters only when every check passes. The nine region rows (Batken, Bishkek city, Osh city, Jalal-Abad, Issyk-Kul, Naryn, Osh, Talas and Chui) add to the overall total in every column, the cumulative column for 1987 to 2012 and the total column included. In every region row, in the overall total and in the foreign-citizens row, the cumulative count plus the yearly counts equals the printed total of the row. The overall total equals the header count of 15,043 citizens, the foreign-citizens total equals the header count of 951, and 15,043 plus 951 equals the header total of 15,994. A year whose count in the age table of page 2 differs from the overall total of page 1 enters no row. On the copy of 20 October 2026 every year from 2015 to 2026 agrees with the age table, and the age-table count for 1996 to 2014 (5,405) equals the page 1 count for 1987 to 2014.
- Refused.
  - 2026, the year of the report date, which covers half a year.
  - The region rows, the age table and the transmission tables, which count citizens only and so differ in population from the national count.
  - The children row, because the reports of 1 January 2026 and 1 July 2026 print different counts for every year from 2013 to 2025 (24 against 27 for 2013, for example), with no note on the change.
  - The rate per 100,000 people, the cumulative counts, the deaths since 1987 (3,911) and the deaths from AIDS since 1987 (1,027), which no panel id holds (R31).
  - The cascade of page 2 (11,015 people living with HIV registered, 7,962 on antiretroviral therapy and 7,366 with viral suppression on 1 July 2026). The report states no population for the cascade, and the count on treatment differs from the who_gho count of 7,596 for 2025, so the cascade cannot be placed under a panel definition.
- Revision. The report of 1 January 2026 prints 1,087 citizens for 2025, and the report of 1 July 2026 prints 1,086 after the Naryn count for 2025 fell from 18 to 17. extra.revision_note carries the change, and the latest year can still move by a case or two.
- Reconcile. Against HFA_350 for 2013 to 2019 the counts read 500 and 503, 647 and 649, 619 and 653, 748 and 764, 836 and 840, 867 and 876, and 842 and 850, within two per cent in five of the seven years. Inference needs 90 per cent of the comparisons within two per cent to class a pair as derived, so reconcile declares the pair derived, as R31 does for Georgia. Table 1 of the 2025 joint report prints 619 for 2015 and 748 for 2016, the kg_aidscenter counts, so the larger gaps against HFA_350 are revisions of the register after the 2020 edition. The pair of ecdc_hiv_report and kg_aidscenter needs no declaration, because inference classes the pair as derived (10 comparisons, 100 per cent within two per cent), as for Belarus under R33.
- Test. The offline test of 20 October 2026 on the downloaded copy, with the network blocked, gives 13 rows, 2013 to 2025 (500, 647, 619, 748, 836, 867, 842, 674, 840, 1,091, 1,054, 1,051 and 1,159), with no validation finding. The tamper test refused page 1 on all 10 altered copies of the page 1 text, a changed Batken count, a changed overall total, a changed foreign-citizens total, a changed header count of citizens, a changed header total, a missing Talas row, a second overall total row, a missing title, a gap in the year line and a short Chui row. A changed age-table count for 2025 refused 2025 alone.
- In-memory reconcile. Reconcile of the panel of 20 October 2026, with the R36 test rows in place and the 13 kg_aidscenter rows added, gives 67,266 national comparisons (from 67,249 after R36), 221 source pairs (from 219) and 45 declared pairs (from 44), with the 78 independent pairs and the 11,294 independent comparisons unchanged.
- Effect. A run of kg_aidscenter should add 13 rows, taking the panel from 455,750 rows after the R36 run to 455,763 rows.
- Status. Built on 20 October 2026, with the connector, the registration in hivimpact/connectors/country/__init__.py, the declared pair in hivimpact/reconcile.py, the notified_hiv_cases comment of hivimpact/config/indicators.py and the kg_aidscenter entry of scripts/build_site.py. Jason ran kg_aidscenter on 21 October 2026, together with the R36 run and me_ijzcg.
- Run outcome. Jason's run of 21 October 2026 wrote the 13 kg_aidscenter rows of the offline test, the sha256 of the downloaded report matched the copy read on 20 October 2026, and the log emits no row for 2026, the year of the report date. Reconcile infers ecdc_hiv_report and kg_aidscenter as derived over 10 comparisons and shows the declared pair of kg_aidscenter and who_europe_hfa over seven comparisons. No validation finding names kg_aidscenter.

## R39. Montenegro, notified HIV cases from the annual HIV report of the Institute of Public Health (me_ijzcg), and the Serbian statistics portal (NOT CARRIED)
- Decision. BUILT for Montenegro. Connector 61, me_ijzcg (hivimpact/connectors/country/montenegro_ijzcg.py), carries national notified_hiv_cases for Montenegro for 2001 to 2025 from Tabela 5 of the 2025 annual HIV report of the Institute of Public Health of Montenegro (Institut za javno zdravlje Crne Gore), as people registered with HIV in the year including people first registered in Montenegro after an HIV diagnosis made abroad, with men and women for 2025 from Tabela 1. Reconcile declares the pair of me_ijzcg and who_europe_hfa and the pair of ecdc_hiv_report and me_ijzcg under notified_hiv_cases as derived. NOT CARRIED for Serbia, because the data portal of the Statistical Office of the Republic of Serbia holds only a republication of a UNAIDS count.
- Source. "Godišnji izvještaj o HIV/AIDS-u u Crnoj Gori za 2025. godinu" (the annual report on HIV and AIDS in Montenegro for 2025, Podgorica, May 2026), https://api.ijzcg.me/media/files/1784726176-godisnji-hiv-za-2025.pdf (1,098,408 bytes, sha256 prefix a07b778e406a), downloaded on 20 October 2026. The public report list of the institute (https://api.ijzcg.me/v1/reports, report 18) links the 2025, 2024 and 2023 editions with no sign-in and no key. The detail route /v1/reports/31 answered 400 "Access token required", which counts as an auth refusal, so the work used the public list alone and never retried the detail route. The report is born-digital and read with pdfplumber and no optical character recognition (R25). The connector downloads the 2025 edition once into data/raw/me_ijzcg and reads the cached copy on every later run.
- Why the total registered. Tabela 5 prints, for every year, the total registered with HIV, the newly diagnosed at the HIV stage, the newly diagnosed at the AIDS stage, the people diagnosed abroad and first registered in Montenegro, and the deaths from AIDS. The total registered equals measure HFA_350 of who_europe_hfa in every year from 2001 to 2019 and Table 1 of the 2025 joint ECDC and WHO Europe report in every year from 2015 to 2024, so the total registered keeps one definition under notified_hiv_cases. Tabela 1 of the 2023 edition excludes the people diagnosed abroad, so the connector reads the 2025 edition alone. Tabela 5 of the 2024 edition prints the same five counts for every year from 2001 to 2024.
- Gates. The report enters only when every check passes. The year rows run without a gap and end in the year of the report title. In every year row the total registered equals the newly diagnosed at the HIV stage plus the newly diagnosed at the AIDS stage plus the people diagnosed abroad. The year rows add to the row "UKUPNO" (total) in every column. Men plus women in Tabela 1 equal the 2025 row of Tabela 5 in every column. The municipalities of Tabela 2 add to the printed total, and the printed total equals the newly diagnosed of 2025 at the HIV stage plus the AIDS stage.
- Refused.
  - The deaths from AIDS, a count of deaths caused by AIDS, which differs from the deaths from any cause among people reported with AIDS under aids_deaths_reported.
  - The newly diagnosed at the AIDS stage, a count of late diagnoses, which differs from the people newly reported with AIDS under aids_cases_reported.
  - The municipality counts of Tabela 2 and Tabela 6, which cover the newly diagnosed alone and so differ in population from the national count.
  - The five-year age groups of Tabela 1, which no panel id holds for notified cases (R31).
  - The testing counts of Tabela 3 and Tabela 4, persons tested outside private laboratories and blood donors, which differ from the tests counted under hiv_tests_performed.
  - The count on antiretroviral therapy at the end of 2025 (330 people), because who_gho already carries 331 for 2025.
- Serbia. The data portal of the Statistical Office of the Republic of Serbia (data.stat.gov.rs) lists every table in the tree of the home page (701,782 bytes, read on 20 October 2026). The single table naming HIV or AIDS is SDGUN03030101, the Sustainable Development Goal indicator of new HIV infections, whose result page (790,354 bytes) names the source "UN - AIDS/HIV", a republication of the UNAIDS modelled estimate the panel already carries from the UNAIDS connectors. The portal is NOT CARRIED, and Serbia closes with no national connector.
- Reconcile. Against HFA_350 the 19 comparisons for 2001 to 2019 agree exactly, and against Table 1 of the 2025 joint report the 10 comparisons for 2015 to 2024 agree exactly. Inference would class the pair of me_ijzcg and who_europe_hfa and the pair of ecdc_hiv_report and me_ijzcg as derived. Reconcile still declares the pair of me_ijzcg and who_europe_hfa and the pair of ecdc_hiv_report and me_ijzcg, because a later edition which revises one year by a few cases would move a pair of small counts outside two per cent, and a declared relationship keeps the classification of a pair tied to the provenance of the counts.
- Test. The offline test of 20 October 2026 on the downloaded copy, with the network blocked, gives 27 rows, 2001 to 2025 (1, 4, 7, 1, 10, 8, 9, 11, 14, 15, 9, 13, 11, 20, 19, 34, 26, 23, 26, 15, 13, 40, 54, 37 and 41) and 39 men and two women for 2025, with no validation finding. The tamper test refused the report on all five altered copies of the text, a changed count in a year row, a changed count in the row "UKUPNO", a changed count of women, a changed municipality count and a missing year row.
- In-memory reconcile. Reconcile of the panel of 20 October 2026, with the R36 and kg_aidscenter test rows in place and the 27 me_ijzcg rows added, gives 67,295 national comparisons (from 67,266 after R38), 223 source pairs (from 221) and 47 declared pairs (from 45), with the 78 independent pairs and the 11,294 independent comparisons unchanged.
- Effect. A run of me_ijzcg should add 27 rows, taking the panel from 455,763 rows after the R38 run to 455,790 rows.
- Status. Built on 20 October 2026, with the connector, the registration in hivimpact/connectors/country/__init__.py, the two declared pairs in hivimpact/reconcile.py, the notified_hiv_cases comment of hivimpact/config/indicators.py and the me_ijzcg entry of scripts/build_site.py. Jason ran me_ijzcg on 21 October 2026, together with the R36 and R38 runs.
- Run outcome. Jason's run of 21 October 2026 wrote the 27 me_ijzcg rows of the offline test, the sha256 of the downloaded report matched the copy read on 20 October 2026, and the panel reached the 455,790 rows of the effect. Reconcile shows the declared pair of ecdc_hiv_report and me_ijzcg over 10 comparisons and the declared pair of me_ijzcg and who_europe_hfa over 19 comparisons. No validation finding names me_ijzcg.

## R40. Bosnia and Herzegovina, the HIV reports of the two entity public health institutes (NOT CARRIED)
- Decision. NOT CARRIED. The World AIDS Day reports of the Institute for Public Health of the Federation of Bosnia and Herzegovina stay out of the panel, and the health status reports of the Public Health Institute of Republika Srpska stay out of the panel. Bosnia and Herzegovina keeps the national notified_hiv_cases rows of ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa, and the country closes with no national connector.
- Sources, downloaded on 20 October 2026. From www.zzjzfbih.ba, "Epidemiološki izvještaj HIV/AIDS 2024" (https://www.zzjzfbih.ba/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/Epidemioloski-izvjestaj-HIV_AIDS-2024-00000003.pdf, 818,847 bytes) and "Epidemiologija HIV infekcije i AIDS-a u Federaciji BiH" for 2023 (https://www.zzjzfbih.ba/wp-content/uploads/2023/12/Epidemiologija-HIV-infekcije-i-AIDS-a-u-Federaciji-BiH.pdf, 666,565 bytes), found through the WordPress media list of the site. From www.phi.rs.ba, "Здравствено стање становништва Републике Српске у 2024. години" (the health status of the population of Republika Srpska in 2024, https://www.phi.rs.ba/pdf/publikacije/Zdravstveno%20stanje%20stanovni%C5%A1tva%20RS%20u%202024.%20godini.pdf, 4,061,811 bytes). The Federation report for 2025 exists as an image alone, which R25 keeps out of the panel.
- Why the Federation reports fail. The 2024 report counts 26 new HIV diagnoses for 2024 and 363 diagnoses from 1994 to 1 December 2024, and the 2023 report counts 28 for 2023 and 337 from 1994 to 1 December 2023. A yearly count which ends on 1 December misses the registrations of December, so the Federation counts differ in period from the calendar-year counts of every other notified_hiv_cases row. The earlier years appear in a chart alone.
- Why the Republika Srpska report fails. Table 13 of the 2024 report counts 10 people notified with HIV positivity and seven people notified with AIDS in 2024, as two separate notifiable diseases, with no statement of whether the people notified with AIDS were newly diagnosed with HIV in 2024. The report therefore gives no count of new HIV diagnoses under the notified_hiv_cases definition, and the years 2020 to 2023 appear as rates per 100,000 in a chart alone.
- Check against the national count. Table 1 of the 2025 joint ECDC and WHO Europe report prints 29 new HIV diagnoses for Bosnia and Herzegovina in 2023 and 40 in 2024. The Federation count of 28 for 2023 leaves one case for Republika Srpska and the Brčko District, while the Republika Srpska chart prints a rate of at least 1.07 per 100,000 for 2023, about 12 cases at the population of about 1.1 million implied by 10 cases at 0.90 per 100,000 for 2024. The entity counts do not add to the national count, so an entity row would sit beside a national row with no stated relationship between the two counts.
- Admin1 rows. Entity rows would need the same definition and the same period as the national rows. The Federation period ends on 1 December and the Republika Srpska report separates HIV positivity from AIDS, so the Federation and Republika Srpska enter no admin1 row.
- Status. Recorded on 20 October 2026. No connector, no reconcile declaration and no code change.

## R41. Kazakhstan, the HIV table of the national health statistics compilation for 2023 and 2024 (NOT CARRIED)
- Decision. NOT CARRIED. The HIV table of the compilation stays out of the panel at the national level and at the region level, and Kazakhstan keeps the national notified_hiv_cases rows of ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa. Kazakhstan stays open, because the Kazakh Scientific Centre of Dermatology and Infectious Diseases (www.kncdiz.kz) and the Republican AIDS Centre (rcaids.kz) are REACH FAILURES held for the re-probe pass.
- Source. The statistical compilation on the health of the population of the Republic of Kazakhstan and the work of health organisations in 2023 and 2024, prepared by the Salidat Kairbekova National Research Centre for Health Development, https://www.gov.kz/uploads/2025/7/11/01dd198d40ba2718b7e43ced4424442c_original.3387496.docx (3,387,496 bytes), downloaded on 20 October 2026 and read from the XML of the Word file with no optical character recognition.
- Table read. "ВИЧ-инфекция (число больных и носителей)" (HIV infection, number of people ill and carriers), by region, for 2023 and 2024, as counts and rates per 100,000, for all ages and for the ages 0 to 14. The 17 regions and the cities of Astana, Almaty and Shymkent add to the printed national count of 3,861 for 2023 and 3,811 for 2024.
- Why the compilation fails. Table 1 of the 2025 joint ECDC and WHO Europe report prints 4,039 new HIV diagnoses for Kazakhstan in 2023 and 3,999 in 2024, so the compilation counts 178 fewer people in 2023 (4.4 per cent) and 188 fewer in 2024 (4.7 per cent). The compilation names no population for the table and states no reason for the gap, so the compilation count cannot be placed under the notified_hiv_cases definition of everyone newly diagnosed in the country. The region rows add to the compilation count and so share the unexplained population, which keeps the region rows out as R38 keeps out the Kyrgyz region rows.
- Status. Recorded on 20 October 2026. No connector, no reconcile declaration and no code change.

## R42. Bangladesh, the World AIDS Day files of the AIDS/STD Programme, a reporting year from November to October (BUILT)
- Decision. BUILT. Connector 62, bd_asp (hivimpact/connectors/country/bangladesh_asp.py), carries national notified_hiv_cases for Bangladesh for 2016 to 2025 from the World AIDS Day files of the AIDS/STD Programme (ASP) of the Directorate General of Health Services, as the people newly reported HIV positive in the ASP reporting year. The ASP reporting year runs from 1 November of the year before to 31 October. The row year is the year in which the reporting year ends, extra.reporting_period names the 12 months of every row, and the notified_hiv_cases description in hivimpact/config/indicators.py names the reporting year of the Bangladeshi rows.
- Sources. The 10 World AIDS Day files linked from the ASP pages of the Bangladesh national portal (https://asp.gov.bd/), every file on the Oracle cloud object store of the portal under https://objectstorage.ap-dcc-gazipur-1.oraclecloud15.com/n/axvjbnqprylg/b/V2Ministry/o/office-asp/2024/12/, downloaded on 21 October 2026 at the HEAD sizes of 14 October 2026, 2016 (8d4112c2e32d4ebd900c976abb987c11.pdf, 1,469,357 bytes), 2017 (1c7161515f904894b33c9a3ca45b4f49.pdf, 855,140), 2018 (c6bbf88499ba45a1a8857517712f1a40.pdf, 1,163,567), 2019 (a78f0b95cf174cdd8b5e79200f4bd5dd.pdf, 1,207,097), 2020 (788b26dbb9b0423d9a2003539d49809b.pdf, 1,473,040), 2021 (72fcbf5439fe40ef92f0a44e340a7ed8.pdf, 1,042,208), 2022 (f1ae11518ac947828bfffdecd1d3b0a4.pdf, 1,696,168), 2023 (763d813b862d47c199b8ff5d7b979d28.pdf, 1,350,391), 2024 (a30b7bb8bb6844c69bf7319d88084183.pdf, 1,886,421) and 2025 (82940dfa3995443e919f89587c073d1b.pdf, 1,171,656). The annual reports of the ASP for 2018 (6e6a249a91f04edabaddf2c9ea53b609.pdf, 1,597,715 bytes) and 2019 (d68a7c4f8d484593b79f1039f76e0b6d.pdf, 2,751,844 bytes) were downloaded on the same day as a cross-check and never enter the panel. Every file is born-digital and read with pdfplumber and no optical character recognition (R25). The connector downloads every World AIDS Day file once into data/raw/bd_asp and reads the cached copies on every later run.
- Why the reporting year from November to October. Every World AIDS Day file from 2017 states the period under the title, for example "(November, 2016- October, 2017)", and the 2016 file states "Reporting Period: 1st Nov, 2015 to 31st Oct, 2016". The 2019 annual report says the report covers January to December 2019 and still prints the count of the 2019 World AIDS Day file (813 new cases, 105 FDMN cases and one foreign case, 919 in all, with a cumulative 7,374), and the 2018 annual report prints the count of the 2018 World AIDS Day file (681 new cases and 188 FDMN cases, 869 in all, with a cumulative 6,455). The ASP therefore keeps one yearly series from November to October, and the period line of the World AIDS Day file is the authority for the period.
- Why notified_hiv_cases. A count of every person newly reported HIV positive in Bangladesh over 12 months is the notified_hiv_cases definition of a person the ministry of health registers as newly diagnosed anywhere in the country. The two months of shift from the calendar year leave the definition of the count unchanged and move the window, and the panel already carries a 12-month window ending in a month other than December under a shared id, with the window named in extra, in the rows of pepfar_vmmc ("PEPFAR fiscal year 2010, October 2009 to September 2010"). Ruling R40 refused the Federation of Bosnia and Herzegovina counts because a count ending on 1 December covers fewer than 12 months, and a count over fewer than 12 months still never enters notified_hiv_cases. The panel held no Bangladeshi notified_hiv_cases row on 21 October 2026, so no comparison crosses windows.
- FDMN. From 2017 every file names the Forcibly Displaced Myanmar Nationals (FDMN, the Rohingya refugees in the camps of Cox's Bazar) among the new cases, 63, 188, 105, 124, 188, 128, 158, 149 and 217 for 2017 to 2025. The FDMN cases are people newly diagnosed in Bangladesh and stay inside the national count, and extra.fdmn_cases carries the FDMN count of every year from 2017.
- Gates. The series enters only when every check passes. Every file states a reporting period from 1 November of the year before to 31 October of the World AIDS Day year. Every file prints one count line, and the line after the heading "New Cases in" starts with the World AIDS Day year. From 2017 every file prints one FDMN count, no larger than the new cases. The cumulative count of every year equals the cumulative count of the year before plus the new cases of the year, from 2016 to 2025, which holds in all nine steps (4,721, 5,586, 6,455, 7,374, 8,032, 8,761, 9,708, 10,984, 12,422 and 14,313).
- Refused.
  - The deaths. The 2024 file prints 195 deaths for 2024 and a cumulative 2,281 and then adds 131 deaths from the year before and 326 deaths in the year for a cumulative 2,412, and the 2025 file prints 219 deaths and a cumulative 2,666 built on the 2,412, so the yearly death counts do not chain.
  - The split by sex and by age, printed as shares and charts alone.
  - The yearly chart labels before 2016, whose reporting period no file states.
  - The testing and screening counts, which mix tests and screenings across programmes and differ from the tests counted under hiv_tests_performed.
- Test. The offline test of 21 October 2026 on the downloaded copies, with the network blocked, gives 10 rows, 2016 to 2025 (578, 865, 869, 919, 658, 729, 947, 1,276, 1,438 and 1,891), with no validation finding. The tamper test refused the series on all eight altered copies, a changed new count, a changed reporting period, a missing FDMN line, a doubled count line, a changed heading year, an FDMN count above the new cases, a changed cumulative count and a missing file.
- In-memory reconcile. Reconcile of the panel of 21 October 2026 with the 10 bd_asp rows added gives 67,295 national comparisons, 223 source pairs and 47 declared pairs, all unchanged, and no comparison names bd_asp.
- Effect. A run of bd_asp should add 10 rows, taking the panel from 455,790 rows to 455,800 rows.
- Status. Built on 21 October 2026, with the connector, the registration in hivimpact/connectors/country/__init__.py, the notified_hiv_cases description of hivimpact/config/indicators.py and the bd_asp entry of scripts/build_site.py. Waiting for Jason's run.

## R43. Maldives, the Maldives Health Statistics of the Ministry of Health, HIV positive cases detected, 2005 to 2016 (BUILT)
- Decision. BUILT. Connector 63, mv_moh (hivimpact/connectors/country/maldives_moh.py), carries national notified_hiv_cases for the Maldives for 2005 to 2016 from the Maldives Health Statistics of the Ministry of Health, as the HIV positive cases detected in the calendar year, Maldivians and expatriates together. The Maldivian count and the expatriate count of every year travel in extra.maldivians and extra.expatriates, and the notified_hiv_cases description in hivimpact/config/indicators.py names the Maldivian rows.
- Sources. The publications pages of the ministry site (https://health.gov.mv/en/publications) link the 2014 edition (https://health.gov.mv/storage/uploads/xRwX7DoW/cnba141l.pdf, 13,580,004 bytes, 72 pages) and the 2015-16 edition (https://health.gov.mv/storage/uploads/Arovzboj/fbwwmryf.pdf, 7,440,377 bytes, 198 pages), the two editions downloaded on 21 October 2026. The two files are born-digital and read with pdfplumber and no optical character recognition (R25). Table 2.11 of the 2014 edition covers 2005 to 2014, and Table 3-57 of the 2015-16 edition covers 2007 to 2016, every table with a line for Maldivians, a line for expatriates and a line for the total, from the SEARO Integrated Data Analysis System (SIDAS) of the Health Protection Agency (HPA) and the records of the National AIDS Programme of the HPA. The connector downloads the two files once into data/raw/mv_moh and reads the cached copies on every later run.
- Why a calendar year. The 2014 edition says the publication represents data from January to December 2014, and the Maldivian count, the expatriate count and the total of every year from 2007 to 2014 agree in the two editions, so the 2015-16 edition counts the same calendar years.
- Why notified_hiv_cases. The total counts every person found HIV positive in the Maldives in the year and registered in the national surveillance system of the HPA, which is the notified_hiv_cases definition of a person the ministry of health registers as newly diagnosed anywhere in the country. Expatriates found HIV positive in the Maldives are diagnosed in the country and stay inside the national count, as the Forcibly Displaced Myanmar Nationals stay inside the Bangladeshi count under R42, and extra.expatriates names the expatriate count of every year. The panel held no Maldivian notified_hiv_cases or new_diagnoses row on 21 October 2026, so no comparison arises.
- Gates. The series enters only when every check passes. Every edition prints the table heading once outside the contents, with the year range of the heading equal to the 10 consecutive years of the table. In every year of Table 2.11 and Table 3-57 the Maldivian count plus the expatriate count equals the total. The 2014 edition states the period from January to December 2014. The two editions agree on all three counts of every year from 2007 to 2014. The 2014 quick facts give two Maldivian and seven expatriate cases, equal to Table 2.11 for 2014. Table 3-11 of the 2015-16 edition gives the same total, Maldivian count and expatriate count for 2015 (a total of 18, two Maldivians and 16 expatriates) and 2016 (a total of 24, zero Maldivians and 24 expatriates) as Table 3-57.
- Narrative text. The narrative pages differ from the tables in places, and the tables are the authority, because Table 3-11 and Table 3-57 of the 2015-16 edition agree on 2015 and 2016, and Table 3-57 agrees with Table 2.11 of the 2014 edition on 2007 to 2014. The 2014 and the 2015-16 narratives say the highest Maldivian count (three) came in 2013, where Table 2.11 and Table 3-57 give three in 2012 and one in 2013. The 2015-16 narrative says two Maldivian cases came in 2016, where Tables 3-11 and 3-57 give two in 2015 and zero in 2016, and gives an expatriate range of seven (in 2015) to 16 (in 2007), where Table 3-57 gives seven in 2014 and 25 in 2007. The 2015-16 narrative repeats the 2014 narrative with the years changed, and extra.narrative_differs records the differences on every row.
- Refused.
  - The individuals screened for HIV (Table 2.10 of the 2014 edition and Table 3-56 of the 2015-16 edition), a count of people screened, which differs from the tests counted under hiv_tests_performed.
  - The 2017-18 edition (https://health.gov.mv/storage/uploads/Z3qlJ7wX/bhuje3nf.pdf, 14,174,988 bytes, 277 pages), a scanned file whose text layer holds 8,143 characters, which only optical character recognition could read (R25).
  - The 2020 edition (two versions, https://health.gov.mv/storage/uploads/79qgJ9YD/jjokopyg.pdf at 20,678,182 bytes and https://health.gov.mv/storage/uploads/BkoMELod/utbdxbkp.pdf at 20,308,656 bytes, with the same text), which gives six positive results among 53,069 HIV tests of 2020 and says every positive screening result goes to the National HIV/AIDS programme for confirmatory testing. A count of screening test results is a different definition from the cases detected of the 2014 and 2015-16 tables, and three of the six positives were expatriates screened before employment and returned home.
  - The 2021 edition, whose link on the ministry page points to a personal SharePoint share which answered an error page on 21 October 2026, with no further request.
- Test. The offline test of 21 October 2026 on the downloaded copies, with the network blocked, gives 12 rows, 2005 to 2016 (21, 27, 25, 16, 16, 21, 19, 27, 15, 9, 18 and 24), with no validation finding. The tamper test refused the series on all nine altered copies, a changed total, a changed Maldivian count, a changed year with the sum kept, a removed period statement, a changed quick fact, a changed Table 3-11, a doubled heading, a changed heading range and a missing file.
- In-memory reconcile. Reconcile of the panel of 21 October 2026 with the 12 mv_moh rows added gives 67,295 national comparisons, 223 source pairs and 47 declared pairs, all unchanged, and no comparison names mv_moh.
- Effect. A run of mv_moh should add 12 rows. After the bd_asp run, the panel should move from 455,800 rows to 455,812 rows.
- Status. Built on 21 October 2026, with the connector, the registration in hivimpact/connectors/country/__init__.py, the notified_hiv_cases description of hivimpact/config/indicators.py and the mv_moh entry of scripts/build_site.py. Waiting for Jason's run.

## R44. India, the State estimates of the National AIDS Control Organisation, Sankalak 2025, 2024 (BUILT)
- Decision. BUILT. Connector 64, in_naco (hivimpact/connectors/country/india_naco.py), carries the 2024 results of the round India HIV Estimates 2025 of the National AIDS Control Organisation (NACO) from Table 2.2 of Sankalak 2025, the seventh edition of the yearly NACO status report. The connector carries hiv_prevalence_15_49 as a percentage and plhiv, new_infections and aids_deaths as counts, with age group and sex "all" and the printed uncertainty bound in value_lower and value_upper, for India with admin1 empty and for the 34 States and Union Territories of Table 2.2 in admin1. The descriptions of the four ids in hivimpact/config/indicators.py name the in_naco admin1 rows.
- Sources. The Sankalak page of the NACO site (https://naco.mohfw.gov.in/sankalak/) links the version "as on 30.01.2026" (https://naco.mohfw.gov.in/wp-content/uploads/2026/06/Sankalak-Booklet_Seventh-Edition-As-per-NACO-website-as-on-30.01.2026.pdf, 9,727,542 bytes), uploaded on 28 June 2026, with Table 2.2 on page 48 rotated a quarter turn. The NACO media library, read through the public WordPress media search, holds a revised version R_1 (https://naco.mohfw.gov.in/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Sankalak-Booklet_Seventh-Edition_R_1.pdf, 26,851,772 bytes), modified on 31 July 2026 and uploaded on 3 August 2026, with Table 2.2 upright on page 48 and Table 2.1 on page 39. The two files were downloaded on 21 October 2026, are born-digital and are read with pdfplumber and no optical character recognition (R25). The connector downloads the two files once into data/raw/in_naco and reads the cached copies on every later run.
- Why R_1 with the January version. R_1 is the later version and prints Table 2.2 upright, so the panel rows cite the R_1 URL. The January version is the file the Sankalak page links, and the connector reads the rotated Table 2.2 of the January version character by character as a cross-check. The two versions print the same figures and bounds in every row of Table 2.2, and extra.cross_check names the January URL on every row.
- Why one round. Page 60 of Sankalak says NACO uses the Spectrum tool, and says the latest round of the India HIV Estimates replaces the earlier rounds, because every round updates the modelling tools and the input data. The connector carries the 2025 round only, and an earlier Sankalak edition, which would put another round of the same years beside the 2025 round under the same ids, is not read.
- Why the four ids. The adult (15-49) prevalence of Table 2.2 is the hiv_prevalence_15_49 definition, and the PLHIV size, the new HIV infections and the annual AIDS-related deaths of Table 2.2 are the plhiv, new_infections and aids_deaths counts for all ages, age group "all" under R17 and R18. Under R14 every State and Union Territory figure is carried as published and never summed, and the India figure is the India row of Table 2.2. The State counts of Table 2.2 add up to the India count, and the State bounds never add up to the India bound, so extra.aggregation_warning on every admin1 row says to take the India figure from the row with admin1 empty.
- Admin1 names. Table 2.2 abbreviates three names, written out in admin1. "A&N Islands" becomes Andaman and Nicobar Islands, "DNH&DD" becomes Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu, and the shared row of Jammu and Kashmir and Ladakh takes the DHS name "Jammu, Kashmir & Ladakh". Delhi keeps the NACO name. extra.table_label keeps the printed label where the label differs from admin1. Lakshadweep has no row in Table 2.2.
- Gates. The table enters only when every check passes. Every version prints the Table 2.2 heading once outside the contents, for the year 2024, with a header naming the four carried measures. Every line of the table is a State, Union Territory or India row of the expected form, including the Jammu and Kashmir and Ladakh row printed over three lines and the Manipur row whose death rate is printed on the lines around the row. The 34 State and Union Territory rows come in the expected names and order, followed by India. Every figure lies inside the bound printed with the figure. The 34 State and Union Territory counts add up to the India count exactly for people living with HIV (2,561,161), new infections (64,465) and AIDS-related deaths (32,162). Table 2.1 of R_1 prints the India figures of Table 2.2 in lakh and thousand, 25.61 lakh (21.89 to 30.67) people living with HIV, 64.47 thousand (42.96 to 105.11) new infections, 32.16 thousand (21.47 to 49.95) AIDS-related deaths and an adult prevalence of 0.20 per cent (0.17 to 0.24). The January version and R_1 print the same figures and bounds in every row.
- Not carried. The incidence per 1,000 uninfected population, the AIDS deaths per 1,00,000 population, the declines since 2010, the incidence-prevalence ratio and the incidence-mortality ratio of Table 2.2, which the panel has no id for. extra.not_carried names the measures on every row.
- Reconcile. NACO estimates the HIV burden of India with the Spectrum model, UNAIDS publishes the India estimates NACO submits, and Table 2.1 of Sankalak cites the UNAIDS 2025 estimates beside the India HIV Estimates 2025. In the panel of 21 October 2026 the AIDSinfo figures for India of 2024 equal the in_naco figures rounded to two significant figures in all nine values and bounds of the three counts, and the adult prevalence of 0.2 per cent of AIDSinfo and of the World Bank is the in_naco figure of 0.20 per cent rounded. hivimpact/reconcile.py declares in_naco "derived" against unaids_aidsinfo, unicef_hiv and who_gho for plhiv, new_infections and aids_deaths, and against unaids_aidsinfo and world_bank for hiv_prevalence_15_49, 11 declared entries.
- Test. The offline test of 21 October 2026 on the downloaded copies, with the network blocked, gives 140 rows, 35 for every id, with no validation finding, and the State counts add up to the India counts. The tamper test refused the table on all 13 altered copies, a changed India count in R_1, a changed Assam row in R_1, a changed Assam row in the January version, a Goa prevalence outside the bound, a removed Sikkim row, a renamed Delhi, a changed heading year, a doubled heading, a changed lakh figure in Table 2.1, a removed Manipur death-rate line, a changed column header, a changed death bound in the January version and a missing January file.
- In-memory reconcile. Reconcile of the panel of 21 October 2026 with the four national in_naco rows added gives 67,300 national comparisons, 228 source pairs and 52 declared pairs, against 67,295, 223 and 47 without in_naco. The five new pairs are all "derived" with basis declared, in_naco against unaids_aidsinfo for the four ids (median relative differences of 0.0151 for plhiv, 0.0072 for new_infections, 0.0051 for aids_deaths and zero for prevalence) and against world_bank for prevalence (zero). The UNICEF and WHO GHO entries form no pair, because the panel holds no 2024 India row of unicef_hiv or who_gho for the three counts.
- Effect. A run of in_naco should add 140 rows. After the bd_asp and mv_moh runs, the panel should move from 455,812 rows to 455,952 rows.
- Status. Built on 21 October 2026, with the connector, the registration in hivimpact/connectors/country/__init__.py, the descriptions of four ids in hivimpact/config/indicators.py, the declared pairs of hivimpact/reconcile.py and the in_naco entry of scripts/build_site.py. Waiting for Jason's run.

## R45. Armenia, the HIV situation analysis 2025 of the National Centre for Infectious Diseases, citizens plus foreign citizens, and the Armenian counts of ECDC and WHO Europe (BUILT, with refusals)
- Decision. BUILT. Connector 65, am_ncid (hivimpact/connectors/country/armenia_ncid.py), carries national notified_hiv_cases for Armenia for 2015 to 2025 from the HIV situation analysis 2025 (ՄԻԱՎ ՎԱՐԱԿԻ ԻՐԱՎԻՃԱԿԱՅԻՆ ՎԵՐԼՈՒԾՈՒԹՅՈՒՆ 2025) of the National Centre for Infectious Diseases (NCID) of the Ministry of Health of Armenia, as the HIV cases registered in the year among citizens of Armenia (Figure 2) plus foreign citizens (Figure 1). The citizen count and the foreign-citizen count of every year travel in extra.citizens and extra.foreign_citizens, and the notified_hiv_cases description in hivimpact/config/indicators.py names the Armenian rows. The same ruling refuses every Armenian notified_hiv_cases and aids_cases_reported row of ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa, because the Armenian counts of the two aggregators count citizens of Armenia alone.
- Sources. The statistics page of the NCID site (https://ncid.am/statistics) links the situation analysis as a Word file (https://ncid.am/u/statistics/files/pbN9GNZTwNVrfE9fe5.docx, 245,389 bytes, created on 13 February 2026 and modified on 1 June 2026), downloaded on 21 October 2026. The Word file holds every chart as chart XML with the plotted values, and the connector reads the values from the chart XML with zipfile and ElementTree, with no optical character recognition (R25). The performance report on the national strategic plan for 2024 (https://ncid.am/u/statistics/files/lMBmBAkTXl38UNxSse.pdf, 383,381 bytes), the situation note of June 2026 (https://ncid.am/u/statistics/files/vYRWGd0knThK4SamMM.pdf, 246,337 bytes) and the yearbook 2024 (https://ncid.am/u/statistics/files/fCLFtQr5rsqUk9eLvA.pdf, 2,944,430 bytes) were downloaded on 21 October 2026 as cross-checks and never enter the panel. The connector downloads the Word file once into data/raw/am_ncid and reads the cached copy on every later run.
- Why citizens plus foreign citizens. notified_hiv_cases counts every person the ministry of health registers as newly diagnosed anywhere in the country. Section 1.1 of the situation analysis counts 6,625 HIV cases among citizens of Armenia from 1988 to 2025, and section 1.2 counts 490 HIV cases among foreign citizens registered in Armenia, so the national register holds two counts, and only the sum of the citizen count and the foreign-citizen count of a year counts everyone diagnosed in Armenia. Ruling R38 carries Kyrgyzstan as citizens plus foreign citizens for the same reason, and ruling R37 keeps a citizens-only count out of the panel.
- Why the ECDC and WHO Europe Armenian rows are refused. Table 1 of HIV/AIDS surveillance in Europe 2025 (ecdc_hiv_report) gives 294, 303, 358, 429, 448, 369, 425, 535, 500 and 544 for Armenia for 2015 to 2024, equal to the citizen counts of Figure 2 in every year. The Armenian HFA_350 counts of the WHO Europe Health for All database (who_europe_hfa) for 2000 to 2019 lie within nine cases of the citizen counts of Figure 2, equal in six years (2000, 2003, 2004, 2005, 2008 and 2009), and run 16 to 39 cases below citizens plus foreign citizens for 2015 to 2019. The performance report on the national strategic plan for 2024 prints 544 for 2024, the citizen count. Under ruling R37 a citizens-only count never enters notified_hiv_cases, and keeping the aggregator rows beside the am_ncid rows would put two definitions under one id for Armenia. The Armenian AIDS counts go with the Armenian HIV counts. The joined TESSy record holds the Armenian AIDS diagnosis inside an Armenian HIV case, Table 13 lies within 14 cases of the AIDS counts among citizens in Figure 3 of the situation analysis for 2015 to 2024, and the Armenian HFA_348 counts equal the citizen AIDS counts of Figure 3 in six years from 2006 to 2011 and lie within eight cases in every year from 2005 to 2019. Figure 3 counts citizens alone, so no Armenian aids_cases_reported row remains in the panel.
- Gates. The series enters only when every check passes. The title of the situation analysis is followed by the report year 2025. Exactly one chart follows every caption the connector reads (Figure 1, foreign citizens by year of registration, Figure 2, citizens by year of registration, and Figure 3, HIV cases, AIDS cases and deaths among citizens). The years of Figure 1 run without a gap from the first year of the caption range to the report year. Figure 2 runs from "<1996" through consecutive years to the report year. The yearly citizen counts of Figure 2 add to the 6,625 cases of section 1.1. The yearly foreign-citizen counts of Figure 1 add to no more than the 490 cases of section 1.2 (405 for 2015 to 2025). The citizen HIV series of Figure 3 equals Figure 2 in every shared year. The 2025 counts of the text, 467 citizens (338 men and 129 women) and 47 foreign citizens, equal the 2025 chart values.
- Text against chart. Section 1.2 says about 60 per cent of the foreign-citizen cases were registered in the last five years. Figure 1 gives 268 of the 490 (55 per cent) for 2021 to 2025 and 292 (60 per cent) for 2020 to 2025. The connector reads the yearly counts of Figure 1 and no share, so the share sentence enters no row.
- Not carried.
  - The years before 2015, because the situation analysis prints no yearly count of foreign citizens before 2015.
  - The citizen series alone (R37).
  - The AIDS series and the death series of Figure 3, which count citizens alone, and the deaths of Figure 3 count deaths among people with HIV, a different measure from aids_deaths_reported.
  - The test counts of Figure 14 (citizens tested) and Figure 17 (tests carried out), which differ from who_hts for 2019 (161,152 against 164,933) and 2024 (215,272 against 215,472).
  - The 4,257 people on antiretroviral therapy of section 1.1, which the who_gho row for Armenia already holds, and the who_gho row stays, because the situation analysis prints 4,257 inside the national cascade with no citizen qualifier.
  - The count of people who know the HIV status, which the situation analysis prints as 5,143 in one paragraph and as 5,146 in a second paragraph.
  - The viral load counts, printed for a single year.
  - The key population test counts, which repeat the global_fund rows (10,550 tests among gay, bisexual and other men who have sex with men).
  - The split by sex, printed as chart shares, with counts for 2025 citizens alone.
  - The cumulative, age and transmission charts (R31).
  - The place of infection, which the panel has no id for, and the chart by region.
  - The situation note of June 2026, which holds half-year figures.
  - The yearbook 2024, a scanned file which only optical character recognition could read (R25).
  - The Statistical Committee of Armenia (armstat.am) and the National Institute of Health (nih.am), which publish no HIV table.
- Test. The offline test of 21 October 2026 on the downloaded copy, with the network blocked, gives 11 rows, 2015 to 2025 (312, 321, 377, 450, 485, 393, 447, 605, 575, 598 and 514), with no validation finding. The tamper test refused the series on all 14 altered copies, a changed Figure 2 count for 2020, a changed Figure 1 count for 2025, a Figure 1 count for 2015 raised above the 490 of the text, a changed Figure 3 citizen count for 2010, a missing title, a repeated Figure 2 caption and chart, a missing Figure 1 caption, a gap in Figure 1, a Figure 2 ending in 2024, a changed 6,625, a changed 47, a changed 129, a missing foreign-citizen paragraph and a value which is not a count. The offline check of hivimpact/connectors/ecdc_hiv_report.py and hivimpact/connectors/who_europe_hfa.py, with the network blocked, refuses Armenia in every year under Table 1, Table 13, HFA_350 and HFA_348, and refuses no Georgian or Azerbaijani year.
- In-memory reconcile. Reconcile of the panel of 21 October 2026 with the 60 Armenian aggregator rows dropped and the 11 am_ncid rows added gives 67,285 national comparisons, 223 source pairs and 47 declared pairs, against 67,295, 223 and 47 before. The 10 comparisons gone are the Armenian comparisons of ecdc_hiv_report against who_europe_hfa for 2015 to 2019 under notified_hiv_cases and aids_cases_reported, and no comparison names am_ncid, because no other source holds an Armenian notified_hiv_cases row after the refusals. With the in_naco rows of R44 also added, the figures would be 67,290 comparisons, 228 pairs and 52 declared pairs.
- Effect. A run of ecdc_hiv_report, who_europe_hfa and am_ncid should drop 60 rows and add 11. ecdc_hiv_report should fall from 888 rows to 868 (notified_hiv_cases 483 rows over 50 countries, aids_cases_reported 385 rows over 42), and who_europe_hfa from 1,759 rows to 1,719 (notified_hiv_cases 872 rows over 48 countries, aids_cases_reported 847 rows over 45). After the bd_asp, mv_moh and in_naco runs, the panel should move from 455,952 rows to 455,903 rows. The run command is `cd "/Users/newlivehung/Desktop/15. HIV Funding Impacts" && /opt/anaconda3/bin/python3 scripts/run_all.py --sources ecdc_hiv_report,who_europe_hfa,am_ncid --countries all`.
- Status. Built on 21 October 2026, with the connector, the registration in hivimpact/connectors/country/__init__.py, the Armenian refusals and docstrings of hivimpact/connectors/ecdc_hiv_report.py and hivimpact/connectors/who_europe_hfa.py, the notified_hiv_cases and aids_cases_reported notes of hivimpact/config/indicators.py, and the am_ncid, ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa entries of scripts/build_site.py. Waiting for Jason's run.

## R46. Kazakhstan, the country reports, activity reports and Optima analyses of the Kazakh Scientific Centre of Dermatology and Infectious Diseases, and the regional AIDS centre pages (NOT CARRIED)
- Decision. NOT CARRIED. The yearly counts of new HIV cases in the Global AIDS Monitoring (GAM) country reports, the national progress reports and the activity reports of the AIDS service published by the Kazakh Scientific Centre of Dermatology and Infectious Diseases (KNCDIZ, kncdiz.kz) stay out of the panel, with the Optima HIV allocation analyses, the Almaty city AIDS centre pages (gcaids.kz) and the Astana AIDS centre site (www.astana-aids.kz). Kazakhstan keeps the national notified_hiv_cases rows of ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa, and ruling R41 stands.
- Sources. Born-digital PDF files under https://kncdiz.kz/files/, downloaded on 21 October 2026 and read with pdfplumber and no optical character recognition (R25). The country reports page (/ru/documentation/publications1/country_reports/) links the national progress report on 2013 (00000843.pdf, 819,672 bytes), the GAM reports on 2019 (00007836.pdf, 543,448 bytes), 2020 (00011934.pdf, 197,217 bytes), 2021 (00011935.pdf, 232,514 bytes), 2022 (00012190.pdf, 895,657 bytes), 2023 (otchet2023AID.pdf, 352,740 bytes) and 2024 (00019432.pdf, 214,065 bytes), and the older reports 00001894.pdf (790,099 bytes), 00002582.pdf (637,865 bytes), 00003162.pdf (214,089 bytes), 00004968.pdf (540,559 bytes) and 00007835.pdf (1,370,154 bytes). The archive page (/ru/documentation/publications1/archive/) links the activity reports for 2012 (00002420.pdf, 981,634 bytes), 2013 (00000867.pdf, 467,107 bytes), 2014 (00001779.pdf, 441,256 bytes), 2015 (00002614.pdf, 309,185 bytes), 2016 (00003084.pdf, 3,231,294 bytes) and 2017 (00004587.pdf, 506,352 bytes). The Optima analyses are 00018527.pdf (9,149,515 bytes, a Google machine translation of the 2023 analysis) and 00023009.docx (2,223,410 bytes, the 2025/26 analysis of April 2026).
- Why the yearly counts are refused. The reports print the yearly count of new HIV cases among citizens of Kazakhstan alone in most years, 1,862 for 2012, 1,993 for 2013, 2,208 for 2014 ("среди граждан РК", among citizens of Kazakhstan), 3,518 for 2019, 3,342 for 2020 and 3,862 for 2023, against 2,131 (2013) and 2,342 (2014) in who_europe_hfa and 3,665 (2019), 3,467 (2020) and 4,039 (2023) in ecdc_hiv_report. Under ruling R37 a citizens-only count never enters notified_hiv_cases. The GAM reports on 2022 and 2024 print everyone, 4,007 new cases in 2022 (3,877 citizens and 130 foreign citizens) and 4,003 in 2024 (3,811 citizens and 192 foreign citizens), within four cases of the ecdc_hiv_report counts of 4,003 and 3,999. Two years of a total beside the ECDC rows would put a second source under the same definition for 2022 and 2024 alone, and the ECDC and WHO Europe rows already cover every year from 2000.
- What the reports confirm. The ECDC and WHO Europe counts exceed the citizen counts by 125 to 188 cases in every year read (2013, 2014, 2019, 2020, 2022, 2023 and 2024) and lie within four cases of the GAM totals of 2022 and 2024, so the Kazakh rows of ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa count citizens and foreign citizens together and stay in the panel under the notified_hiv_cases definition. The 3,811 citizens of the 2024 GAM report equal the 2024 count of the R41 compilation, which confirms the R41 compilation as a citizens-only count. The activity report on 2014 gives the cumulative register at 1 January 2015 as 24,216 cases, 22,109 citizens, 1,589 foreign citizens and 518 people tested anonymously, a cumulative count with no id (R31).
- Refused with the yearly counts.
  - The other country reports (00001894.pdf, 00002582.pdf, 00003162.pdf, 00004968.pdf, 00007835.pdf and 00011935.pdf), which print no yearly national count of new cases.
  - The activity report on 2016, whose text layer is unreadable (a broken font encoding), which only optical character recognition could read (R25).
  - The Optima analyses, which hold modelled allocation scenarios.
  - The Almaty city AIDS centre, whose home page (78,980 bytes) and statistics page (/ru/ctatistika, 51,595 bytes) show the epidemiological situation as JPG images alone (R25), and www.astana-aids.kz, which answered a page under construction (3,759 bytes).
  - rcaids.kz, which failed the TLS handshake in the re-probe pass of 21 October 2026, with no further request.
- Status. Recorded on 21 October 2026. No connector, no reconcile declaration and no code change. The surveys among people who inject drugs on the same site are built under R47.

## R47. Kazakhstan, the KNCDIZ surveys among people who inject drugs, weighted HIV prevalence for 2020, 2022 and 2024 (BUILT)
- Decision. BUILT. Connector 66, kz_kncdiz (hivimpact/connectors/country/kazakhstan_kncdiz.py), carries national kp_hiv_prevalence for Kazakhstan with population group pwid, sex "all" and age group "18+", as the weighted HIV prevalence among people who inject drugs printed in Table 6 of the KNCDIZ survey reports, 8.3 per cent for 2020, 7.2 per cent for 2022 and 6.4 per cent for 2024. The row year is the survey year, as ruling R24 stamps unodc_pwid. value_lower and value_upper stay blank, because the reports print no interval for the weighted figure, and the sample share with the 95 per cent interval of the sample share travels in extra. The kp_hiv_prevalence description in hivimpact/config/indicators.py names the Kazakh rows.
- Sources. The report on the sentinel surveillance of HIV prevalence among people who inject drugs in Kazakhstan in 2022 (https://kncdiz.kz/files/00019909.pdf, 1,690,128 bytes, Almaty 2023) and the report on the bio-behavioural survey among people who inject drugs in Kazakhstan in 2024 (https://kncdiz.kz/files/00019805.pdf, 1,872,847 bytes, Almaty 2025), linked from the country reports page and the surveys page of the KNCDIZ site, downloaded on 21 October 2026. The two files are born-digital and read with pdfplumber and no optical character recognition (R25). The connector downloads the two files once into data/raw/kz_kncdiz, checks the size and the SHA-256 prefix of every copy, and reads the cached copies on every later run.
- Survey design, as the two reports print the design. A cross-sectional survey at sentinel sites (23 sites in 2022 and 24 in 2024), respondent-driven sampling (RDS) with at most three recruits per participant, people aged 18 and over who injected drugs at least once in the last 12 months and lived at the site for six months, linked anonymous testing for HIV, hepatitis C and syphilis, and ethics approval IRB-A042 of 12 December 2013. The 2022 sample holds 4,554 respondents and the 2024 sample 3,944. The reports weight the answers with RDS-Analyst and print the weighted figure as the HIV prevalence of the population of people who inject drugs in Kazakhstan. The 2024 report renames the survey from sentinel surveillance (ЭСР) to a bio-behavioural survey (БПИ) and names a new method when explaining the fall in hepatitis C, with the design, the eligibility rule and the weighting of 2022 kept.
- Why the weighted figure and age group "18+". kp_hiv_prevalence is the share of a key population living with HIV, and the RDS weighting is the step the survey uses to move from the sample to the population, so the weighted figure is the population estimate and the sample share describes the sample alone. The survey counts people aged 18 and over, and the age group follows the eligibility rule of the survey, as the za_thembisa rows of kp_hiv_prevalence carry "18+" for gay, bisexual and other men who have sex with men and for transgender people.
- Gates. The two reports enter only when every check passes in the 2022 report and in the 2024 report. The title page names people who inject drugs and the survey year. Table 6 compares the survey year with the round two years earlier and prints exactly two rounds. Every sample share lies inside the printed interval. The text after Figure 22 prints the weighted prevalence of the survey year and, in brackets, of the round before, equal to Table 6. The conclusions print the weighted prevalence of the survey year, equal to Table 6. The HIV-positive and HIV-negative respondents printed beside the hepatitis C results add to the sample size of the methods, and the positives over the sample round to the sample share of Table 6 (348 of 4,554 in 2022 and 283 of 3,944 in 2024). The two reports print the same 2022 round in Table 6 (sample share 7.6 per cent, interval 6.9 to 8.4, weighted 7.2 per cent), and the earlier report is the source of every row the two reports share.
- Reconcile. unaids_aidsinfo carries 7.2 per cent for Kazakhstan among people who inject drugs, stamped 2024 (the compile year) with age group "all". The 7.2 equals the 2022 weighted prevalence, the 2024 sample share and the figure the introduction of the 2024 report prints for 2024, so the UNAIDS row cannot be tied to one survey round. The age group "18+" keeps the kz_kncdiz rows and the UNAIDS row in separate comparisons, and the UNAIDS row stays in the panel as published. unodc_pwid holds no Kazakh row.
- Not carried.
  - The sample shares as values, carried in extra.
  - The 2020 sample size, printed as 4,661 in Figure 20 and as 4,761 in other charts of the 2024 report.
  - The sample shares of Figure 20 for 2010 to 2018, printed without a weighted figure.
  - The hepatitis C and syphilis prevalence, for which the panel holds no id, and which the 2022 conclusions print as 69.2 and 8 per cent against 66.1 and 8.3 per cent in Table 6.
  - The site figures, read from charts, and the behaviour and coverage indicators.
- Test. The offline test of 21 October 2026 on the downloaded copies, with the network blocked, gives three rows, 2020 (8.3), 2022 (7.2) and 2024 (6.4), with no validation finding. The tamper test refused the reports on all 13 altered copies.
- In-memory reconcile. Reconcile of the panel of 21 October 2026 with the three kz_kncdiz rows added gives 67,295 national comparisons, 223 source pairs and 47 declared pairs, all unchanged, and no comparison names kz_kncdiz. The validator gives the same 200 messages on the panel with and without the three rows.
- Effect. A run of kz_kncdiz should add three rows. Run alone on the panel of 21 October 2026, the panel should move from 455,790 rows to 455,793 rows, and after the bd_asp, mv_moh, in_naco, ecdc_hiv_report, who_europe_hfa and am_ncid runs, from 455,903 rows to 455,906 rows. The run command is `cd "/Users/newlivehung/Desktop/15. HIV Funding Impacts" && /opt/anaconda3/bin/python3 scripts/run_all.py --sources kz_kncdiz --countries all`.
- Status. Built on 21 October 2026, with the connector, the registration in hivimpact/connectors/country/__init__.py, the kp_hiv_prevalence description of hivimpact/config/indicators.py and the kz_kncdiz entry of scripts/build_site.py. Waiting for Jason's run. Kazakhstan closes after the run, with every reachable source under a BUILT or NOT CARRIED ruling (R41, R46 and R47).

## R48. Solomon Islands, the Demographic and Health Survey reports of 2006-2007 and 2015 (BUILT), and the SINSO HIV fact sheet (NOT CARRIED)
- Decision. BUILT. Connector 67, sb_sinso_dhs (hivimpact/connectors/country/solomon_islands_sinso.py), carries national rows for Solomon Islands with population group "all" and age group "15-49", read from the printed tables of the Solomon Islands Demographic and Health Survey (DHS) reports of the Solomon Islands National Statistics Office (SINSO). hiv_tested_12m_result_survey_pct for 2015 is 1.5 per cent for women (Table 13.9.1) and 0.9 per cent for men (Table 13.9.2). hiv_comprehensive_knowledge_survey_pct for men is 38.6 per cent for 2007 and 41.1 per cent for 2015 (Table 13.3.2 of the 2006-2007 report and Table 13.3.2 of the 2015 report). condom_last_higher_risk_sex_survey_pct for men is 26.0 per cent for 2007 (Table 13.7.2 of the 2006-2007 report). The 2006-2007 survey is stamped 2007, as pacific_data_hub stamps the survey. value_lower and value_upper stay blank, because the sampling-error appendix of the 2015 report holds no HIV variable, and the men's sampling-error table of the 2006-2007 report (Table B.5) prints no knowledge variable and no condom variable for higher-risk sex.
- Sources. "Solomon Islands Demographic and Health Survey 2015" (SINSO and the Ministry of Health and Medical Services, 488 pages) at https://statistics.gov.sb/download/54/dhs_2015/760/final-report_si-demographic-and-health-survey-2015, 12,301,932 bytes, and "Solomon Islands Demographic and Health Survey 2006-2007" (SINSO, the Secretariat of the Pacific Community and Macro International, May 2009, 350 pages) at https://catalog.ihsn.org//catalog/2963/download/44676, the International Household Survey Network (IHSN) catalogue copy, 1,757,342 bytes, downloaded on 21 October 2026. The two files are born-digital and read with pdfplumber and no optical character recognition (R25). The connector downloads the two files once into data/raw/sb_sinso_dhs, checks the size and the SHA-256 prefix of every copy, and reads the cached copies on every later run. The copies of the 2006-2007 report at psro.dataforall.org, www.spc.int and microdata.pacificdata.org were not reachable. psro.dataforall.org serves an expired certificate (curl code 60), and TLS stays on. www.spc.int answered with a Cloudflare block page (403). microdata.pacificdata.org answered 403. The related-materials page of IHSN catalogue entry 2963 lists the file as "Solomon Islands Demographic and Health Survey 2006-2007, Report", a document of type report.
- Why the connector exists. pacific_data_hub carries the Solomon Islands rows the Pacific Community publishes, comprehensive knowledge for women aged 15 to 49, men aged 15 and over and youth aged 15 to 24, condom use at last higher-risk sex for women aged 15 to 49 and men aged 15 and over in 2007, and sex before 15. Every pacific_data_hub value equals the printed tables (2007 women 29.0, men aged 15 and over 35.4, young women 29.3 and young men 35.1, condom use women 18.0 and men aged 15 and over 25.8, and 2015 women 31.0, men aged 15 and over 40.0, young women 28.8 and young men 33.6, sex before 15 for young women 11.0 and young men 11.2). pacific_data_hub carries no testing row and no row for men aged 15 to 49. The DHS Program API holds no Solomon Islands survey, so the dhs connector carries no Solomon Islands row. sb_sinso_dhs writes only the testing rows and the rows for men aged 15 to 49, the age group of every men's row the dhs connector writes, so no sb_sinso_dhs row repeats a pacific_data_hub key.
- Why age group "15-49" for men. The two Solomon Islands surveys interviewed men aged 15 to 54, and the tables print a "Total 15-49" row, a "50+" row and a total for all men. The dhs connector writes every men's row for 15 to 49, the age range the DHS indicators of the DHS Program API share across countries, and records the survey's own age range in extra.survey_age_range. sb_sinso_dhs follows the dhs connector, with "15-54" in extra.survey_age_range for men and "15-49" for women.
- Why the testing measure matches hiv_tested_12m_result_survey_pct. The column of Table 13.9.1 and Table 13.9.2 is the percentage tested in the 12 months before the survey who received the result of the last test, over all women or all men interviewed, and the footnote of the "Never tested" column reads "Includes 'don't know'", so a missing or unknown answer counts as not tested, as DHS indicators HA_CPHT_W_T1R and HA_CPHT_M_T1R count the answer.
- Gates. A table enters only when every check passes. The caption names the table and "Solomon Islands" with the survey year, prints exactly once outside the contents list, and the definition sentence or footnote of the table names the measure. For testing, received the result, did not receive the result and never tested add to 100 within 0.3, ever tested equals 100 less never tested within 0.05, the 12-month share is no larger than the share which received the result, and the urban and rural rows weighted by the printed numbers give the total within 0.1, with numbers adding to the total within two. For men, the "Total 15-49" and "50+" rows weighted by the printed numbers give the row for all men within 0.1, the 2015 testing table and the 2015 knowledge table print the same numbers of men, and the number of all men equals the men the report text names with the survey's own age range (3,591 men aged 15 to 54 in 2015, 2,056 men aged 15 to 54 in 2007). The text must name exactly one age range for the number, and the range must reach 50 or over whenever the tables print men aged 50 and over. For condom use, the share of men with higher-risk sex times the men who had sex gives the men with higher-risk sex within 1.5, the 15-49 and 50+ numbers add to the number of all men within two in every number column of the table (the test covered the condom column alone until R49 extended the test to every column), and the printed value is no suppressed figure.
- Reconcile. No other source in the panel carries a Solomon Islands row on the five keys of sb_sinso_dhs. pacific_data_hub carries the rows for men aged 15 and over on separate keys (knowledge 35.4 in 2007 and 40.0 in 2015, condom use 25.8 in 2007), and the panel never compares an age group "15+" row with an age group "15-49" row.
- Not carried.
  - Every value pacific_data_hub carries on the same key, as duplicates equal to the printed tables.
  - The 2007 testing variable, because the 2006-2007 report prints no testing table, and the sampling-error tables print the variable as 0 with standard error 0 for every domain, which marks an empty variable and not a measured zero.
  - The 2015 condom table, which counts men and women with two or more partners in the last 12 months and not higher-risk sex, a different measure from condom_last_higher_risk_sex_survey_pct.
  - The region rows, because "Other provinces" pools six provinces, and the testing row for men aged 15 and over (0.8 per cent), as the dhs connector writes no 15+ testing row.
  - The SINSO HIV fact sheet (https://statistics.gov.sb/download/53/fact-sheets/757/sol-dhs_7-hiv.pdf, 262,583 bytes), which prints the 2007 figures of the 2006-2007 report, with 38.6 per cent for men equal to the men aged 15 to 49 of Table 13.3.2.
- Test. The offline test of 21 October 2026 on the downloaded copies, with the network blocked, gives five rows, 2007 knowledge for men (38.6), 2007 condom use for men (26.0), 2015 knowledge for men (41.1) and 2015 testing for women (1.5) and men (0.9), with no validation finding. The tamper test refused the reports on all 23 altered copies, and on all 26 after R49 added three copies with a 50+ number altered outside the condom column. The first tamper run accepted a text which named the men as aged 15 to 49, which gave the age-range gate above, and the clean reports give the same five values with the gate in place.
- In-memory reconcile. Reconcile of the panel of 21 October 2026 with the five sb_sinso_dhs rows added gives 67,295 national comparisons, 223 source pairs and 47 declared pairs, all unchanged, and no comparison names sb_sinso_dhs. The validator gives the same 200 messages on the panel with and without the five rows.
- Effect. A run of sb_sinso_dhs should add five rows. Run alone on the panel of 21 October 2026, the panel should move from 455,790 rows to 455,795 rows, and after the bd_asp, mv_moh, in_naco, ecdc_hiv_report, who_europe_hfa, am_ncid and kz_kncdiz runs, from 455,906 rows to 455,911 rows. The run command is `cd "/Users/newlivehung/Desktop/15. HIV Funding Impacts" && /opt/anaconda3/bin/python3 scripts/run_all.py --sources sb_sinso_dhs --countries all`.
- Status. Built on 21 October 2026, with the connector, the registration in hivimpact/connectors/country/__init__.py and the sb_sinso_dhs entry of scripts/build_site.py. Waiting for Jason's run. The Ministry of Health site (solomons.gov.sb) stays blocked. Solomon Islands closes after the run, with every reachable source under a BUILT or NOT CARRIED ruling (pacific_data_hub and R48).

## R49. Kiribati, the Demographic and Health Survey report of 2009 (NOT CARRIED), and the MICS reports of Kiribati, Tuvalu and Vanuatu (NOT REACHED)
- Decision. NOT CARRIED. No row enters from the Kiribati Demographic and Health Survey (KDHS) report of 2009, because the report prints no testing-status figure and the men's HIV tables fail the gates of R48. The condom gate of sb_sinso_dhs now tests every number column of the men's table, a change which keeps all five Solomon Islands rows and refuses the Kiribati table on the same test.
- Source. "Kiribati Demographic and Health Survey 2009" (KIR_2009_DHS_Reports_EN.pdf, 303 pages, 2,051,937 bytes, SHA-256 prefix 0585fa778812) at https://catalog.ihsn.org//catalog/4131/download/55354, the International Household Survey Network (IHSN) catalogue copy, downloaded on 21 October 2026 and read with pdfplumber and no optical character recognition (R25). The DHS Program API holds no Kiribati survey.
- Testing. Table 12.12 (women) and Table 12.13 (men) print in the text layer only the column "Percentage who know where to get an HIV test" and the number column. The two pages hold no image, and the characters of every page stop at x 508 of a portrait page, so the testing-status columns the caption describes are not printed and hiv_tested_12m_result_survey_pct cannot enter.
- Men's knowledge. Table 12.4 prints 51.7 per cent of 943 men aged 15 to 49, 42.0 per cent of 115 men aged 50 and over and 49.5 per cent of 1,135 men aged 15 and over. The numbers add to 1,058, against 1,135 printed. The first column prints 78.1 per cent for all men against 78.9 per cent for men aged 15 to 49 and 80.8 per cent for men aged 50 and over, and no weighted mean of the two age groups can fall below the share for men aged 15 to 49 and the share for men aged 50 and over at once. The men's gate of R48 fails on the numbers and on the weighted mean.
- Men's condom use. Table 12.11 prints 29.1 per cent of 269 men aged 15 to 49 with higher-risk sex, a suppressed value for four men aged 50 and over, and 28.5 per cent of 274 men aged 15 and over. The condom column adds within two (269 plus four against 274), and 34.8 per cent of 772 men who had sex in the last 12 months gives 269. Three of the four other number columns fail the addition, 943 plus 115 against 1,135 for all men, 772 plus 91 against 901 for men who had sex in the last 12 months, and 829 plus 104 against 999 for the men asked about lifetime partners. Table 3.1 of the same report prints 192 men aged 50 and over among the 1,135 men aged 15 and over (15 to 54) interviewed, and 943 plus 192 gives 1,135. The rows for men aged 50 and over in the HIV tables count 115 men where the respondent table counts 192, so the HIV tables print rows over two different sets of men, and the tabulation behind the row for men aged 15 to 49 is unknown.
- Why the gate covers every number column. R48 refuses a table when two printed figures disagree, because the true figure is then unknown. A number for men aged 50 and over which fails the addition in one column of a table marks every row of the table as tabulated over uncertain sets of men, whichever column carries the value. The condom column of Table 12.11 passes only because four men aged 50 and over had higher-risk sex, so a gate on the condom column alone would carry a value from a table which fails the same test in three columns. The sb_sinso_dhs 2007 table adds in all four number columns (1,252 plus 236 gives 1,488, 112 plus one against 114, 400 plus three gives 403, and 1,338 plus 395 gives 1,733), so the wider gate changes no Solomon Islands row.
- The pacific_data_hub rows. pacific_data_hub carries eight Kiribati rows for 2009 (comprehensive knowledge for women aged 15 to 49, men aged 15 and over and youth aged 15 to 24, condom use at last higher-risk sex for women aged 15 to 49 and men aged 15 and over, and sex before 15). The rows for men aged 15 and over equal the printed totals of Table 12.4 (49.5) and Table 12.11 (28.5). The pacific_data_hub rows stay in the panel as the Pacific Community publishes the rows, as R47 keeps the unaids_aidsinfo row as published, and the gates of R48 and R49 apply to connectors reading the printed reports.
- MICS reports, not reached.
  - Kiribati Social Development Indicator Survey 2018-2019, a round of the Multiple Indicator Cluster Surveys (MICS), IHSN catalogue entry 10024. IHSN links the snapshot of key findings on mics-surveys-prod.s3.amazonaws.com, which answered 403 AccessDenied (243 bytes). pacific.unfpa.org answered 403 to the page of the findings report (4,569 bytes). The page of the snapshot at sdd.spc.int (183,056 bytes) sends the file through www.spc.int/digitallibrary, and www.spc.int stays blocked (R48).
  - Tuvalu MICS 2019-2020, IHSN catalogue entry 10056 and World Bank Microdata Library catalogue entry 4288 (related materials page, 60,615 bytes). The two catalogues link the snapshot and the survey findings report on mics-surveys-prod.s3.amazonaws.com, which answered 403 AccessDenied to the snapshot (263 bytes) and to a HEAD request for the findings report. The Pacific Environment Data Portal pages 78326 and 40489 answered 404.
  - Vanuatu MICS 2007, IHSN catalogue entry 1014. IHSN holds the questionnaire (download 40983) and links the final report at www.childinfo.org, which does not resolve (curl code 6).
  - mics-surveys-prod.s3.amazonaws.com, pacific.unfpa.org and www.childinfo.org get no further request. The three MICS reports join the Wayback list held until the fifth ni_mapasalud run. psro.dataforall.org stays blocked (expired certificate, R48).
- IHSN holds no entry for the Tuvalu DHS of 2007 or the Vanuatu DHS of 2013, the two other Pacific DHS rounds pacific_data_hub carries.
- Test. The sb_sinso_dhs tamper test of 21 October 2026, with the network blocked, refused the reports on all 26 altered copies, three of which alter a number for men aged 50 and over outside the condom column, and the clean reports give the same five values.
- Status. Recorded on 21 October 2026. No Kiribati connector and no reconcile declaration. The code change is the every-column gate of sb_sinso_dhs (hivimpact/connectors/country/solomon_islands_sinso.py). Kiribati, Tuvalu and Vanuatu stay open until the Wayback pass checks the three MICS reports, as Maldives stays open for the years after 2016 under the same hold.

## R50. Türkiye, the AIDS counts of the Ministry of Health yearbook and of the TÜİK SDG platform (NOT CARRIED)
- Decision. NOT CARRIED. The panel takes no Turkish aids_cases_reported row from the Health Statistics Yearbook 2024 of the Turkish Ministry of Health or from indicator 3.3.1 of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDG) platform of TÜİK (the Turkish Statistical Institute). Türkiye keeps no notified_hiv_cases row and no aids_cases_reported row, as under R7, R35 and R36.
- Sources.
  - The SDG platform of TÜİK (sdg.tuik.gov.tr), an Open SDG site whose page for indicator 3.3.1 (45,609 bytes) reads the data file /data/tr/3-3-1.json (6,501 bytes, read on 21 October 2026). TÜİK reports the proxy indicator "AIDS vaka sayıları ve 100 000 kişi başına AIDS insidansı" (AIDS case counts and AIDS incidence per 100,000 people) in place of the global indicator on new HIV infections. The file gives the Turkish AIDS count for 2010 to 2023 as a total, a count for foreign nationals ("Yabancı") and a count for Turkish nationals ("Yerli"), and AIDS incidence per 100,000 by sex. The national metadata names the Ministry of Health as the data source and links the Health Statistics Yearbook.
  - The Health Statistics Yearbook 2024 of the Ministry of Health (siy2024en.pdf, 347 pages, 45,108,527 bytes, SHA-256 prefix d5ff2016284a, downloaded on 21 October 2026 from https://dosyamerkez.saglik.gov.tr/Eklenti/52862/0/siy2024en31122025pdf.pdf through the yearbook page of sbsgm.saglik.gov.tr). Table 3.1 on page 75 prints the AIDS count for 2002 and for 2020 to 2024 as local, imported and total cases, Table 3.3 prints the 2024 count by sex, and the General Directorate of Public Health is the named source. The text layer is read with pdfplumber and no optical character recognition (R25).
- The two sources agree. The SDG totals for 2020 to 2023 (75, 105, 128 and 154) and the splits between Turkish and foreign nationals (59 and 16, 90 and 15, 93 and 35, 134 and 20) equal the local and imported counts of Table 3.1. The SDG counts for Turkish and foreign nationals add to the total in all 14 years from 2010 to 2023, and the SDG incidence for men and women together equals the total count over the Turkish population within rounding. The yearbook prints 133 for 2024 (115 local and 18 imported, 27 women and 106 men).
- Why NOT CARRIED.
  - The same surveillance. Annex 4b of the joint ECDC and WHO Europe report for 2024 data (the 2025-Annual-HIV-Report.pdf of R35) names TR-MOH as the Turkish AIDS source for 1985 to 2024, and Annex 5 says the reported Turkish AIDS cases only include people diagnosed with AIDS at the time of HIV diagnosis. The yearbook names the General Directorate of Public Health of the same ministry and the same HIV/AIDS surveillance system. Under R36 a country refused for a definition reason is refused in every source which reads the same national AIDS surveillance, so aids_cases_reported holds one definition across sources.
  - The date basis. The explanations for Chapter 3 of the yearbook (page 94) date every case by the confirmation test in the HIV/AIDS surveillance system, and count the cases notified with a positive confirmation test until 24 June 2025. An AIDS case dated by the HIV confirmation test falls in the year of HIV confirmation, which matches the Annex 5 statement and differs from a count of people newly reported with AIDS in a calendar year.
  - Two printed series disagree. The yearbook and SDG counts exceed the Turkish counts of Table 13 of the joint report in every year from 2015 to 2024, by six (124 against 118 for 2015), nine (108 against 99), seven (128 against 121), 25 (133 against 108), 26 (138 against 112), 29 (75 against 46), 25 (105 against 80), 44 (128 against 84), 74 (154 against 80) and one (133 against 132 for 2024). The yearbook and the SDG platform print no reason for the gap. A national series wider than the Annex 5 statement would fit aids_cases_reported, and a series on the Annex 5 basis would not, and no printed text says which basis the national series holds, so the definition of the Turkish count stays unknown.
- Other parts of the sources. The SDG AIDS incidence per 100,000 has no panel id. The yearbook prints no HIV case count. A sum of the Turkish HIV and AIDS counts stays out of the panel under R7.
- TÜİK data portal. The data portal (veriportali.tuik.gov.tr) is a JavaScript application whose bundle (index-DHVaePSb.js, 31,036 bytes, and main-C0Z3qMtl.js, 1,132,247 bytes) reads the SDMX service at nsiws.tuik.gov.tr. The request for the list of SDMX data sets (https://nsiws.tuik.gov.tr/rest/dataflow/TR/all/latest) answered 401 Unauthorized (48 bytes), an auth refusal, and nsiws.tuik.gov.tr gets no further request.
- Status. Recorded on 21 October 2026. No connector, no reconcile declaration and no code change. Türkiye stays open, because the HIV statistics page of the General Directorate of Public Health (hsgm.saglik.gov.tr) stays unreached after the re-probe pass.

## R51. Uzbekistan, indicator 3.3.1 of the national SDG platform (NOT CARRIED)
- Decision. NOT CARRIED. The panel takes no row from indicator 3.3.1 of the national Sustainable Development Goals (SDG) platform of the National Statistics Committee of Uzbekistan (nsdg.stat.uz).
