- Source. The page for SDG 3 of the platform (https://nsdg.stat.uz/ru/goal/6, 392,486 bytes on disk, read on 21 October 2026) prints a table of national indicators for 2000 to 2025. The row "Индикатор 3.3.1. Число новых заражений ВИЧ на 1 000 неинфицированных в разбивке по полу и возрасту" (new HIV infections per 1,000 uninfected people, by sex and age) gives a rate for all people, women and men for 2011 to 2025, with a dash for 2000 to 2010 and no age row. The rate for all people runs from 0.122 for 2011 to a peak of 0.141 for 2013, falls to 0.082 for 2020 and reads 0.095 for 2025.
- Why NOT CARRIED.
  - No panel id fits. hiv_incidence_survey_per_1000 holds incidence measured by a recency test in a Population-based HIV Impact Assessment survey, and new_infections holds a modelled count. A rate the platform derives from national HIV registration fits no id of the panel, and turning the rate into a count would need a denominator the platform does not print.
  - No method. The platform prints no numerator, no denominator and no note on whether the rate counts registered HIV cases or modelled infections.
  - Mixed rounding. The rate for men is printed to the nearest tenth for 2011 to 2018 (0.1 or 0.2) and to three decimal places from 2019 (0.143), while the rate for women and the rate for all people carry three decimal places in every year, so the men's series for 2011 to 2018 cannot be checked against the rate for all people.
- Status. Recorded on 21 October 2026. No connector and no code change. Uzbekistan stays open, because the Republican AIDS Centre (aids.uz) stays unreached after the re-probe pass.
## R52. Bangladesh, indicator 3.3.1 of the SDG Tracker, notified HIV cases by gender for 2022 and 2024 (BUILT)
- Decision. BUILT. Connector 68, bd_sdg (hivimpact/connectors/country/bangladesh_sdg.py), carries national notified_hiv_cases for Bangladesh with sex "male" and "female" and age group "all" for 2022 (668 men, 271 women) and 2024 (1,110 men, 313 women), from the series of the National AIDS/STD Programme (NASP) under indicator 3.3.1 of the SDG Tracker of the Bangladesh Bureau of Statistics. The count of hijra and transgender people (eight for 2022 and 15 for 2024) travels in extra, because the panel holds no sex value for hijra and transgender people.
- Source. The goal 3 page of the SDG Tracker (https://sdg.gov.bd/indicator-information/7AYriX9, 134,036 bytes, read on 21 October 2026) carries a form token, and the page script posts the token, source id 27 (NASP) and indicator id 32 (3.3.1) to /get-source-wise-data-value. The NASP reply (src_27.json, 17,943 bytes) prints, for 2019, 2022 and 2024, new HIV cases by age (0-14, 15-24, 15-49 and 50 years and older), by gender (Male, Female and Transgender) and a "Total" column holding a rate per 1,000 population (0.006, 0.006 and 0.008). The UNAIDS reply (src_28.json, 25,458 bytes) prints 0.01 per 1,000 for 2016, for all people, men aged 15 to 49 and women aged 15 to 49. The route the search index holds for the old indicator page (/page/indicator-wise/1/32/3/0) answered 404.
- Year. Male, Female and Transgender sum to the World AIDS Day counts of the AIDS/STD Programme (ASP) carried by bd_asp, 919 for 2019, 947 for 2022 and 1,438 for 2024, so the tracker years follow the ASP reporting year from 1 November of the year before to 31 October (R42).
- Check against the ASP files. The gender chart of the 2022 World AIDS Day file (page 10) prints 70.5, 28.6 and 0.8 per cent, and the tracker counts give 70.5, 28.6 and 0.8 per cent of 947. The gender chart of the 2024 file (page 8) prints 77, 22 and 1 per cent, and the tracker counts give 77, 22 and 1 per cent of 1,438.
- Gates. The connector stores the NASP reply once in data/raw/bd_sdg and reads the cached copy on every later run. A year enters only when the table header names the 11 columns read on 21 October 2026 in the same order, when Male, Female and Transgender sum to the ASP count of the year, and when every gender count over the ASP count rounds to the ASP share (2022 to the nearest tenth of a per cent and 2024 to the nearest whole per cent). The offline test on the reply gives four rows with no validation finding, and every tamper case gives zero rows (a changed count, a changed share at the same total, a renamed header column and a missing 2024 row).
- Reconcile. No other source in the panel holds Bangladeshi notified_hiv_cases rows by sex, so the four rows add no comparison.
- Not carried.
  - The 2019 gender counts (638 men, 271 women and 10 hijra and transgender people). The 2019 ASP gender chart covers the 813 cases of the host population alone ("Total HIV positive cases reported= 813"), while the tracker counts sum to 919, the count with the cases among Forcibly Displaced Myanmar Nationals (FDMN), so no ASP figure checks the 2019 counts.
  - The age counts. The 2019 and 2022 ASP files print the age groups 0-5, 6-9, 10-18, 19-24 and 25-49, from which the tracker age groups 0-14 and 15-24 cannot be formed. The tracker count for 0-14 in 2019 (53) equals the ASP host count for 0 to 18 years (26, 10 and 17). The text layer of the 2024 ASP age chart prints the shares apart from the age labels, so no share can be tied to an age group.
  - The "Total" rate, a rate per 1,000 population derived from national case reports, which fits no indicator id of the panel (R51).
  - The UNAIDS series, 0.01 per 1,000 for 2016, a rounded copy of the UNAIDS estimate which unaids_aidsinfo carries.
  - The national metadata file of the tracker ("HIV SDG 2019 2022 2024.pdf", 105,529 bytes), a scanned page with no text layer, which would need optical character recognition (R25).
- Status. Built on 21 October 2026 and waiting for Jason's run. Bangladesh is DONE once Jason runs bd_asp and bd_sdg.

## R53. Kazakhstan, indicators 3.3.1 and 3.3.1.1 of the national SDG platform (NOT CARRIED)
- Decision. NOT CARRIED. The national platform for the Sustainable Development Goals (SDG) of the Bureau of National Statistics of Kazakhstan gives a rate per 1,000 uninfected people under indicator 3.3.1 and a count of new HIV cases among people who inject drugs under the national indicator 3.3.1.1, and the panel takes no row from the 3.3.1 series or from the 3.3.1.1 series.
- Sources. The portal home page (https://sdg.stat.gov.kz/, 161,600 bytes on disk) links the indicator pages of sdgdata.stat.gov.kz, an Open SDG site. The pages for 3.3.1 (https://sdgdata.stat.gov.kz/3-3-1/, 42,879 bytes) and 3.3.1.1 (https://sdgdata.stat.gov.kz/3-3-1-1/, 42,627 bytes) read the CSV files of the data repository at kazstat.github.io/sdg-data-kazstat. The Russian CSV of 3.3.1 (870 bytes) gives 0.12 per 1,000 uninfected people for 2010 to 0.17 for 2025, and the Russian CSV of 3.3.1.1 (410 bytes) gives "Число вновь выявленных ВИЧ из числа потребителей инъекционных наркотиков" (the number of newly detected HIV cases among people who inject drugs) from 1,043 for 2010 to 357 for 2025, with the unit "человек" (people). The metadata JSON files (3,661 bytes on disk for 3.3.1 and 2,849 bytes for 3.3.1.1) name the UNAIDS custodian definition for 3.3.1 and give no national source agency and no method for 3.3.1 or for 3.3.1.1. The PDF metadata link of the 3.3.1 page and of the 3.3.1.1 page answered 404 at the site root and at the page-relative path.
- Not carried.
  - Indicator 3.3.1. A rate per 1,000 uninfected people with no named method fits no indicator id of the panel (R51), and the metadata JSON does not say whether the rate comes from the UNAIDS model or from the national case register.
  - Indicator 3.3.1.1. No panel id holds notified cases by transmission category or by key population (R31), and the metadata JSON does not say whether the count classes a case by the route of transmission or by the drug use of the person, so a row under notified_hiv_cases with population_group pwid would carry a definition no source states.
- Status. Recorded on 21 October 2026. R53 adds no row, and Kazakhstan closes after Jason's run of kz_kncdiz, as R47 states.

## R54. Armenia, indicator 3.3.1 of the national SDG platform (NOT CARRIED)
- Decision. NOT CARRIED. The platform of the Statistical Committee of Armenia for the Sustainable Development Goals (SDG) gives indicator 3.3.1 as a national rate per 1,000 uninfected people, and the panel takes no row from the series.
- Source. The indicator page (https://sdg.armstat.am/3-3-1/, 86,272 bytes on disk) reads the CSV file of the data repository at armstat.github.io/sdg-data-armenia. The English CSV (457 bytes) gives 0.06 per 1,000 uninfected people for 2015 to 0.18 for 2024, with no disaggregation and no count. The metadata JSON (1,369 bytes) names the Ministry of Health of Armenia as the source organisation and the UNAIDS custodian definition, and gives no method.
- Not carried. A rate per 1,000 uninfected people fits no indicator id of the panel (R51), and turning the rate into a count would need a denominator the platform does not print. The national HIV counts of Armenia enter through am_ncid (R45).
- Status. Recorded on 21 October 2026. R54 adds no row, and Armenia closes after Jason's run of ecdc_hiv_report, who_europe_hfa and am_ncid, as the Armenian section of research/phase8_eeca/eeca_round1_notes.md states.

## R55. Tajikistan, people newly registered with HIV by region, Ministry of Health yearbooks (BUILT, tj_moh)
- Decision. BUILT as tj_moh (connector 70 since ruling R59, first recorded as connector 68, hivimpact/connectors/country/tajikistan_moh.py). The connector writes notified_hiv_cases for the five first-level units of Tajikistan (Dushanbe, the Districts of Republican Subordination, Sughd Oblast, Khatlon Oblast and GBAO) for 2014, 2015, 2016 and 2021, 20 rows, and writes no national row.
- Sources. The medical statistics page of the Ministry of Health and Social Protection of the Population (https://moh.tj/omori-tibbi/, 124,423 bytes on disk) links the yearbooks "Солимии аҳолӣ ва фаъолияти муассисаҳои тандурустӣ" for 2014 (30,189,532 bytes), 2015 (6,527,393 bytes), 2016 (9,598,396 bytes) and 2021 (8,102,859 bytes), all under https://moh.tj/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/. The HIV table prints the people infected with HIV for the yearbook's year and for the year before, by region and district, with the Republic total.
- Gates. The five regions add to the Republic total in the earlier column and in the own column, the districts add to the Districts of Republican Subordination, and the earlier column of the next yearbook equals the own column of the yearbook in every region. Every gate passes on the copies read on 21 October 2026.
- National check. The Republic total equals ecdc_hiv_report for 2021 (922), sits two cases above ecdc_hiv_report for 2016 (1,040 against 1,038), sits three cases below for 2015 (1,146 against 1,149) and sits 22 cases above who_europe_hfa for 2014 (1,008 against 986). The Statistical Agency compendium "Statistika 2018-2019" prints the same totals for 2014, 2015 and 2016.
- Not carried.
  - The earlier column of every yearbook. The 2021 yearbook prints 1,397 people for 2020 against 1,083 in ecdc_hiv_report, and the gap of 314 matches the excess of the Dushanbe row (460 for 2020 against 141 for 2021) and of the Varzob district row (64 for 2020 against six for 2021). No 2020 yearbook appears on the ministry page, and 2020 therefore enters with no regional row.
  - The district rows, a second administrative level (the Sri Lankan district rule of ruling R14).
  - The rates per 100,000 people, the AIDS columns (86 for 2020 and 76 for 2021 against 110 and 130 in ecdc_hiv_report), the treatment columns and the deaths (the Fiji deaths ruling).
- Open. The compendium "Statistika 2018-2019" of the Statistical Agency under the President of Tajikistan (https://moh.tj/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/Statistika-2018-2019-Agenstvo.pdf, 2,217,367 bytes on disk, corrected under ruling R59) prints national counts by sex and age group for 2014 to 2018 (1,008, 1,146, 1,040, 1,207 and 1,422 people), a sex split no panel source carries for Tajikistan. The yearbooks for 2013 and the Russian edition "Здравоохранение в Республике Таджикистан" are not read. Tajikistan stays open until the next session rules on the three files.
- Reconcile. No other source carries notified_hiv_cases for Tajik regions. pepfar_snu carries new_diagnoses for the PEPFAR-supported sites of the same regions, and Jason's ruling of 8 October 2026 forbids comparing notified_hiv_cases with new_diagnoses.
- Status. Recorded on 21 October 2026. tj_moh downloads the four yearbooks on Jason's run (54,418,140 bytes in all).

## R56. Closure of Moldova, Uzbekistan, Albania, Turkey and the Federated States of Micronesia (NOT CARRIED, R47)
- Decision. The five countries close. Every reachable source holds a BUILT or NOT CARRIED ruling, and every remaining host failed in the re-probe pass, which under R47 keeps no country open.
- Moldova. The National Bureau of Statistics tables exclude the left bank (R32). ansp.gov.md failed in the re-probe pass, msmps.gov.md timed out (curl code 28), data.gov.md and date.gov.md/hiv answered 404, sdg.statistica.md failed, and the national open data catalogue at dataset.gov.md returns only the NBS gender dataset for the searches HIV and SIDA (4,988 bytes every reply). ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa carry the national counts.
- Uzbekistan. aids.uz failed in the re-probe pass, and data.egov.uz refused the connection (curl code 7). The Ministry of Health page of the government portal (https://gov.uz/oz/ssv, 191,778 bytes) names the Republican AIDS Centre and prints no figure. The national SDG series is a rate per 1,000 uninfected people (R51).
- Albania. The INSTAT public health indicators of 2024 (https://www.instat.gov.al/media/ysjp0id1/treguesit-e-shendetit-publik-2024.pdf, 226,511 bytes, three pages) print no HIV line. The causes-of-death series falls under the Fiji deaths ruling. databaza.instat.gov.al failed, the ISHP host answered with a WAF page, and sdg.instat.gov.al does not resolve. ecdc_hiv_report carries the national counts.
- Turkey. The SDG and yearbook series are NOT CARRIED (R50). nsiws.tuik.gov.tr answered 401, an auth refusal with no workaround, and hsgm.saglik.gov.tr and sgb.saglik.gov.tr failed in the re-probe pass.
- Federated States of Micronesia. hhs.gov.fm and health.gov.fm do not resolve, www.fsmstats.fm refused the connection, www.fsmhealth.fm presents a certificate for another name, fsmhealth.fm timed out, and the statistics office at stats.gov.fm publishes no HIV table (searches for HIV and for health return empty lists).
- Status. Recorded on 21 October 2026. R56 adds no row.

## R57. Pakistan, the IDSR weekly bulletins of the National Institute of Health (NOT CARRIED), and the closure of Pakistan (R47)
- Source. The National Institute of Health (NIH) of Pakistan publishes a weekly bulletin of Integrated Disease Surveillance and Response (IDSR) at https://www.nih.org.pk/phb/weekly-bulletin (80,472 bytes on 21 October 2026). The bulletin for epidemiological week 39 of 2026 (https://www.nih.org.pk/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Weekly%20Report-39-2026.pdf, 2,720,500 bytes, 27 pages) was read with pdfplumber.
- Weekly suspected cases. Table 1 of the week 39 bulletin prints suspected cases by province for the week, with 24 suspected HIV/AIDS cases (six in Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, 18 in Sindh, and no case in Azad Jammu and Kashmir, Balochistan, Gilgit-Baltistan and Islamabad) and Punjab marked NR, not reported. The bulletin text calls every count a suspected case needing field verification.
- Weekly laboratory counts. Table 5 prints the tests and the positive results of the public health laboratories for the week, with 6,022 HIV tests and 48 positive results in Sindh, 396 tests and two positive results in Balochistan, 216 tests in Gilgit-Baltistan and 30 tests in Azad Jammu and Kashmir with no positive result, and no figure for Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, Islamabad and Punjab.
- Decision. NOT CARRIED. A suspected case is not a notified HIV diagnosis, and notified_hiv_cases holds notified diagnoses alone. A yearly figure would be a sum of 52 weekly counts with Punjab missing in some weeks, a figure no agency published. The laboratory table covers the public health laboratories of four areas out of seven, and a weekly laboratory test count is not a yearly count of HIV tests performed in a province.
- Pakistan Bureau of Statistics. www.pbs.gov.pk and pbs.gov.pk timed out after 30 seconds on two probes on 21 October 2026 (curl code 28), a REACH FAILURE.
- Provincial programmes. No host resolves for pacp.gov.pk, pacp.punjab.gov.pk, sacp.gov.pk, sacp.sindh.gov.pk, www.sacp.gov.pk and kpacp.gov.pk. Two web searches for the Punjab and Sindh AIDS Control Programmes found news reports alone, and the panel never takes a news report as a source.
- Closure. Pakistan closes. The NACP registered counts are cumulative counts, REFUSED under R14. The Wayback captures of nacp.gov.pk print the same cumulative counts, and the Wayback hold therefore keeps Pakistan open no longer. The NACP on-ART count is a DUPLICATE of the global_fund on_art_count row for Pakistan for 2025, the NACP trend charts are PNG images under the project rule under which no figure read by optical character recognition (OCR) enters the panel, the WHO EMRO regional update of 2012 prints no yearly Pakistani count, and www.nacp.gov.pk and aidsdatahub.org failed in the re-probe pass, which under R47 keeps no country open.
- Status. Recorded on 21 October 2026. R57 adds no row.

## R58. North Korea, the DPRK MICS 2009 report (BUILT as prk_cbs_mics), the DPRK MICS 2017 report (NOT CARRIED), and the closure of North Korea
- Source. "Democratic People's Republic of Korea Multiple Indicator Cluster Survey 2009, Final Report" (Central Bureau of Statistics of the DPRK and the United Nations Children's Fund, December 2010, 150 pages) is at https://dprk.un.org/sites/default/files/2019-07/03.10.b_MICS_DPRK_2009.pdf (2,030,354 bytes on 21 October 2026, sha256 prefix e07d4d66ca42), read with pdfplumber.
- Tables. Table HA.1 prints comprehensive knowledge about HIV for women aged 15 to 49 (8.8 per cent of 8,249 women), and Table HA.2 prints the share for women aged 15 to 24 (7.9 per cent of 2,344 women), for the 10 provinces and the total. The report names transmission by mosquito bites and by sharing food as the two most common local misconceptions. Tables SE.2 and SE.5 to SE.14 print the confidence limits of the Table HA.2 share for the total and for every province.
- Decision. BUILT as connector 69, prk_cbs_mics, with 22 rows on hiv_comprehensive_knowledge_survey_pct for women in 2009, 11 rows for age_group 15-49 (national and 10 provinces, no interval) and 11 rows for age_group 15-24 (national and 10 provinces, with the printed confidence limits). The connector refuses the report unless the provinces add to the totals, the 15-24 row of Table HA.1 equals the total of Table HA.2, and every sampling-error row prints the share and the number of women of Table HA.2. An offline test on a scratch copy wrote 22 rows, writers.validate returned no problem, and four tampered copies were refused.
- Not carried. Table HA.3 (knowledge of mother-to-child transmission) and Table HA.4 (accepting attitudes), because the panel holds no id for knowledge of mother-to-child transmission or for accepting attitudes, and the urban, rural, age and education rows. The DPRK MICS 2017 report (https://www.unicef.org/dprk/media/156/file/MICS.pdf, 4,113,875 bytes, 316 pages) is NOT CARRIED, because the report holds no HIV module and names HIV and AIDS only in the abbreviations list.
- Closure. North Korea closes once Jason runs prk_cbs_mics, because every other reachable source for North Korea holds a BUILT or NOT CARRIED ruling.
- Status. Recorded on 21 October 2026. R58 adds 22 rows after Jason's run.

## R59. Tajikistan, the 2017 HIV chapter of the Ministry of Health (BUILT into tj_moh), the sex and age table of the Agency on Statistics compendium (BUILT as tj_stat), the other files of the ministry page (NOT CARRIED), and the closure of Tajikistan
- Sources. The medical statistics page of the Ministry of Health and Social Protection of the Population (https://moh.tj/omori-tibbi/) links four files beyond the yearbooks of ruling R55, all read on 21 and 22 October 2026 with no sign-in.
- The HIV chapter of the 2017 yearbook, "11. ВНМО", a Word 97 file (https://moh.tj/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/11.-VNMO.doc, 255,488 bytes, sha256 prefix f649a6d50fcb). The chapter prints Table 63 (the five regions and the Republic total for 2016 and 2017) and the district tables 63.1 to 63.3.
- The compendium "Здравоохранение в Республике Таджикистан / Тандурустӣ дар Ҷумҳурии Тоҷикистон" of the Agency on Statistics under the President of the Republic of Tajikistan, 2019, 85 pages (https://moh.tj/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/Statistika-2018-2019-Agenstvo.pdf, 2,217,367 bytes, sha256 prefix c8700f2d8068). Ruling R55 calls the publisher the Statistical Agency and records the size as 2,076,672 bytes, and the copy on disk holds 2,217,367 bytes.
- The link labelled as the 2013 yearbook (https://moh.tj/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/Indikatorhoi-millii-tandurusti-2013.pdf, 4,784,808 bytes) and the link labelled as the Russian edition of the yearbook (https://moh.tj/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/Zdravoohranenie-v-Respublike-Tadzhikistan-TANDURUST-DAR-UM-URII-TO-IKISTON.pdf, 2,217,367 bytes).
- Decision on the 2017 chapter. BUILT into tj_moh as the yearbook of 2017, five rows of notified_hiv_cases for the five regions (Districts of Republican Subordination 288, Dushanbe 219, GBAO 47, Khatlon Oblast 344 and Sughd Oblast 309, adding to the Republic total of 1,207). The connector reads the Word file with the standard-library reader hivimpact/connectors/country/_msdoc.py, because macOS textutil prints the table with no cell boundary and glues "200" and "219" into "200219". The gates of ruling R55 run unchanged, with a printed single hyphen read as zero (the Lakhsh row for 2017). The 2016 column of the chapter equals the own column of the 2016 yearbook in every region, and the 2017 Republic total sits two cases above ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa (1,205). tj_moh now writes 25 rows for 2014, 2015, 2016, 2017 and 2021.
- Decision on the compendium. BUILT as tj_stat (connector 71, hivimpact/connectors/country/tajikistan_stat.py), 115 rows of national notified_hiv_cases for 2014 to 2018 from page 32 ("Количество ВИЧ - инфицированных по полу и возрасту", registered with a diagnosis made for the first time). The rows cover men and women of all ages (10 rows), all people by age group (35 rows), and men and women by age group (70 rows), with the age groups 0-14, 15-19, 20-29, 30-39, 40-49, 50-59 and 60+. No row for all people of all ages is written, because ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa carry the national count, and the printed total travels in extra.national_total_printed. The connector refuses the compendium unless the page prints the caption, the registration line and the year header, every block heading and every row prints exactly once, the age groups add to the printed total of every sex in every year, and men plus women equal all people in every row and every year.
- National check. The printed totals for 2014 to 2017 (1,008, 1,146, 1,040 and 1,207) equal the Republic totals of tj_moh. The 2015 total sits three cases below ecdc_hiv_report and who_europe_hfa (1,149), the 2016 and 2017 totals sit two cases above (1,038 and 1,205), the 2018 total sits a single case above (1,421), and the 2014 total sits 22 cases above who_europe_hfa (986). No other panel source carries a Tajik split by sex or age.
- Not carried.
- Page 33 of the compendium, pregnant women with HIV infection by region (99, 89, 75, 60 and 77 for 2014 to 2018). The panel holds no routine indicator for registered pregnant women with HIV, the modelled pregnant_women_hiv follows a different definition, and the page does not say whether the count covers new diagnoses alone or every pregnant woman followed.
- Page 33 of the compendium, AIDS cases of 2018 by sex and region, under ruling R55, because the Republic count (110 men and 52 women, 162 people) differs from ecdc_hiv_report (212) and from who_europe_hfa (207).
- The AIDS columns of the 2016 and 2017 chapter (200 for 2016 and 180 for 2017, against 237 and 264 in ecdc_hiv_report), under ruling R55.
- The file labelled as the 2013 yearbook is byte-identical to the national health indicators catalogue "Национальные индикаторы здравоохранения в Республике Таджикистан" already on the page (sha256 prefix d990622e74e0, 123 pages). The catalogue defines every national indicator with the formula and the data source and prints no figure, and the catalogue is NOT CARRIED. The file labelled as the Russian edition is byte-identical to the compendium. The links labelled as the 2013 yearbook and as the Russian edition therefore add no new file.
- Numbering. Ruling R55 recorded tj_moh as connector 68, the number ruling R52 gave to bd_sdg. bd_sdg keeps 68, prk_cbs_mics keeps 69 under ruling R58, tj_moh becomes connector 70, and tj_stat takes 71.
- Tests. Offline runs on scratch copies with the network blocked wrote 25 tj_moh rows and 115 tj_stat rows, and writers.validate returned no problem. Five tampered copies of the 2017 chapter and nine tampered copies of page 32 were refused. In memory, the 115 tj_stat rows add no comparison to reconcile and leave the 228 pairs unchanged.
- Closure. Tajikistan closes once Jason runs tj_moh and tj_stat, because every reachable source for Tajikistan now holds a BUILT or NOT CARRIED ruling (rulings R55 and R59, with aids.tj and sdg.stat.tj under ruling R47).
- Status. Recorded on 22 October 2026. R59 adds five tj_moh rows and 115 tj_stat rows after Jason's run, and tj_stat downloads the compendium once (2,217,367 bytes). Jason ran tj_moh and tj_stat on 22 October 2026, the run wrote 25 tj_moh rows and 115 tj_stat rows, the panel holds 456,077 rows, and Tajikistan is closed.

## R60. The Wayback pass after the fifth ni_mapasalud run, the addresses the Wayback pass may read, and the closure of Kiribati
- Hold. Jason reported the fifth ni_mapasalud run on 22 October 2026. The run ended at 05:15:44 UTC on HTTP 429 (Too Many Requests) from the Wayback Machine at the Madriz capture and wrote no row. A single request of the Wayback pass to the capture index (CDX) server of the Wayback Machine, sent at about 08:50 UTC on 22 October 2026 for the Vanuatu MICS 2007 files on childinfo.org, answered 429 (162 bytes). The Wayback requests of the project share a single address with the ni_mapasalud runs, and a further request could lengthen the block. No Wayback request (CDX, HEAD or GET) goes out before 08:50 UTC on 23 October 2026. The next attempt opens with a single CDX request, every later request waits at least 10 seconds after the previous request, and the pass stops at the first 429.
- Order. The Wayback pass for South Asia, East Asia and the Pacific runs before the sixth ni_mapasalud run, because the three regions carry the end-of-October deadline Jason gave Adrian Smith and Nicaragua sits outside the three regions.
- Scope of the Wayback pass. The Wayback pass reads an archived copy only where the live address failed by a host name which no longer resolves, a timeout, an empty reply or a removal (HTTP 404 or a rebuilt site). The Wayback pass never reads an archived copy of an address whose live host answered 401 or 403, sent a firewall or CAPTCHA page, or sits on the blocked list, because an archived copy of a refused file is a workaround of the refusal (Jason's rule on 401, 403 and firewall answers outranks the Wayback list of ruling R49).
- Addresses the Wayback pass may read. The factsheets of 2010 to 2021 of the National Centre for AIDS and STD Control (NCASC) of Nepal under the file paths of www.ncasc.gov.np before the rebuild of May 2025 (Nepal). The Afghan AIDS data book of 2020 on aidsdatahub.org, which timed out (Afghanistan). The Vanuatu MICS 2007 final report on www.childinfo.org, which no longer resolves (Vanuatu). The Pacific Environment Data Portal pages 78326 and 40489, which answered 404 (Tuvalu).
- Addresses the Wayback pass may not read. The snapshots and findings reports on mics-surveys-prod.s3.amazonaws.com (403 AccessDenied), the findings report page on pacific.unfpa.org (403) and every file of www.spc.int/digitallibrary (blocked).
- Kiribati. The Kiribati Social Development Indicator Survey 2018-2019 sits only on mics-surveys-prod.s3.amazonaws.com, pacific.unfpa.org and www.spc.int, and the survey report is therefore NOT REACHED with no Wayback read. Every other reachable source for Kiribati holds a BUILT or NOT CARRIED ruling (rulings R48 and R49, with health.gov.ki and www.health.gov.ki under ruling R47), and Kiribati closes.
- Tuvalu. The Tuvalu MICS 2019-2020 copies on mics-surveys-prod.s3.amazonaws.com get no Wayback read. Tuvalu stays open for the Wayback copies of the Pacific Environment Data Portal pages 78326 and 40489.
- Status. Recorded on 22 October 2026. R60 adds no row. Nepal, Afghanistan, Vanuatu and Tuvalu stay open for the Wayback pass, and Fiji stays open for the public workbook of the Ministry of Health and Medical Services.

## R61. Live copies of the Wayback targets, and the Vanuatu Demographic and Health Survey of 2013
- Search. On 22 October 2026 web searches looked for live copies of the Wayback targets of ruling R60 on hosts the project has not refused, blocked or marked as a reach failure.
- Third-party copies. A copy of a survey report or factsheet on a third-party repository is read when the repository host is not refused and not blocked, as connector 67 reads the Solomon Islands DHS 2006-2007 report from the IHSN catalogue. A copy on a host of the Pacific Community (SPC) or of the pacificdata.org catalogue gets no request (rulings R49 and R60).
- Vanuatu MICS 2007. Copies of the final report sit on aidsdatahub.org (a re-probe failure under ruling R47), on new.aidsdatahub.org, which does not resolve (curl code 6, a single request), on the Pacific Environment Data Portal resource page 67801 (HTTP 404, 24,949 bytes) and on the publication page of the Reproductive Health Supplies Coalition at rhsupplies.org (HTTP 404, 223,730 bytes). Copies on microdata.pacificdata.org and uatweb.pacificdata.org get no request, because uatweb.pacificdata.org serves the blocked pacificdata.org catalogue.
- Vanuatu DHS 2013. The Vanuatu Demographic and Health Survey 2013 (Ministry of Health, Vanuatu National Statistics Office and SPC, November 2014, 395 pages) prints HIV testing and knowledge tables, and pacific_data_hub carries eight Vanuatu rows for 2013 (comprehensive knowledge, condom use at last higher-risk sex and sex before 15). The copies of the Vanuatu Bureau of Statistics are removed. The fact sheets at https://vnso.gov.vu/sites/default/files/VDHS_Fact_sheets_combine_FINAL.pdf answered 404 (a single byte), and the survey report page answered 404 (5,342 bytes). The eVols repository of the University of Hawaii at Manoa (evols.library.manoa.hawaii.edu, item 41596f3b-db0c-4e46-a4b8-c17171bddf72) timed out on a request of the item record (60 seconds) and on a HEAD request of the home page (30 seconds), a REACH FAILURE. The copy on www.spc.int gets no request (blocked), and IHSN holds no entry (ruling R49).
- Afghanistan. The Data Hub prints Afghan data books for 2022 and 2023 next to the data book of 2020, on new.aidsdatahub.org (does not resolve) and www.aidsdatahub.org (the timed-out host of ruling R47).
- Nepal. The Data Hub holds the NCASC factsheets of 2014, 2015, 2016 and 2020 and the factsheet of the national HIV estimates of 2019, on the same hosts.
- Tuvalu DHS 2007. Copies sit on psro.dataforall.org, microdata.pacificdata.org and uatweb.pacificdata.org, which get no request.
- Wayback list. The list of ruling R60 gains the Vanuatu DHS 2013 report and fact sheets under vnso.gov.vu (404), the eVols item of the Vanuatu DHS 2013 report (timeout) and the SPREP page 67801 (404) for Vanuatu, the Afghan data books of 2022 and 2023 under aidsdatahub.org (timeout) for Afghanistan, and the NCASC factsheets under aidsdatahub.org (timeout) for Nepal. A Wayback copy of the Vanuatu DHS 2013 report enters only for the keys pacific_data_hub does not carry, under the gates of connector 67, and a data book enters only for figures with no UNAIDS model estimate behind the figures.
- Status. Recorded on 22 October 2026. R61 adds no row. Nepal, Afghanistan, Vanuatu and Tuvalu stay open for the Wayback pass.

## R62. The Wayback addresses for the Nepalese factsheets of 2022 to 2025 and for the annual report of the Department of Health Services
- Gap. Ruling R60 allows a Wayback read of the NCASC (National Centre for AIDS and STD Control) factsheets of 2010 to 2021. The NCASC factsheets of 2022 to 2025 and the annual report of the Department of Health Services (DoHS) sit at addresses which failed in the ways ruling R60 covers, a removal or an empty reply.
- NCASC files of 2022 and 2023. tabData/63872fdabc735.pdf (a CDX length of 438,923) and tabData/65681abbee32d.pdf (a CDX length of 1,114,675) sat under the www.ncasc.gov.np paths which the rebuild of May 2025 removed. The file names encode upload times of 30 November 2022 and 30 November 2023, the day before World AIDS Day in 2022 and in 2023.
- NCASC files of 2024 and 2025. The 2024 factsheet (dd989-wad2024-factsheets_bvmcoeq.pdf) and the 2025 factsheet (WAD_2025_Factsheet_Final-PressCopy_hejxv4v.pdf) sit under https://giwmscdnone.gov.np/media/pdf_upload/, where every live GET ended in an empty reply (curl error 52).
- DoHS annual report 2081/82. The file eBook%20Digital%20-%2007%20June%202026_dzebldm.pdf (20,535,167 bytes by HEAD) sits under the same giwmscdnone.gov.np path and got no live GET after the empty replies.
- Decision. R62 adds the five addresses to the Wayback list, and the Wayback pass under ruling R60 reads the five addresses with the same spacing and the same stop at the first 429. A reported-case series from a single publisher for 2010 to 2025 keeps a single definition across the years, and accuracy and consistency come first.
- Entry conditions. A tabData file enters only when the first page names the file as the NCASC factsheet of the year. Every Wayback copy enters under the gates of the factsheet connector, with no optical character recognition, and a figure printed only as an image enters no row. The DoHS annual report enters only for counts with no other source in the panel.
- Limits. No request to the prixacdn mirror of giwmscdnone.gov.np, and no live request to giwmscdnone.gov.np in the first round of the search.
- Status. Recorded on 22 October 2026. R62 adds no row.

## R63. The HIV country profiles of 2016 of the WHO Regional Office for the Eastern Mediterranean for Afghanistan and Pakistan (NOT CARRIED)
- Source. The page "HIV and hepatitis country profiles, 2016" of the WHO Regional Office for the Eastern Mediterranean (EMRO) (https://www.emro.who.int/asd/country-activities/hiv-and-hepatitis-country-profiles-2016.html, 64,459 bytes on disk) links an HTML profile for every country of the region. The Afghan profile (/asd/country-activities/afghanistan-hiv-profile.html, 108,501 bytes on disk) and the Pakistani profile (/asd/country-activities/pakistan-hiv-country-profile-2016.html, 109,277 bytes on disk) were read on 22 October 2026. The profiles name the EMRO regional surveillance data, the estimates of 2016 from Spectrum (the UNAIDS estimation software) and Global AIDS Monitoring 2016 as sources.
- Estimates. NOT CARRIED. The Pakistani profile prints 130,000 people with HIV, 19,000 new infections and 5,500 AIDS deaths for 2016, against 160,000, 21,000 and 7,500 in unaids_aidsinfo for 2016. The Afghan profile prints 7,500 people with HIV, 204 new infections and 26 AIDS deaths. The profile figures come from an older round of estimates, and mixing estimate rounds would put two definitions behind plhiv, new_infections and aids_deaths, as for the EMRO profiles of 2020.
- Key populations. NOT CARRIED. The Afghan profile prints the survey prevalence of 2012 (0.2 per cent among sex workers, 0.4 per cent among men who have sex with men and 4.4 per cent among people who inject drugs) and size estimates with no year. The panel carries Afghan and Pakistani kp_hiv_prevalence and kp_size_estimate from unaids_aidsinfo alone, and the unaids_aidsinfo row for Afghan people who inject drugs already reads 4.4 per cent. A second series of the same survey values under a different year would break the consistency of the key population rows.
- Routine testing, Afghanistan 2016. NOT CARRIED. The profile prints 3,028 tuberculosis (TB) patients tested for HIV and 12 positive, against 16,221 tested and eight positive in who_tme for 2016. Two printed figures which disagree leave the true figure unknown. The key population testing counts come from a different collection from the global_fund kp_tested series, which starts in 2017, and the count of clients of testing services is not the hiv_tests_performed definition.
- Reported counts. NOT CARRIED. The Afghan profile prints 2,131 reported people with HIV, a cumulative count with no panel indicator. The Pakistani profile prints 20,639 reported people with HIV, 5,156 under the label "New infections" in the column "Reported" and 320 reported AIDS deaths for 2016. The profile does not define the reported count of 5,156, and a single year from an undefined count would sit alone in the Pakistani series.
- Status. Recorded on 22 October 2026. R63 adds no row. Pakistan stays closed under ruling R57, and Afghanistan stays open for the Wayback list alone.

## R64. The evaluation report of 2020 on the Multi-Country Western Pacific HIV programme, for Vanuatu and Tuvalu (NOT CARRIED)
- Source. "Focused Country Evaluations, Multi-Country Western Pacific HIV Evaluation, Regional Program", March 2020, 29 pages, published by the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP) (https://files.acquia.undp.org/public/migration/pacific/MWP-HIV-Evaluation-Report-Mar2020.pdf, 1,114,064 bytes on disk, read on 22 October 2026 with pdfplumber). The Multi-Country Western Pacific (MWP) programme is a Global Fund grant for 11 small Pacific countries, Tuvalu and Vanuatu among the 11.
- Figures. A footnote on page 11 gives the "Country breakdown of known PLHIV on treatment", where PLHIV stands for people living with HIV, from the Global Fund progress update of 2018 (PUDR, progress update and disbursement request), four of six for Vanuatu and zero of zero for Tuvalu. The panel holds no Vanuatu or Tuvalu on_art_count row for 2018.
- Decision. NOT CARRIED. The body text of page 11 says the denominator is the Global Fund performance framework estimate of people with HIV, while the footnote calls the denominator known people with HIV, and the footnote does not state the reporting period of the PUDR. The PUDR is not public, and the footnote is a secondary citation of a grant performance count, not the national programme record.
- Status. Recorded on 22 October 2026. R64 adds no row. Vanuatu and Tuvalu stay open for the Wayback list.

## R65. The AIDS Data Hub copies for Vanuatu and Tuvalu on the Wayback list
- Gap. Ruling R61 found a copy of the final report of the Vanuatu Multiple Indicator Cluster Survey (MICS) of 2007 on aidsdatahub.org, a re-probe failure under ruling R47, and the Wayback list of ruling R61 holds no aidsdatahub.org address for Vanuatu or Tuvalu. The report of the second generation surveillance (SGS) surveys of 2004 and 2005 on HIV, other sexually transmitted infections and risk behaviours in Pacific countries sits on aidsdatahub.org as well, and the country list of the SGS report is read from the Wayback capture before any Vanuatu or Tuvalu figure is taken.
- Decision. The Wayback list gains two capture index searches on aidsdatahub.org, for PDF captures with "Vanuatu" in the address and for PDF captures with "Tuvalu" in the address, and the PDF capture of the SGS report of 2004 and 2005.
- Entry conditions. A MICS figure enters only for the keys pacific_data_hub does not carry, under the gates of connector 67. An SGS prevalence figure enters only when the report states the sampled population, the survey year and the test algorithm, and only for a population group the panel already defines. No figure printed only as an image enters, and no figure read by OCR enters.
- Status. Recorded on 22 October 2026. R65 adds no row. Vanuatu and Tuvalu stay open for the Wayback pass.

## R66. Fiji, the public HIV surveillance workbook and the summary report of 2025 of the Ministry of Health and Medical Services (BUILT as fj_mohms), the other Fijian files (NOT CARRIED or NOT REACHED), and the closure of Fiji
- Source. The page https://www.health.gov.fj/fiji-hiv-surveillance-report-2025/ of the Ministry of Health and Medical Services (MOHMS) links the public workbook "Fiji HIV Surveillance Data 2025" (Fiji_HIV_Surveillance_Data_2025_Public-1.xlsx, 118,422 bytes, sha256 prefix 9a7a07eceee9), the summary report of the "2025 National HIV Statistics" (2025_HIV_Summary_Report_Final_02042026.pdf, 462,412 bytes, 92068e52f92c) and the full surveillance report (Fiji_HIV_Surveillance_Report_2025_FINAL.pdf, 6,427,134 bytes, d95674756cd2). The three files were last modified on 1 May 2026 and read on 14 October 2026. The workbook source line reads "National HIV Database, Ministry of Health and Medical Services, Fiji".
- Decision. BUILT as connector 72, fj_mohms (hivimpact/connectors/country/fiji_mohms.py), source type routine_programme_record. During Jason's run the connector downloads the workbook and the summary report once into data/raw/fj_mohms, and every later run reads the cached copies, with openpyxl for the workbook and the pdfplumber text layer for the summary report. The connector writes notified_hiv_cases nationally for every year of the Cases sheet from 1989 to 2025, nationally for men, women, transgender people (population group transgender), the age group 0-14 and the age group 15+ from the Divisions sheet for 2018 to 2025, for the three divisions (admin1 Central/Eastern, Western and Northern) for all people, men, women, transgender people, 0-14 and 15+ for 2018 to 2025, and nationally by the age groups 0-4 to 45-49 in five-year steps and 50+ from the Age sheet for 2022, 2024 and 2025. The connector also writes know_status_count from the PLHIV row of the Treatment Cascade sheet for 2020 to 2025 (people living with HIV reported in Fiji by the end of the year) and viral_suppression_count from the "Viral suppression" row for 2022 to 2025.
- Gates. The Cases sheet runs without a gap from 1989, and the cumulative column equals the running sum of the new cases. The nine figures of every Divisions row add to the printed row total, and the three divisions add to the Cases count in every year from 2018 to 2025. A blank entry counts as zero only where the printed figures already add to the row total, which happens once, at Western transgender children in 2024, and the blank travels in extra.blank_cells. The Gender sheet total equals the Cases count from 2019, and the Ethnicity total equals the Cases count from 2022 to 2025, and a disagreement on a total drops the year. A year where the Gender sheet and the Divisions sheet give a different count of men, women or transgender people gets no row for the sex concerned, nationally and in every division, and keeps the totals and the age rows. An age group year enters only when the age groups add to the Cases count and the three age groups under 15 years add to the paediatric rows of the Divisions sheet. Table 1 of the summary report (2025 by division for adults and children) equals the workbook, or the 2025 case rows drop, and a 2025 count of men, women or transgender people in the summary report which disagrees with the workbook drops the 2025 rows for the sex concerned. know_status_count enters only when the summary report prints the workbook counts for the end of 2025 (3,535 people living with HIV and 1,979 on ART) and the ART coverage of the workbook for every year from 2022 to 2025 (54, 42, 68 and 56 per cent). viral_suppression_count enters only when know_status_count enters, the summary report prints the 288 people with documented viral suppression of the workbook, and the workbook row prints a figure for every year from 2022 to 2025 and for no earlier year.
- Disagreements in the source. The 2021 Gender sheet prints 79 men and 71 women, against 80 men and 70 women in the Divisions sheet, with 151 cases on the Gender sheet and on the Divisions sheet, and 2021 therefore gets no row for men or women. The age groups of 2023 add to 419 against 415 in the Cases sheet, and 2023 therefore gets no age group row.
- Test. An offline test on a synthetic workbook built from the workbook dump of 14 October 2026, with the pdfplumber text of the summary report, wrote 245 rows for 2000 to 2025, with 235 rows of notified_hiv_cases, six of know_status_count and four of viral_suppression_count. writers.validate returned no problem and no series key repeated. With the 2021 Gender row set to the Divisions counts the connector wrote 253 rows, adding the eight rows for men and women in 2021. A misplaced viral suppression row and a summary count of 289 people with viral suppression kept know_status_count and dropped viral_suppression_count. A summary coverage of 67 per cent for 2024 and an unreadable summary report dropped every cascade row. A summary count of nine transgender people dropped the four transgender rows of 2025. A changed paediatric count in Table 1 dropped the 2025 case rows, a broken Divisions row total dropped 2020, an Ethnicity total of 1,584 dropped 2024, a changed age group dropped the 2024 age rows, and a broken cumulative column refused the workbook. With --start 1989 the connector wrote 256 rows.
- Not carried. The national On ART row is a DUPLICATE of who_gho on_art_count, with three disagreements (574 against 568 for 2022, 580 against 711 for 2023, 1,979 against 2,003 for 2025). The division blocks of the Treatment Cascade sheet print West, Central and North where the case sheets print Central / Eastern, Western and Northern, and the 2025 on-ART rows of the division blocks do not add to the printed division totals. The deaths fall under the Fiji deaths ruling. The ethnicity rows, the mode of transmission and the care status at diagnosis have no panel indicator, and the panel carries no notified cases by transmission category (ruling R31). The key population split of the Key Pops sheet for 2025 (144 men who have sex with men and 362 people who inject drugs) is NOT CARRIED, because 1,164 of the 2,016 cases of 2025 carry an unknown key population, which makes every key population count a lower bound. The screening tests and the positivity rate of the summary report count screening tests conducted, with repeat tests of people already diagnosed among the screening tests, while hiv_tests_performed counts tests with the result returned to the person. The full surveillance report repeats the case, cascade, death and test tables of the workbook and the summary report, and quotes UNAIDS estimates of people living with HIV (a DUPLICATE of unaids_aidsinfo). The CD4 count table of 2025 in the full surveillance report (table 7, page 54) is NOT CARRIED under late_hiv_diagnosis_pct, because page 56 calls the 2025 CD4 data "a partial picture" of immune status at diagnosis, and page 60 dates interruptions to CD4 testing at Labasa Hospital from February 2025.
- Other Fijian files. The /hivaids/ page of the ministry links HIV-1.pdf from 2014, never sized and never read. HIV-1.pdf is NOT REACHED, because downloads from health.gov.fj by the research assistant stay refused since 16 October 2026, and a Wayback copy would route around the refusal. A yearly case count in a 2014 file of the ministry would be an earlier copy of the register which the workbook prints from 1989. The /hivaids/ tables and the hiv-factsheet page hold no panel figure, and the SDMX service of the Fiji Bureau of Statistics lists no health table (research/phase7_eap/eap_round1_notes.md).
- Closure. Fiji closes once Jason runs fj_mohms, because every reachable source for Fiji now holds a BUILT or NOT CARRIED ruling.
- Status. Recorded on 22 October 2026. R66 adds about 245 rows after Jason's run, the count of the offline test.

## R67. Nepal, Table 1 of the World AIDS Day factsheets of the National Centre for AIDS and STD Control (BUILT as np_ncasc), and the other Nepalese factsheet content (NOT CARRIED)
- Source. Wayback captures of six editions of the NCASC (National Centre for AIDS and STD Control) factsheets, read on 25 October 2026. Factsheet 3 of 2014 (154,061 bytes, sha256 prefix 23ac8f5697bf), of 2015 (398,419 bytes, dcb2c18c8770), of 2016 (957,900 bytes, 73c8d9bd7ec8, a draft by the file name) and of 2017 (1,065,618 bytes, 39776047f7c1), and the combined factsheets of 2018 (904,305 bytes, c7c50a578d09) and 2019 (1,119,569 bytes, c880c39f5114, the capture of 6 December 2021). Table 1 of every edition prints the clients tested for HIV and the clients with an HIV positive result in every calendar year from 2008, 2009 or 2010, with January to July of the edition year as the last column.
- Decision. BUILT as connector 73, np_ncasc (hivimpact/connectors/country/nepal_ncasc.py), source type routine_programme_record. The connector writes notified_hiv_cases nationally for 2008 to 2018 (2,387, 2,110, 2,015, 2,060, 2,433, 2,426, 1,907, 1,610, 2,144, 1,842 and 2,226) from the HIV positive row, every year from the latest edition which prints the year in full and whose file name marks no draft. The age groups of the reported HIV infections in Factsheet 2 of 2012 add to the HIV positive row for 2009, 2010 and 2011, and Factsheet 7 of 2019 says routine case reporting runs from the testing and counselling sites and the PMTCT (prevention of mother-to-child transmission) sites, so the HIV positive row is the NCASC count of reported cases.
- Gates. Every capture starts with %PDF and ends with %%EOF. Every edition prints exactly one Table 1 title ending on the edition year, header years equal to the expected columns with a July part year last, and exactly one Tested for HIV row and one HIV positive row with one whole number per column. The HIV positive count sits below the clients tested in every column. Every full year printed by two or more editions agrees in every edition on the Tested for HIV row and on the HIV positive row, with one known revision, 2017 at 1,856 in the 2018 edition and 1,842 in the 2019 edition, where the later count enters. The part-year column never enters.
- Test. An offline test on the scratchpad copies wrote 11 rows, writers.validate returned no problem and no series key repeated. The connector refused every row when the 2017 copy was truncated, when the 2019 edition printed a changed 2015 count, when the 2018 edition lost a header year or printed a changed 2017 count, when the 2015 edition printed a second HIV positive row, when the 2014 title ended on 2013, when the 2016 HIV positive count reached the 2016 clients tested, when the 2017 HIV positive row lost a value and when the 2017 edition printed a changed count of clients tested for 2016. A change of 2018, a year printed by the 2019 edition alone, passed as expected.
- Not carried. The Tested for HIV row counts clients tested and not tests with the result returned (hiv_tests_performed), and travels in extra.clients_tested. The 2012 Factsheet 3 prints chart labels between the table rows, and the 2013 Factsheets 2 and 3 hold unreadable character identifiers (no OCR). The 2019 capture of 18 August 2022, the 2020 captures of 20 January 2021 and 18 August 2022 and the 2021 captures of 24 January 2022 and 18 August 2022 stop at 1,048,576 bytes. Factsheet 1 of 2010 prints the estimates of 2009 and a cumulative count of reported cases. Factsheet 2 of 2012 and of every year from 2014 to 2017 prints cumulative counts by risk group, age and sex (R14), and the editions of 2014 to 2017 add charts of fiscal-year counts by region and of reported cases by mode of transmission. The age table of 2009 to 2011 uses age groups matching no panel label and covers three years. Cumulative counts fall under R14, and the fiscal-year counts of the 2018 and 2019 editions sit outside a calendar-year series. The modelled estimates are a DUPLICATE of the UNAIDS estimates. PMTCT mothers receiving ART is a DUPLICATE of unaids_aidsinfo pmtct_arv_count (agreeing except 2011, 117 against 134, and 2017, 192 against 191). PMTCT testing and positive pregnant women have no count indicator, and the infant PCR counts have a percent indicator only. The ART profile as of July is a DUPLICATE of on_art_count. The viral load table counts samples, while viral_load_tests_performed counts people. The IBBS (integrated biological and behavioural surveillance) prevalence covers survey areas below the national level, mostly in charts. The STI and hepatitis figures have no panel indicator. The 2022 file has no text layer and the 2023 file prints Preeti-font Nepali text.
- Closure. Nepal stays open on two Wayback files, which wait for the Wayback pass. The 2021 factsheet captured on 20 April 2025 under the demo path (https://ncasc.gov.np/demo/public/wad-2021/file/World-AIDS-Day-2021-Factsheet.pdf, CDX length 2,496,957) has not been requested, and a complete copy may carry Table 1 for 2019 and 2020. The 2018 round of subnational estimates (subnational-estimates-nepal-2018.pdf) has not been read and may add modelled estimates by province. The 2024 and 2025 factsheets and the DoHS (Department of Health Services) annual report sit on giwmscdnone.gov.np, which failed the re-probe pass, and under R47 keep Nepal open no longer. R68 records the reading of both Wayback files on 25 October 2026 and closes Nepal.
- Status. Recorded on 25 October 2026. R67 adds about 11 rows after Jason's run, the count of the offline test. Jason's first run on 25 October 2026 cached five of the six captures at the expected sizes and wrote no row, because the Wayback Machine answered HTTP 429 (too many requests) to all four requests for the 2019 capture of 6 December 2021. Jason's second run on 25 October 2026 read the five cached captures, downloaded the 2019 capture at 1,119,569 bytes, passed every gate and wrote 11 rows, taking the panel from 456,077 to 456,088 rows and notified_hiv_cases from 6,234 to 6,245 rows, with no validation warning on Nepal.

## R68. Nepal, the 2021 World AIDS Day factsheet and the 2018 subnational estimates of the National Centre for AIDS and STD Control (NOT CARRIED), and the closure of Nepal
- Source. Three Wayback GET requests on 25 October 2026, with at least 10 seconds between requests. The World AIDS Day 2021 factsheet of the NCASC (National Centre for AIDS and STD Control), captured on 20 April 2025 under the demo path (https://ncasc.gov.np/demo/public/wad-2021/file/World-AIDS-Day-2021-Factsheet.pdf, capture 20250420045902), came back at 5,068,884 bytes (sha256 prefix 7e551a2bb844), a complete PDF of 32 pages. The 2018 round of subnational estimates, captured on 20 April 2025 under the 2019 World AIDS Day folder (https://ncasc.gov.np/demo/public/wad-2019/file/subnational-estimates-nepal-2018.pdf, capture 20250420165034), came back at 4,552,272 bytes (sha256 prefix 094437d70258), a PDF of six pages. The capture of 12 August 2024 under the older publication path (http://ncasc.gov.np/uploads/frontend/publication/subnational-estimates-nepal-2018.pdf, capture 20240812063254) holds the same bytes.
- Decision. NOT CARRIED. np_ncasc keeps the six editions and the 11 rows of R67, 2008 to 2018. Table 1 of the 2021 factsheet (page 7) prints the clients tested for HIV and the HIV positive results of every calendar year from 2011 to July 2021, with 204,834 clients tested and 2,796 HIV positive results in 2019 and 103,742 clients tested and 2,313 HIV positive results in 2020. The columns of 2011 to 2018 agree with the six R67 editions, including the 2017 revision to 1,842. The columns of 2019 and 2020 contradict the monthly chart of the same edition. Figure 10 of Fact Sheet S1 (page 28) prints the clients tested and the HIV positive results of every month from January 2020 to July 2021. The months from August 2020 to July 2021 add to 242,689 clients tested and 2,579 HIV positive results, the totals of the Nepali fiscal year 077/78 (mid-July 2020 to mid-July 2021) in Figure 9 and in Fact Sheets 2 and 3. The months from January to July 2021 add to 109,986 and 1,587, the January to July 2021 column of Table 1. The months from January to December 2020 add to 198,988 clients tested and 2,147 HIV positive results, against 103,742 and 2,313 in the 2020 column of Table 1. The 2019 column fails a second check. Fiscal year 076/77 in Figure 9 (147,968 clients tested and 2,416 HIV positive results) less January to July 2020 in Figure 10 (66,285 and 1,155) leaves 81,683 and 1,261 for August to December 2019, and adding January to July 2019 from the 2019 edition (134,399 and 1,417) gives 216,082 and 2,678, against 204,834 and 2,796 in the 2019 column. The same arithmetic on 2018 (fiscal year 075/76 at 237,496 and 2,298, less January to July 2019, plus January to July 2018 from the 2018 edition at 161,958 and 1,314) gives 265,055 clients tested, the 2018 count of Table 1 in the 2019 and 2021 editions, and 2,195 HIV positive results against 2,226. 2019 and 2020 appear as full years in the 2021 edition alone, so no second edition can settle which count of the 2021 edition is right, and no row for 2019 or 2020 enters. The monthly labels of Figure 10 never enter, because a calendar total built from the labels would contradict Table 1 of the same edition.
- Gates. A draft of np_ncasc with seven editions passed every R67 gate and wrote 13 rows, because the R67 gates compare every edition with the other editions and never compare Table 1 with the monthly chart of the same edition. The draft was withdrawn on 25 October 2026, and the connector, the notified_hiv_cases description in hivimpact/config/indicators.py and the np_ncasc entry in scripts/build_site.py stay as recorded under R67.
- Test. The offline test on the six R67 editions, rerun on 25 October 2026 after the seven-edition draft was withdrawn, wrote the same 11 rows, writers.validate returned no problem, no series key repeated, every refusal case of R67 refused again, and the change of 2018 passed as before. scripts/build_site.py compiles with py_compile.
- Not carried. The subnational estimates of 2018. Every page of the file draws the text as outlined vector shapes, and pdfplumber finds no character on any of the six pages, so a reading needs OCR (optical character recognition), and no figure read by OCR enters the panel (R25). Fact Sheet 1 of 2021. The modelled estimates are a DUPLICATE of the UNAIDS estimates. Fact Sheet 2 of 2021. The cumulative counts as of 15 July 2021 fall under R14. The reported cases by province and gender for fiscal year 077/78 (2,579 in all, 1,464 male, 917 female and 198 transgender) cover a fiscal window, and R67 keeps the fiscal-year counts of the NCASC editions outside the calendar-year np_ncasc series. The charts by mode of transmission, age and risk group print percentages only. Fact Sheet 3 of 2021. The clients tested and the yield by province for fiscal year 077/78 (242,689 clients tested in all) cover a fiscal window, as in Fact Sheet 2. Fact Sheet 4 of 2021. The national ART (antiretroviral therapy) profile as of July 2021 is a DUPLICATE of on_art_count. The people on ART by province as of the end of fiscal year 077/78, the multi-month dispensing by province and the testing and treatment cascade by province for fiscal year 077/78 cover a fiscal window, as in Fact Sheet 2. The retention charts print percentages only. Tables 2 to 5 print the fiscal-year achievements of community and home-based care (CHBC), the community care centres (CCC), community-led testing (CLT) with index testing, and the community care services (CCS), with no panel indicator for CHBC, CCC or CCS and a fiscal window for CLT with index testing. Fact Sheet 5 of 2021. The PMTCT (prevention of mother-to-child transmission) mothers receiving ART for 2010 to 2020 (96, 117, 126, 142, 162, 145, 181, 192, 146, 123 and 121) are a DUPLICATE of unaids_aidsinfo pmtct_arv_count, which agrees on 2018 to 2020 and carries the same gaps on 2011 and 2017 as in R67. PMTCT testing, positive pregnant women and infant prophylaxis have no count indicator, the infant PCR (polymerase chain reaction) counts have a percent indicator only, and the provincial PMTCT chart covers a fiscal window. Fact Sheet 6 of 2021. The IBBS (integrated biological and behavioural surveillance) table of 2020 prints 11 prevalence values under 13 column heads, so no value can be matched to a survey group. The STI (sexually transmitted infection) counts by province and gender have no panel indicator. Fact Sheet 7 of 2021. The targeted interventions for the fiscal years 070/71 to 077/78 nationally, and by province for fiscal year 077/78, for people who inject drugs, female sex workers and the clients of female sex workers, male sex workers, men who have sex with men with transgender people, male labour migrants with the spouses of male labour migrants, and prison inmates, cover fiscal windows. The panel already carries global_fund kp_reached and kp_tested for Nepal (people who inject drugs for 2018 to 2025, and gay and bisexual men, sex workers and transgender people for 2018 to 2020), so fiscal rows would cross windows with the global_fund rows, and the R42 condition fails. The condoms given to key populations are part of the national total in unaids_aidsinfo male_condoms_distributed (2019 to 2024). Needles, syringes and opioid substitution have no panel indicator, and the table on children affected by AIDS (CABA) has no panel indicator. Fact Sheet S1 of 2021. The monthly charts and the fiscal-year charts never enter, as set out under the Decision bullet.
- Closure. Nepal closes. The two Wayback files which kept Nepal open under R67 are read and NOT CARRIED. The 2024 and 2025 factsheets and the DoHS (Department of Health Services) annual report sit on giwmscdnone.gov.np, which failed the re-probe pass, and under R47 keep Nepal open no longer. Every other reachable Nepalese source holds a BUILT or NOT CARRIED ruling. South Asia keeps Afghanistan open.
- Status. Recorded on 25 October 2026. R68 adds no row. np_ncasc keeps the 11 rows of Jason's second run on 25 October 2026, and no new run is needed.

## R69. The Wayback pass of 25 October 2026 for Vanuatu, Tuvalu, Nepal and Afghanistan, the Vanuatu MICS report of 2007 (BUILT as vu_mics), the other archived files (NOT CARRIED or NOT REACHED), and the closure of Vanuatu, Tuvalu and Afghanistan
- Source. Capture index (CDX) searches and Wayback GET requests on 25 October 2026, with at least 10 seconds between requests and no HTTP 429 (too many requests) answer. The capture index searches covered the PDF captures on aidsdatahub.org naming Vanuatu (10 captures), Tuvalu (two captures), Nepal (128 captures) and Afghanistan (38 captures), the captures on www.childinfo.org naming Vanuatu (12 captures), the Pacific Environment Data Portal pages 78326 and 40489 of the Secretariat of the Pacific Regional Environment Programme (SPREP), the report of the second generation surveillance (SGS) surveys of 2004 and 2005 in Pacific countries on aidsdatahub.org, the files of the National Centre for AIDS and STD Control (NCASC) of Nepal on giwmscdnone.gov.np, the NCASC tabData files of 2022 and 2023, the Vanuatu Demographic and Health Survey (DHS) files of 2013 under vnso.gov.vu and the eVols item of the Vanuatu DHS report of 2013. The Wayback GET requests returned complete PDFs, every file starting with %PDF. For Vanuatu, the final report of the Multiple Indicator Cluster Survey (MICS) of 2007 (http://www.childinfo.org/files/MICS3_Vanuatu_FinalReport_2007_Eng.pdf, capture 20101222164918, 3,868,540 bytes, sha256 prefix b72c64515d5e), the Children and AIDS fact sheet of the United Nations Children's Fund (UNICEF) on www.childinfo.org (capture 20100622104418, 125,891 bytes, 57687cdc2fce), the Global AIDS Response progress report of 2015 (capture 20240617213514, 1,172,290 bytes, 0ac77a4499e2), the SGS report of 2008 on antenatal women, clients of a clinic for sexually transmitted infections (STI) and youth (capture 20210914103444, 777,588 bytes, 8aa90761609f), the integrated biological and behavioural surveillance (IBBS) report of 2011 on the population size of men who have sex with men (MSM) and transgender people (capture 20230123051931, 839,495 bytes, 037717c46454) and the National Composite Policy Index (NCPI) of 2010 (capture 20110817160735, 102,622 bytes, 568ee7dcc6f4). For Tuvalu, the SGS report of 2007 (capture 20170811062522, 588,261 bytes, 784fd17b7cc5) and the NCPI of 2008 (capture 20110816152148, 48,110 bytes, 181f8610b80b). For Nepal, the World AIDS Day factsheets of 2022 (capture 20250612113656, 5,778,024 bytes, 2a0a3a28635d, 28 pages) and the national IBBS fact sheet of 2021 on people who inject drugs (PWID), capture 20230627120251, 907,077 bytes, 8a27eab3f010, four pages. For Afghanistan, the slides of the HIV and AIDS Data Hub for Asia and the Pacific of September 2021 (capture 20220722154320, 945,725 bytes, 30c01b42b3fd, 51 pages). The Data Hub copy of the Nepal World AIDS Day factsheet of 2021 (capture 20220517235416) stopped at 1,048,576 bytes with the sha256 prefix ff07e2093ab3 of the truncated NCASC capture of 2021 under R67, and R68 reads the complete NCASC copy of the 2021 factsheet.
- Decision. The Vanuatu MICS report of 2007 is BUILT as connector 74, vu_mics (hivimpact/connectors/country/vanuatu_mics.py), source type survey. During Jason's run the connector downloads the capture once into data/raw/vu_mics, and every later run reads the cached copy with the pdfplumber text layer. The connector writes hiv_comprehensive_knowledge_survey_pct for women in Vanuatu nationally for 2007, at 16.3 per cent for women aged 15 to 49 (2,692 women) and 15.4 per cent for women aged 15 to 24 (1,083 women weighted and 979 unweighted, MICS indicator 82), with the printed confidence interval of 12.4 to 18.4 per cent from Table SE 01 in extra. The MICS weights are normalised, which is why the weighted number of women aged 15 to 49 equals the unweighted number. The summary table at the front of the report prints 16.3 per cent against indicator 82, which the report defines for young people, while Table HA.3 prints 16.3 per cent for women aged 15 to 49 and 15.4 per cent for women aged 15 to 24, and Table SE 01 prints 15.4 per cent for young people. The summary table therefore mislabels the national share, the connector writes the Table HA.3 figures, and extra.summary_table records the mislabel. pacific_data_hub carries the Vanuatu DHS of 2013 on hiv_comprehensive_knowledge_survey_pct and no row for 2007, so the vu_mics rows overlap no other source.
- Gates. Tables HA.1, HA.2 and HA.3 print once, with the caption and "Vanuatu, 2007", and Table HA.3 prints the footnote "MICS Indicator 82". The eight regions, the urban and rural rows, the seven five-year age rows, the row for women aged 15 to 24 and the national row print once in every table, with the same number of women in Tables HA.1, HA.2 and HA.3. In every row of Table HA.3 the knowledge share is no larger than the share knowing the two ways of prevention or the share rejecting the three misconceptions, the misconception share equals the fourth column of Table HA.2, and the share knowing the two ways of prevention lies between the Table HA.1 share knowing all three ways and the smaller of the Table HA.1 condom and faithful-partner shares. The urban and rural numbers add to the national number exactly, the region numbers and the five-year age numbers add to the national number within one, the numbers for women aged 15 to 19 and 20 to 24 add to the number for women aged 15 to 24 exactly, and every breakdown weighted by the numbers gives the printed total share within 0.1. The Table SE 01 row prints once, r times 100 equals the Table HA.3 share for women aged 15 to 24 within 0.05, and the printed limits equal r minus and plus two standard errors within 0.001 and hold r. The text of the report rounds the national share to 16 per cent and names the misconceptions of transmission by mosquito bites and by sharing food. The text layer of the sampling-error tables runs backwards character by character, and the connector reverses the page string and splits the result on white space, a fixed rearrangement of the characters pdfplumber reads with no optical character recognition (OCR), consistent with R25.
- Test. An offline test on a scratchpad copy of the capture wrote two rows, and writers.validate returned no problem. The connector wrote no row for a country other than Vanuatu, for a year window starting in 2008 or for a cache truncated at 2,000,000 bytes. The connector refused every row under 17 tampered copies, a changed national share, a changed Shefa share, a changed Torba number, a changed Penama share in Table HA.2, a changed Tafea share in Table HA.1, a changed Penama knowledge share, a raised share for women aged 15 to 24, a share of 15.3 per cent for women aged 15 to 24 (refused by Table SE 01 alone), a changed number of women aged 15 to 24, a changed r, upper limit or standard error in Table SE 01, a removed footnote, a summary table printing 15.4 per cent, a changed caption year, a changed rounding in the text and a removed misconception. The connector also refused a second copy of the Table HA.3 page.
- Not carried. Vanuatu MICS report of 2007. The other columns of Tables HA.1 to HA.3, the region rows, which split Port Vila from Shefa and Luganville from Sanma and fit no admin1, the urban, rural, age, education, wealth and language rows, and Tables HA.4 to HA.6 (mother-to-child transmission, accepting attitudes and women tested), which fit no panel id. Children and AIDS fact sheet. A one-page secondary compilation, which prints antenatal care coverage of 84 per cent from the MICS of 2007, 7,000 births from the United Nations Population Division and a dash for every HIV estimate, a DUPLICATE of the MICS of 2007 with no HIV figure. The other 10 captures on www.childinfo.org name water, sanitation, maternal health, nutrition, regional profiles, statistical tables and a child-friendly edition of the MICS report in the file names, and the R60 list names the MICS final report alone, so the pass requested no other capture on www.childinfo.org. Vanuatu Global AIDS Response progress report of 2015. The report prints cumulative counts (R14), four people on antiretroviral therapy (ART) on page 16 and five on pages 28 and 31 of the PDF, and an antenatal testing count of 1,553 pregnant women with no stated period or denominator. Vanuatu and Tuvalu NCPI files. The NCPI is a policy questionnaire with no panel id. Vanuatu IBBS report of 2011. The size estimate covers Port Vila alone, the point estimate of 327 lies outside the printed interval of 764 to 2,032, the author cautions against use of the estimate, and the survey detected no HIV. Vanuatu SGS report of 2008. The survey covered antenatal women at Vila Central Hospital, STI clinic clients at Wan Smol Bag (74 men and 131 women) and youth, all in Port Vila on Efate, and no panel population group covers antenatal women or STI clinic clients, and Port Vila fits no admin1. The other six captures on aidsdatahub.org naming Vanuatu hold a report on gender violence of 2016, an assessment of the STI response in Fiji and Vanuatu, a training package on mother-to-child transmission of 2007, a case study of health information systems of 2009, a process evaluation and the national survey of women's lives and family relationships of 2011, with no panel id. Tuvalu SGS report of 2007. The surveys covered 114 pregnant women, 209 seafarers and 305 youths in Funafuti from August 2005 to February 2006, detected no HIV among pregnant women and seafarers, and print the shares with chlamydia, gonorrhoea, syphilis and hepatitis B at 17.5, 0.9, 1.7 and 9.8 per cent of antenatal women and 8.1, 0.5, 5.2 and 13.4 per cent of seafarers. No panel population group covers seafarers or antenatal women, the panel has no STI indicator, and the youth survey covers Funafuti alone over a window crossing two calendar years. Nepal World AIDS Day factsheets of 2022. Table 1 prints the modelled estimates, a DUPLICATE of the UNAIDS estimates. The testing, reported case, ART, community programme and targeted intervention figures cover the Nepali fiscal year 2078/79 (mid-July 2021 to mid-July 2022) or earlier fiscal years, outside the calendar-year np_ncasc series (R67), and the targeted intervention rows fail the R42 condition as under R68. The monthly charts of January 2020 to July 2022 never enter, as under R68. The PMTCT (prevention of mother-to-child transmission) table of 2010 to July 2022 prints the mothers receiving ART, a DUPLICATE of unaids_aidsinfo pmtct_arv_count, and the PMTCT testing counts have no count indicator. The IBBS table repeats the PWID values of the national IBBS fact sheet of 2021. The edition prints no calendar-year table of clients tested and HIV positive results, so np_ncasc gains no year and R68 stands. Nepal national IBBS fact sheet of 2021 on PWID. The survey of 2020 sampled 1,840 PWID (1,690 men and 150 women) and prints a weighted HIV prevalence of 2.8 per cent for men and two per cent for women, the women recruited by convenience in three provinces. The fact sheet prints no test algorithm, no confidence interval and no unweighted share, and gives the HIV positive men as 34 in the passage on ART and 37 in the chart of recent infection (Figure 4), so the figures fail the gates of R65 and of the kz_kncdiz ruling. The other Nepalese captures on aidsdatahub.org. The NCASC factsheets of 2015 to 2017 repeat editions or content which R67 reads or rules NOT CARRIED (modelled estimates, cumulative counts, PMTCT counts and IBBS figures by survey area). The IBBS reports and fact sheets of 2002 to 2018 cover survey areas below the national level (the Kathmandu valley, the Pokhara valley, the 22 Terai highway districts and the eastern, western and far-western regions), which fit no admin1, as under R67. The data books of 2020, 2022 and 2023, the UNAIDS data sheet of 2019, the country cards and the country snapshot of 2018 are secondary compilations of the Data Hub and sit outside the R61 list item for Nepal, the NCASC factsheets. The strategic plans, guidelines, investment plans, legal and policy reviews, the preliminary DHS report of 2011 (the dhs connector reads the DHS programme) and the studies of migration, violence and stigma hold no panel figure. Afghanistan slides of September 2021. Every slide names a source compiled by the Data Hub, the UNAIDS estimates of 2021, the Global AIDS Response Progress Reporting (GARPR) of 2015 and 2018, the IBBS of 2012 refused under the Afghan key population ruling, the size estimates of 2015, the spending and the ART counts of AIDSinfo, so the slides are a secondary compilation. The other 37 Afghan captures hold country reviews of 2009 and 2011, the country progress report of 2014, the DHS of 2015 and the key findings of the DHS of 2015, which the DHS ruling refuses for an ever-married sample, the service provision assessment of 2018 and 2019, strategies, assessments and single-site studies, with no panel figure.
- Not reached. The capture index on the Vanuatu DHS files of 2013 under vnso.gov.vu answered HTTP 503 (an outage page of 11,832 bytes, sha256 prefix e084d5279217), and the capture index on the eVols item answered HTTP 504 (160 bytes, c9c89d274726), on 25 October 2026 after an earlier failure on the same day. The only capture of SPREP page 78326 is an HTTP 301 redirect, and the capture index holds no capture of page 40489. The capture indexes on the Pacific SGS report of 2004 and 2005 and on giwmscdnone.gov.np returned an empty list. The capture index on aidsdatahub.org naming Afghanistan holds no capture of the Afghan data books of 2020, 2022 and 2023. Under R60 and R66 a NOT REACHED file keeps no country open, and pacific_data_hub already carries the Vanuatu DHS of 2013.
- Closure. Vanuatu closes once Jason runs vu_mics, Tuvalu and Afghanistan close, and every reachable source for Vanuatu, Tuvalu and Afghanistan holds a BUILT or NOT CARRIED ruling. South Asia closes with Afghanistan, after Nepal under R68. East Asia and the Pacific keeps Fiji open until Jason runs fj_mohms (R66), and Eastern Europe and Central Asia has no open country.
- Status. Recorded on 25 October 2026. R69 adds about two rows after Jason's run, the count of the offline test. Jason's run of vu_mics on 25 October 2026 matched the sha256 of the copy read for R69 (prefix b72c64515d5e) and wrote the two rows, the panel holds 456,090 rows, and Vanuatu closes.

## R70. Nicaragua, the national rows of ni_mapasalud from the cached Wayback captures (BUILT as a partial run), and the six SILAIS captures still refused (NOT REACHED)
- Source. The 20 Wayback captures of the Mapa Nacional de la Salud of MINSA (the Nicaraguan Ministry of Health), the national page and the pages of the 19 SILAIS (Sistemas Locales de Atención Integral en Salud, the local health systems), read in full on 14 October 2026 (research/phase5_lac/lac_round1_notes.md, section "Nicaragua SILAIS check, 14 October 2026"). Jason's ninth run on 27 October 2026 read the 13 cached captures and downloaded the Masaya capture (20240619201831, 323,225 bytes) on the retry after a wait of 600 seconds, and the run log shows no WARNING of a size or hash mismatch, so the Masaya capture matches the size and the sha256 prefix 5209212248ce recorded in the connector. The Matagalpa capture (20231204125406) then drew HTTP 429 (too many requests) on the first attempt and on the retry, and the run wrote no row. 14 of the 20 captures now sit in data/raw/nicaragua_mapasalud. The captures not cached are the SILAIS of Matagalpa, Nueva Segovia, RACCS (Región Autónoma de la Costa Caribe Sur), Río San Juan, Rivas and Zelaya Central.
- Decision. BUILT as a partial run of ni_mapasalud (hivimpact/connectors/country/nicaragua_mapasalud.py). A run with a SILAIS capture not cached writes the seven national notified_hiv_cases rows for 2017 to 2023 (1,033, 1,102, 1,118, 953, 938, 1,129 and 1,155) and no SILAIS row. A run with all 20 captures cached writes the 118 rows of the reading of 14 October 2026, 111 SILAIS rows and seven national rows, as before. The connector requests a missing capture once with no retry, and the first capture lost to a refusal or a network error ends the downloads of the run, so a refused capture costs one request and no wait. A capture which comes through stays cached, and a later run requests only the captures still missing. The fifth to ninth runs (22 to 27 October 2026) made 12 retries after waits of 10 or 15 minutes, and 11 retries were refused.
- Gates. The page gates of the full run apply to every cached page. A partial run refuses every row unless the national capture is cached, every cached capture matches the size and sha256 prefix recorded from the copy read on 14 October 2026, every cached page prints the Casos Nuevos values read on 14 October 2026, the cached SILAIS sum to no more than the national page in every year, and the values read on 14 October 2026 from all 20 captures pass the sum gates of the full run. The 19 SILAIS sum exactly to the national count for 2017, 2019, 2020 and 2021, and to 1,092 against the national 1,102 for 2018, a shortfall standing in the source.
